द्रष्टृत्वेनोपयुक्तत्वात्तदैव स्यान्न दृश्यता ।

When it is engaged as the seer, it cannot then be seen;

Verse 2.27

2.27

कालान्तरे चेद्दृश्यत्वं न ह्यद्रष्टृकमिष्यते ॥ २७॥

if it were seen at another time, it would be admitted only through a seer.

Text 77

2 · 77

सन्तु काममनात्मधर्मा ममत्वादयो यथोक्तन्यायबलादनात्मतयैव च तेषु व्यवहारादहंरूपस्य तु प्रत्यगात्मसंबन्धितयैव प्रसिद्धेरहंब्रह्मास्मीति श्रुतेश्चानात्मधर्मत्वमयुक्तमिति चेत्तन्न ।

One might object: “Let ‘mine’ and desire be properties of the not-Self, as reason shows; ordinary usage confirms that. But ‘I’ is known only in relation to the inward Self, and the revelation ‘I am Brahman’ shows that it cannot belong to the not-Self.” This is not so.

Text 78

2 · 78

अहंधर्मस्त्वभिन्नश्चेदहंब्रह्मेति वाक्यतः ।

If ego-ness were not different from the Self, then the sentence “I am Brahman”

Verse 2.28

2.28

गौरोऽहमित्यनैकान्तो वाक्यं तद्व्यपनेतृ तत् ॥ २८॥

would not be conclusive; a sentence such as “I am fair” can also be corrected by another statement.

Text 80

2 · 80

कथं वाक्यं तद्व्यपनेतृ तदिति । उच्यते ।

How does that sentence remove the error? The answer is:

Text 81

2 · 81

योऽयं स्थाणुः पुमानेष पुंधिया स्थाणुधीरिव ।

Just as one who mistakes a post for a person later recognizes, “This is a post,”

Verse 2.29

2.29

ब्रह्मास्मीतिधियाशेषा ह्यहंबुद्धिर्निवर्त्यते ॥ २९॥

so the thought “I am Brahman” removes the whole cognition of “I.”

Text 83

2 · 83

अहंपरिच्छेदव्यावृत्तौ न किंचिदव्यावृत्तं द्वैतजातमवशिष्यते द्वितीयसंबन्धस्य तन्मूलत्वात् । अत आह ।

When the limited notion “I” is removed, no duality remains; the relation to a second thing has that notion as its root. Therefore he says:

Text 84

2 · 84

निवृत्तायामहंबुद्धौ ममधीः प्रविलीयते ।

When the thought “I” has ceased, the thought “mine” dissolves;

Verse 2.30

2.30

अहंबीजा हि सा सिद्ध्येत्तमोऽभावे कुतः फणी ॥ ३०॥

it is rooted in “I”; without darkness, where could a snake appear?

Text 86

2 · 86

विवक्षितदृष्टान्तांशज्ञापनाय दृष्टान्तव्याख्या ।

The example is explained to show the point intended:

Text 87

2 · 87

तमोऽभिभूतचित्तो हि रज्ज्वां पश्यति रोषणम् ।

A mind overcome by darkness sees something frightening in a rope;

Verse 2.31

2.31

भ्रान्त्या भ्रान्त्या विना तस्मान्नोरगं स्रजि वीक्षते ॥ ३१॥

without delusion, one does not see a snake in a garland.

Text 89

2 · 89

अनन्वयाच्च नात्मधर्मोऽहङ्कारः ।

The ego is not a property of the Self because it is not continuous with it.

Text 90

2 · 90

आत्मनश्चेदहंधर्मो यायान्मुक्तिसुषुप्तयोः ।

If ego were a property of the Self, it would remain through liberation and deep sleep;

Verse 2.32

2.32

यतो नान्वेति तेनायमन्यदीयो भवेदहम् ॥ ३२॥

since it does not remain, this “I” belongs to something else.

Text 92

2 · 92

आत्मधर्मत्वाभ्युपगमेऽपरिहार्यदोषप्रसक्तिश्च ।

If ego is accepted as a property of the Self, an unavoidable defect follows:

Text 93

2 · 93

यद्यात्मधर्मोऽहङ्कारो नित्यत्वं तस्य बोधवत् ।

If ego is a property of the Self, it must be permanent, like awareness;

Verse 2.33

2.33

नित्यत्वे मोक्षशास्त्राणां वैयर्थं प्राप्नुयाद् ध्रुवम् ॥३३॥

and if it is permanent, the scriptures on liberation would surely be useless.

Text 95

2 · 95

स्यात्परिहारः स्वाभाविकधर्मत्वाभ्युपगमेऽप्याम्रादिफलवदिति चेत्तन्न ।

One might answer that ego is a natural property [of the Self], like the fruit of a mango. That is not so.

Text 96

2 · 96

आम्रादेः परिणामित्वाद्गुणहानिर्गुणान्तरैः ।

A mango changes and loses one quality while gaining another;

Verse 2.34

2.34

अविकारि तु तद्ब्रह्म "न हि द्रष्टुरि"तिश्रुतेः ॥ ३४॥

Brahman, however, is changeless, as scripture says, “For the seer …”

Text 98

2 · 98

अहङ्कारस्य चागमापायित्वात्तद्धर्मिणश्चानित्यत्वं प्राप्नोति ।

Since ego arises and passes away, the Self that bears it would also be impermanent.

Text 99

2 · 99

आगमापायिनिष्ठत्वादनित्यत्वमियाद्दृशिः ।

If awareness rested on a property that comes and goes, awareness too would be impermanent;

Verse 2.35

2.35

उपयन्नपयन्धर्मो विकरोति हि धर्मिणम् ॥ ३५॥

for a property that arises and passes away transforms its bearer.

Text 101

2 · 101

अस्त्वनित्यत्वं कमुपालभेमहि प्रमाणोपपन्नत्वादिति चेत्तन्न ।

One might say, “Let it be impermanent; why object, since it is established by a means of knowledge?” That is not so.

Text 102

2 · 102

सदाविलुप्तसाक्षित्वं स्वतस्सिद्धं न पार्यते ।

Ever-present witnessing awareness is self-established and cannot be denied

Verse 2.36

2.36

अपह्नोतुं घटस्येव कुशाग्रीयधियात्मनः ॥ ३६॥

by the sharpest intellect, any more than one can deny a pot that is plainly seen.

Text 104

2 · 104

एतस्माच्च हेतोरहंकारस्यानात्मधर्मत्वमवसीयताम् ।

For this reason, understand that ego is not a property of the Self.

Text 105

2 · 105

प्रमाणैश्चावगम्यत्वाद्घटादिवदहंदृशेः ।

The cognition “I” is known through means of knowledge, like a pot;

Verse 2.37

2.37

यतो राधिः प्रमाणानां स कथं तैः प्रसिध्यति ॥ ३७॥

how could that which is the ground of the means of knowledge itself be established by them?

Text 107

2 · 107

धर्मधर्मिणोश्चेतरेतरविरुद्धात्मकत्वादसङ्गतिः ।

Moreover, the property and its bearer have opposed natures, so their relation is impossible.

Text 108

2 · 108

धर्मिणश्च विरुद्धत्वान्न दृश्यगुणसङ्गतिः ।

A property that is seen cannot be joined to its bearer when their natures are opposed;

Verse 2.38

2.38

मारुतान्दोलितज्वालं शैत्यं नाग्निं सिसृप्सति ॥ ३८॥

coldness does not enter fire whose flame is tossed by the wind.

Text 110

2 · 110

तस्माद्विस्रब्धमुपगम्यताम् ।

Therefore accept this without hesitation:

Text 111

2 · 111

द्रष्टृत्वं दृश्यता चैव नैकस्मिन्नेकदा क्वचित् ।

seerhood and being seen are never present together in one thing at one time;

Verse 2.39

2.39

दृश्यदृश्यो न च द्रष्टा द्रष्टुर्दर्शी दृशिर्न च ॥३९॥

the seer is not an object, and seeing itself is not an object for the seer.

Text 113

2 · 113

सर्वसंव्यवहारलोपश्च प्राप्नोति । यस्मात् ।

If this were otherwise, all ordinary dealings would collapse. For:

Text 114

2 · 114

द्रष्टापि यदि दृश्याया आत्मेयात्कर्मतां धियः ।

If even the seer, the Self, became an object of the intellect,

Verse 2.40

2.40

यौगपद्यमदृश्यत्वं वैयर्थ्यं चाप्नुयाच्छृतिः ॥ ४०॥

it would have to be both at once, unseen and seen, and scripture would be pointless.

Text 116

2 · 116

कुतः । यस्मात् ।

Why? Because:

Text 117

2 · 117

नालुप्तदृष्टेर्दृश्यत्वं दृश्यत्वे द्रष्टृता कुतः ।

What is uninterrupted seeing cannot be an object; if it were an object, where would its seerhood be?

Verse 2.41

2.41

स्याच्चेद्दृगेकं निर्दृश्यं जगद्वा स्यादसाक्षिकम् ॥ ४१॥

If sight were one and nothing were seen, the world would be without a witness.

Text 119

2 · 119

उक्तयुक्तिं दृढीकर्तुमागमोदाहरणोपन्यासः ।

A scriptural example is introduced to strengthen the argument:

Text 120

2 · 120

आर्तमन्यद्दृशेः सर्वं "नेति नेती"ति चासकृत् ।

“Everything other than the seeing Self is subject to suffering”; and repeatedly, “not this, not this.”

Verse 2.42

2.42

वदन्ती निर्गुणं ब्रह्म कथं श्रुतिरुपेक्ष्यते ॥ ४२॥

If scripture teaches that Brahman is without qualities, how can it be ignored?

Text 122

2 · 122

"महाभूतान्यहंकार" इत्येतत्क्षेत्रमुच्यते ।

“The great elements and ego”—all this is called the field;

Verse 2.43

2.43

न दृशेर्द्वैतयोगोऽस्ति विश्वेश्वरमतादपि ॥ ४३॥

even according to the view of Viśveśvara, the seer has no relation to duality.

Text 124

2 · 124

अधुना प्रकृतार्थोऽपसंहारः ।

The subject is now brought to a close.

Text 125

2 · 125

एवमेतद्धिरुग्ज्ञेयं मिथ्यासिद्धमनात्मकम् ।

Thus one should know that this [duality] is falsely established, not-Self,

Verse 2.44

2.44

मोहमूलं सुदुर्बोधं द्वैतं युक्तिभिरात्मनः ॥ ४४॥

rooted in delusion and very hard to understand by reasoning about the Self.

Text 127

2 · 127

कुतो मिथ्यासिद्धत्वं द्वैतस्येति चेत् ।

If one asks how duality is falsely established,

Text 128

2 · 128

न पृथङ्नात्मना सिद्धिरात्मनोऽन्यस्य वस्तुनः ।

no object apart from the Self is ever established as a separate reality;

Verse 2.45

2.45

आत्मवत्कल्पितस्तस्मादहंकारादिरात्मनि ॥ ४५॥

therefore ego and the rest are imagined in the Self, as though they were the Self.

Text 130

2 · 130

तस्मादज्ञानविजृम्भितमेतत् ।

This is thus an expansion of ignorance.

Text 131

2 · 131

दृश्याः शब्दादयः क्लृप्ता द्रष्टृ च ब्रह्म निर्गुणम् ।

Objects such as sound are imagined as seen; the seer is Brahman, without qualities;

Verse 2.46

2.46

अहं तदुभयं बिभ्रद्भ्रान्तिमात्मनि यच्छति ॥४६॥

I, bearing both, casts delusion upon the Self.

Text 133

2 · 133

तत एवेयमभिन्नस्यात्मनो भेदबुद्धिः ।

It is for this very reason that difference is imagined in the undivided Self.

Text 134

2 · 134

दृगेका सर्वभूतेषु भाति दृश्यैरनेकवत् ।

One seeing shines in all beings, yet appears as many through the things seen;

Verse 2.47

2.47

जलभाजनभेदेन मयूखस्रग्विभेदवत् ॥ ४७॥

as [one sun’s] rays appear divided among different vessels of water.

Text 136

2 · 136

यथोक्तार्थस्य प्रतिपत्तये दृष्टान्तः ।

An example is given to help understand the point:

Text 137

2 · 137

मित्रोदासीनशत्रुत्वं यथैकस्यान्यकल्पनात् ।

One person is imagined as a friend, a stranger, or an enemy by another;

Verse 2.48

2.48

अभिन्नस्य चित्तेस्तद्वद्भेदोऽन्तःकरणाश्रयः ॥ ४८॥

so one undivided consciousness appears divided, depending on the inner instrument.

Text 139

2 · 139

अपहारो यथा भानोः सर्वतो जलपात्रकैः ।

As the sun appears obscured by vessels of water all around,

Verse 2.49

2.49

तत्क्रियाकृतिदेशाप्तिस्तथा बुद्धिभिरात्मनः ॥ ४९॥

so the Self’s action, form, and location are attributed to it by the intellect.

Text 141

2 · 141

न च विरुद्धधर्मणामेकत्रानुपपत्तिः ।

Contradictory qualities cannot really abide in one thing;

Text 142

2 · 142

कल्पितानामवस्तुत्वात्स्यादेकत्रापि संभवः ।

but since they are imagined and unreal, they may appear in one place,

Verse 2.50

2.50

कमनीयाशुचिः स्वाद्वीत्येकस्यामिव योषिति ॥ ५०॥

as one woman may be thought desirable, impure, or delicious.

Text 144

2 · 144

न चायं क्रियाकारकफलात्मक आभास ईषदपि परमार्थवस्तु स्पृशति तस्य मोहमात्रोपादानत्वात् ।

This appearance of action, agent, and result does not touch reality even slightly, since it is caused only by delusion.

Text 145

2 · 145

अभूताभिनिवेशेन स्वात्मानं वञ्चयत्ययम् ।

Through clinging to something unreal, a person deceives himself,

Verse 2.51

2.51

असत्यपि द्वितीयेऽर्थे सोमशर्मपिता यथा ॥ ५१॥

even when no second object exists, as in the story of Somaśarman’s father.

Text 147

2 · 147

वस्तुयाथात्म्यानवबोधपटलावनद्धाक्षः सन् ।

His eyes are veiled by ignorance of things as they truly are;

Text 148

2 · 148

सुभ्रूः सुनासा सुमुखी सुनेत्रा चारुहासिनी ।

“beautiful-browed, fine-nosed, lovely-faced, beautiful-eyed, with a charming smile”—

Verse 2.52

2.52

कल्पनामात्रसंमोहाद्रामेत्यालिङ्गतेऽशुचिम् ॥ ५२॥

deluded by mere imagination, he embraces an impure woman, calling her Rāmā.

Text 150

2 · 150

सर्वस्यानर्थजातस्य जिहासितस्य मूलमहंकार एव तस्यात्मनात्मोपरागात् । न तु परमार्थत आत्मनोऽविद्यया तत्कार्येण वा संबधोऽभूदस्ति भविष्यति वा तस्यापरिलिप्तदृष्टिस्वाभाव्यात् ।

The ego is the root of every kind of misery to be abandoned, because it colors the Self with itself. In truth, the Self has never been, is not, and will never be connected with ignorance or its effects, since vision is by nature unstained.

Text 151

2 · 151

दृश्यानुरक्तं तद्द्रष्टृ दृश्यं द्रष्ट्रनुरञ्जितम् ।

The seer is colored by attachment to what is seen, and what is seen is colored by the seer;

Verse 2.53

2.53

अहंवृत्त्योहयं रक्तं तन्नाशेऽद्वैततात्मनः ॥ ५३॥

both are attached through the ego-thought; when it ceases, the Self is non-dual.

Text 153

2 · 153

इह केचिच्चोदयन्ति योऽयमन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यामनात्मतयोत्सारितोऽहंकारो वाक्यार्थप्रतिपत्तये सोऽयं विपरीतार्थः संवृत्तो यस्मादहं ब्रह्मास्मीति ब्रह्माहंपदार्थयोः सामानाधिकरण्यश्रवणादनात्मार्थेन सामानाधिकरण्यं प्राप्नोति । वक्तव्या च प्रत्यगात्मनि तस्य वृत्तिरिति सोच्यते प्रसिद्धलक्षणागुणवृत्तिभिः ।

Some object: “The ego, which was rejected as not-Self through agreement and exclusion, has now been brought in with the opposite meaning. When the identity ‘I am Brahman’ is heard, the words ‘I’ and ‘Brahman’ are placed in apposition, so ‘I’ must refer to the not-Self. Its use for the inward Self must still be explained.” The answer is given through common, indirect, and figurative usage.

Text 154

2 · 154

नाज्ञासिषमिति प्राह सुषुप्तादुत्थितोऽपि हि ।

Even one who has risen from deep sleep says, “I did not know”;

Verse 2.54

2.54

अयोदाहादिवत्तेन लक्षणं परमात्मनः ॥ ५४॥

through that [memory], the Supreme Self is indicated, as in the case of heated iron and the like.

Text 156

2 · 156

प्रत्यक्त्वादतिसूक्ष्मत्वादात्मदृष्ट्यनुशीलनात् ।

Because the Self is inward, exceedingly subtle, and known through sustained inquiry,

Verse 2.55

2.55

अतो वृत्तीर्विहायान्या ह्यहंवृत्त्योपलक्षते ॥ ५५॥

it is indicated by the “I”-thought after other mental modes have been set aside.

Text 158

2 · 158

आत्मना चाविनाभावमथवा विलयं व्रजेत् ।

Or it is inseparable from the Self, or it dissolves into it;

Verse 2.56

2.56

न तु पक्षान्तरं यायादतश्चाहंधियोच्यते ॥ ५६॥

it does not fall into another category; therefore it is spoken of through the “I”-cognition.

Text 160

2 · 160

कीदृक्पुनर्वस्तु लक्ष्यम् ।

What sort of thing is the reality being indicated?

Text 161

2 · 161

नामादिभ्यः परो भूमा निष्कलोऽकारकोऽक्रियः ।

Beyond name and the rest is the highest, partless, non-agent, actionless reality;

Verse 2.57

2.57

स एवात्मवतामात्मा स्वतस्सिद्धः स एव नः ॥ ५७॥

that alone is the Self of those who know the Self; it is self-established, and it is ours.

Text 163

2 · 163

अज्ञानोत्थबुद्ध्यादिकर्तृत्वोपाधिमात्मानं परिगृह्यैवान्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यामहं सुखी दुःखी चेत्यहंकारादेरनात्मधर्मत्वमुक्तं केवलात्माभ्युपगमेऽशक्यत्वात्फलाभावाच्च ।

The “I” and the rest were said to be properties of the not-Self by agreement and exclusion, because one takes the Self to be qualified by agency and other conditions that arise from ignorance. The pure Self alone cannot be “happy” or “sad,” and accepting it as such would bring no result.

Text 164

2 · 164

अथेदानीमविद्यापरिकल्पितं साक्षित्वमाश्रित्यकर्तृत्वाद्यशेषपरिणामप्रतिषेधायाह ।

Now, relying on the witnessing status imagined through ignorance, he refutes every change, agency, and other modification:

Text 165

2 · 165

एष सर्वधियां नृत्तमविलुप्तैकदर्शनः ।

This one, whose single vision never ceases, beholds the dance of all intellects;

Verse 2.58

2.58

वीक्षतेऽवीक्षमाणोऽपि निमिषत्तद्ध्रुवोऽध्रुवम् ॥ ५८॥

even while not seeing, it sees what is steady and what is fleeting, through the blinking of the eye.

Text 167

2 · 167

ननु सर्वसिद्धान्तानामपि स्वस्वदृष्ट्यपेक्षयोपपन्नत्वादितरेतरदृष्ट्यपेक्षया दुस्स्थितसिद्धिकत्वान्नैकत्रापि विश्वासं पश्यामो न च सर्वतार्किकैरदूषितं समर्थितं सर्वतार्किकोपद्रवापसर्पणाय वर्त्म संभावयामः ।

One may object: “Every philosophical system seems sound from its own point of view, yet fails from the standpoint of the others. We can trust none of them, nor can we find a path that has been defended without objection by all logicians.”

Text 168

2 · 168

उच्यते । विस्रब्धैः संभाव्यतामनुभवमात्रशरणत्वात् सर्वतार्किकप्रस्थानानाम् । तदभिधीयते ।

The answer: consider it without anxiety, since every system of reasoning rests on direct experience. It is said:

Text 169

2 · 169

इमं प्राशिकमुद्दिश्य तर्कज्वरभृशातुराः ।

Addressing this noble person,

Verse 2.59

2.59

त्वाच्छिरस्कवचोजालैर्मोहयन्तीतरेतरम् ॥ ५९॥

“Those grievously afflicted by the fever of argument bewilder one another with a web of words for armor.”

Text 171

2 · 171

अत्रापि चोदयन्ति । अनुभवात्मनोऽपि विक्रियाभ्युपगमेऽनभ्युपगमेऽपि दोष एव । यस्मादाह ।

An objection is raised here: “Whether one accepts change or no change in the Self that is experience itself, a fault follows.” For it is said:

Text 172

2 · 172

"वर्षातपाभ्यां किं व्योम्नश्चर्मण्येव तयोः फलम् ।

“What do rain and sunlight do to space? Their effects fall only on leather.

Verse 2.60

2.60

चर्मोपमश्चेत्सोऽनित्यः खतुल्यश्चेदसत्समः " ॥ ६०॥

If the Self is like leather, it is impermanent; if like space, it is unreal.”

Text 174

2 · 174

बुद्धिजन्मनि पुंसश्च विकृतिर्यद्यनित्यता ।

If the person changes when the intellect arises, then he is impermanent;

Verse 2.61

2.61

अथाविकृतिरेवायं प्रमातेति न युज्यते ॥ ६१॥

if he is indeed changeless, then he cannot be the knower—neither view is tenable.

Text 176

2 · 176

अस्य परिहारः ।

The answer to this is:

Text 177

2 · 177

ऊर्ध्वं गच्छति धूमे खं भिद्यते स्विन्न भिद्यते ।

Smoke rises into the sky; is the sky split or not split?

Verse 2.62

2.62

न भिद्यते चेत्स्थास्नुत्वं भिद्यते चेद्भिदास्य का ॥ ६२॥

If it is not split, it remains whole; if it is split, what is the division?

Text 179

2 · 179

इत्येतत्प्रतिपत्त्यर्थमाह ।

To explain this point, he says:

Text 180

2 · 180

अविक्रियस्य भोक्तृत्वं स्यादहंबुद्धिविभ्रमात् ।

The changeless Self seems to enjoy through the delusion of the thought “I,”

Verse 2.63

2.63

नौयानविभ्रमाद्यद्वन्नगेषु गतिकल्पनम् ॥ ६३॥

just as motion is imagined in mountains from the motion of a boat.

Text 182

2 · 182

यथोक्तार्थाविष्करणाय दृष्टान्तान्तरोपादानम् ।

Another example is given to make the point clear:

Text 183

2 · 183

यथा जात्यमणेः शुभ्रा ज्वलन्ती निश्चला शिखा ।

Like the bright, clear, motionless flame of a precious jewel,

Verse 2.64

2.64

संनिध्यसंनिधानेषु घटादीनामविक्रिया ॥ ६४॥

the pots and other things do not change whether near or far.

Text 185

2 · 185

अयमत्रांशो विवक्षित इति ज्ञापनायाह ।

To indicate the particular point intended here, he says:

Text 186

2 · 186

यदवस्था व्यनक्तीति तदवस्थैव सा पुनः ।

It is said to be in the same state as that which it reveals;

Verse 2.65

2.65

भण्यते न व्यनक्तीति घटादीनामसंनिधौ ॥ ६५॥

when pots and the like are absent, it is said not to reveal them.

Text 188

2 · 188

तत्र च ।

And in this case,

Text 189

2 · 189

सर्वधीव्यञ्जकस्तद्वत्परमात्मा प्रदीपकः ।

the Supreme Self, like a lamp, illuminates every intellect;

Verse 2.66

2.66

संनिध्यसंनिधानेषु धीवृत्तिनामविक्रियः ॥ ६६॥

it is changeless through the presence or absence of mental states.

Text 191

2 · 191

न प्रकाशक्रिया काचिदस्य स्वात्मनि विद्यते ।

There is no act of illumination in the Self itself;

Verse 2.67

2.67

उपचारात्क्रिया सास्य यः प्रकाशस्य संनिधिः ॥ ६७॥

that act is figuratively attributed to it when illumination is near.

Text 193

2 · 193

मैवं शङ्किष्ठाः सांख्यराध्यान्तोऽयमिति । यतः ।

Do not suspect that this is merely the Sāṃkhya conclusion, because:

Text 194

2 · 194

यथा विशुद्ध आकाशे सहसैवाभ्रमण्डलम् ।

just as a mass of clouds appears suddenly in the pure sky

Verse 2.68

2.68

भूत्वा विलीयते तद्वदात्मनीहाखिलं जगत् ॥ ६८॥

and then disappears, so the entire universe arises in the Self and dissolves there.

Text 196

2 · 196

तस्मादेष कूटस्थो न द्वैतं मनागपि स्पृशति । यतः ।

Therefore this immutable Self is not touched by duality in the least. For:

Text 197

2 · 197

शब्दाद्याकारनिर्भासाः क्षणप्रध्वंसिनीर्दृशा ।

The forms of sound and the rest flash forth in awareness and vanish in an instant;

Verse 2.69

2.69

नित्योऽक्रमदृगात्मैको व्याप्नोतीव धियोऽनिशम् ॥ ६९॥

the eternal, non-sequential, single seeing Self pervades the intellects continually, as it were.

Text 199

2 · 199

एवं च सति बुद्धेः परिणामित्वं युक्तम् ।

Thus it is reasonable that the intellect changes.

Text 200

2 · 200

अतीतानागतेहत्यान्युगपत्सर्वगोचरान् ।

Past, future, and destroyed things, and all objects in their entirety, are present at once.

Verse 2.70

2.70

वेत्त्यात्मवन्न धीर्यस्मात्तेनेयं परिणामिनी ॥ ७०॥

Since it does not know the Self as itself, the intellect is what undergoes change.

Text 202

2 · 202

ततश्चैतत्सिद्धम् ।

From this the point is established.

Text 203

2 · 203

अपश्यन्पश्यतीं बुद्धिमशृण्वन् शृण्वतीं तथा ।

Not seeing, it sees the intellect that sees; not hearing, it hears the intellect that hears;

Verse 2.71

2.71

निर्यत्नोऽविक्रियोऽनिच्छन्निच्छन्तीं चाप्यलुप्तदृक् ॥ ७१॥

effortless, changeless, without desire, with vision undimmed, it sees the intellect that desires.

Text 205

2 · 205

द्विषन्तीमद्विषन्नात्मा कुप्यन्तीं चाप्यकोपनः ।

Not hating, it sees the intellect that hates; not angry, it sees the intellect that is angry;

Verse 2.72

2.72

निर्दुःखो दुःखिनीं चैव निस्सुखः सुखिनीमपि ॥ ७२॥

free of suffering, it sees the suffering intellect; not happy, it sees the intellect that is happy.

Text 207

2 · 207

अमुह्यमानो मुह्यन्तीं कल्पयन्तीमकल्पयन् ।

Not deluded, it sees the intellect that is deluded; not imagining, it sees the intellect that imagines;

Verse 2.73

2.73

स्मरन्तीमस्मरंश्चैव शयानामस्वपन्मुहुः ॥ ७३॥

not remembering, it sees the intellect that remembers; it remains awake while that intellect repeatedly sleeps.

Text 209

2 · 209

सर्वाकारां निराकारः स्वार्थोऽस्वार्थां निरिङ्गनः ।

Formless, it sees every form; motionless, it sees what is directed toward objects;

Verse 2.74

2.74

निस्त्रिकालस्त्रिकालस्थां कूटस्थः क्षणभङ्गुराम् ॥ ७४॥

beyond the three times, it sees what stands in time; changeless, it sees what perishes in a moment.

Text 211

2 · 211

निरपेक्षश्च सापेक्षां पराचीं प्रत्यगद्वयः ।

Independent, it sees what depends on others; inward and non-dual, it sees what is turned outward;

Verse 2.75

2.75

सावधिं निर्गतेयत्तः सर्वदेहेषु पश्यति ॥ ७५॥

without limit, it sees what is limited in all bodies.

Text 213

2 · 213

एतस्माच्च कारणादयमर्थो व्यवसीयताम् ।

For this reason the point should be understood:

Text 214

2 · 214

दुःखी यदि भवेदात्मा कस्साक्षी दुःखिनो भवेत् ।

If the Self were the sufferer, who would witness the one who suffers?

Verse 2.76

2.76

दुःखिनः साक्षितायुक्ता साक्षिणो दुःखिता तथा ॥ ७६॥

If the sufferer were the witness, the witness would be subject to suffering too.

Text 216

2 · 216

पूर्वस्यैव व्याख्यानार्थमाह ।

He explains the preceding statement:

Text 217

2 · 217

नर्ते स्याद्विक्रियां दुःखी साक्षिता का विकारिणः ।

A sufferer cannot be a witness without undergoing change; how could something changing be the witness?

Verse 2.77

2.77

धीविक्रियासहस्राणां साक्ष्यतोऽहमविक्रियः ॥ ७७॥

I am the witness of thousands of changes in the intellect; therefore I am changeless.

Text 219

2 · 219

एवं सर्वस्मिन् व्यभिचारिण्यात्मवस्त्वेवाव्यभिचारीत्यनुभवतो व्यवस्थापनायाह ।

To establish through experience that the Self alone is unchanging amid all that changes, he says:

Text 220

2 · 220

प्रमाणतन्निभेष्वस्या नोच्छित्तिर्मम संविदः ।

No means of knowledge, nor their appearances, can bring my awareness to an end;

Verse 2.78

2.78

मत्तोऽन्यद्रूपमाभाति यत्तत्स्यात्क्षणभङ्गि हि ॥ ७८॥

whatever form other than me appears is indeed momentary.

Text 222

2 · 222

उत्पत्तिस्थितिभङ्गेषु कुम्भस्य वियतो यथा ।

As a pot undergoes arising, persistence, and destruction, while space does not,

Verse 2.79

2.79

नोत्पत्तिस्थितिनाशास्स्युर्बुद्धेरेवं ममापि च ॥७९॥

so arising, persistence, and destruction do not belong to awareness, nor to me.

Text 224

2 · 224

सुखदुःखसंबन्धानां च प्रत्यक्षत्वान्न श्रद्धामात्रग्राह्यमेतत् ।

The connection with pleasure, pain, and the like is directly experienced; it is not accepted merely on faith.

Text 225

2 · 225

सुखदुःखादिसंबन्धां यथा दण्डेन दण्डिनम् ।

Just as one who sees a person with a stick recognizes him through the stick,

Verse 2.80

2.80

राधको वीक्षते बुद्धिं साक्षी तद्वदसंहतः ॥ ८०॥

so the unattached witness sees the intellect connected with pleasure and the rest.

Text 227

2 · 227

एतस्माच्च हेतोर्धियः परिणामित्वं युक्तम् ।

This is another reason why the intellect undergoes change:

Text 228

2 · 228

येनैवास्या भवेद्योगः सुखकुम्भादिना धियः ।

the intellect knows an object connected with it, such as happiness or a pot;

Verse 2.81

2.81

तं विदन्ती तदैवान्यं वेत्ति नातो विकारिणी ॥ ८१॥

then it knows another object; therefore it is changeful.

Text 230

2 · 230

अस्याश्च क्षणभङ्गरत्वे स्वयमेवात्मा साक्षी । न हि कूटस्थावबोधमन्तरेण बुद्धेरेवाविर्भावतिरोभावादिसिद्धिरस्ति ।

The Self itself witnesses the momentary nature of the intellect. Without changeless awareness, the intellect’s arising and disappearance could not be established.

Text 231

2 · 231

परिणामिधियां वृत्तं नित्याक्रमदृगात्मना ।

The events of changing intellects are pervaded by the eternal, non-sequential seeing Self,

Verse 2.82

2.82

षड्भावविक्रियामेति व्याप्तं खेनाङ्करो यथा ॥ ८२॥

and undergo the six modifications, just as space pervades a sprout.

Text 233

2 · 233

सत आत्मनश्चाविकारित्वे युक्तिः ।

Here is the reasoning for the changelessness of the existent Self:

Text 234

2 · 234

स्मृतिस्वप्नप्रबोधेषु न कश्चित्प्रत्ययो धियः ।

In memory, dream, and waking, there is no cognition of the intellect

Verse 2.83

2.83

दृशाव्याप्तोऽस्त्यतो नित्यमविकारी स्वयंदृशिः ॥ ८३॥

that is not pervaded by seeing; therefore the seeing Self itself is eternal and changeless.

Text 236

2 · 236

एवं तावत्पराभ्युपगतप्रक्रियाप्रस्थानेन निरस्ताशेषविकारैकात्म्यं प्रतिपादितमुपपत्तिभिः । अथाधुना श्रौतीं प्रक्रियामवलम्ब्योच्यते ।

Thus, using the position admitted by the opponent, it has been shown through reasoning that the one Self is free from every change. Now the point is stated by relying on the scriptural method.

Text 237

2 · 237

अस्तु वा परिणामोऽस्य दृशेः कूटस्थरूपतः ।

Even if a change in the seeing Self were admitted, it would be only imagined, since its nature is changeless;

Verse 2.84

2.84

कल्पितोऽपि मृषैवासौ दण्डस्येवाप्सु वक्रता ॥ ८४॥

even that imagined change is false, like the bending of a staff [as it appears] in water.

Text 239

2 · 239

षट्सु भावविकारेषु निषिद्धेश्वेवमात्मनि ।

The six changes of being are denied of the Self; therefore,

Verse 2.85

2.85

दोषः कश्चिदिहासक्तुं न शक्यस्तार्किकश्वभिः ॥ ८५॥

no fault can be attached to it here, even by the logicians who bark.

Text 241

2 · 241

प्रकृतमेवोपादाय बुद्धेः परिणामित्वमात्मनश्च कूटस्थत्वं युक्तिभिरुच्यते ।

The reasoning is now stated, taking up the same subject: the intellect changes, while the Self remains immutable.

Text 242

2 · 242

प्रत्यर्थं तु विभिद्यन्ते बुद्धयो विषयोन्मुखाः ।

Intellects differ according to the objects toward which they turn;

Verse 2.86

2.86

न भिदावगतेस्तद्वत्सर्वास्ताश्चिन्निभा यतः ॥ ८६॥

awareness itself is not divided, since all intellects are lit by the same consciousness.

Text 244

2 · 244

स्वसंबद्धार्थ एव ।

Each intellect knows only the object connected with itself;

Text 245

2 · 245

सावशेषपरिच्छेदिन्यत एव न कृत्स्नवित् ।

it grasps only a limited part, and therefore is not all-knowing.

Verse 2.87

2.87

नो चेत्परिणमेद्बुद्धिः सर्वज्ञा स्वात्मवद्भवेत् ॥ ८७॥

If the intellect did not change, it would be all-knowing, like the Self.

Text 247

2 · 247

अतोऽवगतेरेकत्वात् ।

Since awareness is one,

Text 248

2 · 248

चण्डालबुद्धेर्यर्दृ।र्ष्टृ तदेव ब्रह्मबुद्धिदृक् ।

what sees the intellect of an outcaste is the very same seeing that knows the thought of Brahman;

Verse 2.88

2.88

एकं तदुभयोर्ज्योतिर्भास्यभेदादनेकवत् ॥ ८८॥

the light in both is one, appearing as many through the difference of the objects it illuminates.

Text 250

2 · 250

कस्मात् ।

Why? Because

Text 251

2 · 251

अवस्थादेशकालादिभेदो नास्त्यनयोर्यतः ।

there is no difference between them in state, place, or time;

Verse 2.89

2.89

तस्माज्जगद्धियां वृत्तं ज्योतिरेकं सदेक्षते ॥ ८९॥

one eternal light sees all the movements of the intellects in the world.

Text 253

2 · 253

सर्वदेहेष्वात्मैकत्वे प्रतिबुद्धपरमार्थतत्त्वस्याप्यप्रतिबुद्धदेहसंबद्धादशेषदुःखसंबन्ध इति चेत्तन्न ।

One might object: “If the Self is one in every body, then even one awakened to the truth would remain connected with every suffering associated with other bodies.” That is not so.

Text 254

2 · 254

बोधात्प्रागपि दुःखित्वं नान्यदेहोत्थमस्ति नः ।

Even before awakening, our suffering did not arise from another person’s body;

Verse 2.90

2.90

बोधादूर्ध्वं कुतस्तत्स्याद्यत्र स्वगतमप्यसत् ॥ ९०॥

why should it arise after knowledge, when even what is within one’s own body is false?

Text 256

2 · 256

न चेयं स्वमनीषिकेति ग्राह्यम् । कुतः ।

This should not be taken as my own unsupported opinion. Why?

Text 257

2 · 257

श्रुत्यवष्टम्भात् ।

Because it is grounded in revelation.

Text 258

2 · 258

शब्दाद्याकारनिर्भासा हानोपादानधर्मिणी ।

Forms such as sound appear and are subject to acceptance and rejection;

Verse 2.91

2.91

भास्येत्याह श्रुतिर्दृष्टिरात्मनोऽपरिणामिनः ॥ ९१॥

scripture calls them objects of awareness, while the Self is changeless seeing.

Text 260

2 · 260

का त्वसौ श्रुतिः ।

Which passage of revelation?

Text 261

2 · 261

दृष्टेर्द्रष्टारमात्मानं न पश्येर्दृश्यमानया ।

“You cannot see the seer of seeing through what is seen;

Verse 2.92

2.92

विज्ञातारमरे केन विजानीयाद्धियां पतिम् ॥ ९२॥

by what, dear one, could you know the knower, the lord of the intellect?”

Text 263

2 · 263

यस्मात्सर्वप्रमाणोपपन्नोऽयमर्थस्तस्मादतोऽन्यथावादिनो जात्यन्धा इवानुकम्पनीया इत्याह ।

Since this point is established by every means of knowledge, those who argue otherwise are like people blind from birth and deserve compassion.

Text 264

2 · 264

तदेतदद्वयं ब्रह्म निर्विकारं कुबुद्धिभिः ।

This non-dual Brahman is changeless, yet people of poor understanding

Verse 2.93

2.93

जात्यन्धगजदृष्ट्येव कोटिशः परिकल्प्यते ॥ ९३॥

imagine it in countless ways, like blind men describing an elephant.

Text 266

2 · 266

प्रमाणोपपनस्यार्थस्यासंभावनात्तदनुकम्पनीयत्वसिद्धिः ।

Since they cannot accept what is established by means of knowledge, they are indeed to be pitied.

Text 267

2 · 267

तदेतदाह ।

He states the point:

Text 268

2 · 268

यद्यद्विशेषणं दृष्टं नात्मनस्तदनन्वयात् ।

Whatever distinction is seen does not belong to the Self, because it is not continuous with it;

Verse 2.94

2.94

खस्य कुम्भादिवत्तस्मादात्मा स्यान्निर्विशेषणः ॥ ९४॥

just as pots and the like do not belong to space, the Self is without distinctions.

Text 270

2 · 270

अतश्चात्मनो भेदासंस्पर्शो भेदस्य मिथ्यास्वाभाव्यादत आह ।

Difference does not touch the Self because its nature is false. Therefore he says:

Text 271

2 · 271

अवगत्यात्मनो यस्मादागमापायि कुम्भवत् ।

What is perceived comes and goes, like a pot;

Verse 2.95

2.95

साहंकारमिदं विश्वं तस्मात्तत्स्यात्कचादिवत् ॥ ९५॥

therefore this world, together with ego, is as transient as hair and the like.

Text 273

2 · 273

सर्वस्यैवानुमानव्यापारस्य फलमियदेव यद्विवेकग्रहणम् ।

The entire work of inference is only this: to grasp a distinction.

Text 274

2 · 274

तदुच्यते ।

This is stated:

Text 275

2 · 275

बुद्धेरनात्मधर्मत्वमनुमानात्प्रसिद्ध्यति ।

Inference establishes that the intellect’s qualities belong to the not-Self;

Verse 2.96

2.96

आत्मनोऽप्यद्वितीयत्वमात्मत्वादेव सिद्ध्यति ॥ ९६॥

the Self’s non-duality is established simply by its being the Self.

Text 277

2 · 277

यद्यप्ययं ग्रहीतृग्रहणग्राह्यगृहीतितत्फलात्मक आब्रह्मस्तम्बपर्यन्तः संसारोऽन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यामनात्मतया निर्माल्यवदपविद्धः । तथापि तु नैवासौ स्वतस्सिद्धात्म व्यतिरिक्तानात्मप्रकृतिपदार्थव्यपाश्रयः सांख्यानामिव ।

Though saṃsāra, from Brahmā down to a clump of grass, consisting of knower, knowing, known, and their results, has been discarded as not-Self through agreement and exclusion, like a withered garland, it does not depend on a material principle separate from the self-established Self, as the Sāṃkhyas claim.

Text 278

2 · 278

किं तर्हि ।

What, then, is its basis?

Text 279

2 · 279

स्वतस्सिद्धानुदितानस्तमितकूटस्थात्मप्रज्ञानमात्रशरीरप्रतिबिम्बिताविचारितसिद्धात्मानवबोधाश्रय एव तदुपादानत्वात्तस्येतीममर्थं निर्वक्तुकाम आह ।

It rests on ignorance of the Self, which is not examined and is reflected in the self-established, unborn, unceasing, immutable awareness of the Self. Ignorance is its material cause. To explain this point, he says:

Text 280

2 · 280

ऋते ज्ञानं न सन्त्यर्था अस्ति ज्ञानमृतेऽपि तान् ।

Objects do not exist apart from knowledge; knowledge exists even apart from them.

Verse 2.97

2.97

एवं धियो हिरुग्ज्योतिर्विविच्यादनुमानतः ॥ ९७॥

Thus, by inference, one should distinguish the intellect from the light of awareness.

Text 282

2 · 282

यस्मात्प्रमाणप्रमेयव्यवहार आत्मानवबोधाश्रय एव तस्मात्सिद्धमात्मनोऽप्रमेयत्वम् । नैव हि कार्यं स्वकारणमतिलङ्घ्यान्यत्राकारक आस्पदमुपनिबध्नाति ।

Since the dealings between means of knowledge and their objects rest only on ignorance of the Self, the Self is established as beyond objectification. An effect does not set its own cause aside and find a footing elsewhere.

Text 283

2 · 283

अत आह ।

Therefore he says:

Text 284

2 · 284

व्यवधीयन्त एवामी बुद्धिदेहघटादयः ।

The intellect, body, pot, and the rest are all intermediaries;

Verse 2.98

2.98

आत्मत्वादात्मनः केन व्यवधानं मनागपि ॥ ९८॥

what, even slightly, could stand between the Self and itself?

Text 286

2 · 286

स्वयमनवगमात्मकत्वादनवगमात्कत्वं च मोहमात्रोपादानत्वात् ।

Their non-awareness is produced by their own ignorance, and that ignorance

Text 287

2 · 287

प्रमाणमन्तरेणैषां बुद्ध्यादीनामसिद्धता ।

is caused only by delusion; the intellect and the rest are not established without means of knowledge.

Verse 2.99

2.99

अनुभूतिफलार्थित्वादात्मा ज्ञः किमपेक्षते ॥ ९९॥

What could the Self depend on, when its result is direct experience?

Text 289

2 · 289

वक्ष्यमाणेतरेतराध्याससिद्ध्यर्थमुक्तव्यतिरेकानुवादः ।

The earlier distinction is repeated to establish the mutual superimposition that will be explained next:

Text 290

2 · 290

घटबुद्धेर्घटाच्चर्थाद्द्रष्टुर्यद्वद्विभिन्नता ।

Just as a pot is different from the cognition “pot,”

Verse 2.100

2.100

अहंबुद्धेरहंगम्याद्दुःखिनश्च तथा दृशेः ॥ १००॥

so seeing is different from the cognition “I” and from the sufferer.

Text 292

2 · 292

एवमेतयोरात्मानात्मनोः स्वतः परतः सिद्धयोर्लौकिकरज्जु सर्पाध्यारोपवदविद्योपाश्रय एवेतरेतराध्यारोप इत्येतदाह ।

Thus the mutual superimposition of the Self and not-Self—each established in itself and in the other—is supported only by ignorance, like the snake superimposed on a rope.

Text 293

2 · 293

अभ्रयानं यथा मोहाच्छशभृत्यध्यवस्यति ।

Just as, through delusion, one thinks the moon is moving with the clouds,

Verse 2.101

2.101

सुखित्वादीन्धियो धर्मांस्तद्वदात्मनि मन्यते ॥ १०१ ॥

so one regards qualities of the intellect, such as happiness, as belonging to the Self.

Text 295

2 · 295

दग्धृत्वं च यथा वह्नेरयसो मन्यते कुधीः ।

Just as an ignorant person thinks the power to burn belongs to iron, though it belongs to fire,

Verse 2.102

2.102

चैतन्यं तद्वदात्मीयं मोहात्कर्तरि मन्यते ॥ १०२॥

so, through delusion, one thinks that consciousness belonging to the Self belongs to the agent.

Text 297

2 · 297

सर्व एवायमात्मानात्मविभागः प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणवर्त्मन्युनपतितोऽविद्योत्सङ्गवर्त्येव न परमात्मव्यपाश्रयः । अस्याश्चाविद्यायाः सर्वानर्थहेतोः कुतो निवृत्तिरिति चेत्तदाह ।

The whole division between Self and not-Self that appears through perception and the other means of knowledge exists only in the lap of ignorance; it does not depend on the Supreme Self. How is this ignorance removed? He says:

Text 298

2 · 298

दुःखराशेर्विचित्रस्य सेयं भ्रान्तिश्चिरन्तनी ।

This ancient delusion, source of a manifold mass of suffering,

Verse 2.103

2.103

मूलं संसारवृक्षस्य तद्बाधस्तत्त्वदर्शनात् ॥ १०३॥

is the root of the tree of saṃsāra; it is removed by seeing reality.

Text 300

2 · 300

तद्बाधस्तत्त्वदर्शनादिति कुतः संभाव्यत इति चेदत आहागोपालाविपालपण्डितमियमेव प्रसिद्धिः ।

If one asks how seeing reality could remove it, that is well known to everyone, from cowherd to scholar:

Text 301

2 · 301

अप्रमोत्थं प्रमोत्थेन ज्ञानं ज्ञानेन बाध्यते ।

knowledge arising from a valid cognition sublates cognition born of error,

Verse 2.104

2.104

अहिरज्ज्वादिवद्बाधो देहाद्यात्ममतेस्तथा ॥ १०४॥

as the knowledge “rope” sublates the notion that the body and the rest are the Self.

Text 303

2 · 303

लौकिकप्रमेयवैलक्षण्यादात्मनो नेहानधिगताधिगमः प्रमाणफलम् ।

Because the Self differs from ordinary objects of knowledge, the result of a means of knowledge is not the acquisition of something previously unknown.

Text 304

2 · 304

अविद्यानाशमात्रं तु फलमित्युपचर्यते ।

The mere destruction of ignorance is figuratively called its result.

Verse 2.105

2.105

नाज्ञातज्ञापनं न्याय्यमवगत्येकरूपतः ॥ १०५॥

It is not reasonable to say that knowledge reveals something unknown, since awareness is of one nature.

Text 306

2 · 306

यस्मादात्मानवबोधमात्रोपादानाः प्रमात्रादयस्तस्मात् ।

Since the knower and the rest are produced only by ignorance of the Self,

Text 307

2 · 307

न विदन्त्यात्मनः सत्तां द्रष्टृदर्शनगोचराः ।

they do not know the Self’s existence as an object, an act of knowing, or a knower;

Verse 2.106

2.106

न चान्योन्यमतोऽमीषां ज्ञेयत्वं भिन्नसाधनम् ॥ १०६॥

nor can they know one another through some separate means.

Text 309

2 · 309

द्रष्ट्रादेरसाधारणरूपज्ञापनायाह ।

To show the distinctive nature of the knower and the rest, he says:

Text 310

2 · 310

बाह्य आकारवान् ग्राह्यो ग्रहणं निश्चयादिमत् ।

An external object is grasped as having a form; cognition is marked by certainty and the like;

Verse 2.107

2.107

अन्वय्यहमिति ज्ञेयः साक्षी त्वात्मा ध्रुवः सदा ॥ १०७॥

the witness is known continuously as “I,” and the Self is always constant.

Text 312

2 · 312

सर्वकारकक्रियाफलविभागात्त्मकसंसारशून्य आत्मेति कारकक्रियाफलविभागसाक्षित्वादात्मनस्तदाह ।

The Self is free of saṃsāra, which consists of the divisions of all agents, actions, and results. Because the Self witnesses those divisions, he says:

Text 313

2 · 313

ग्राहकग्रहणग्राह्यविभागे योऽविभागवान् ।

Amid the divisions of knower, knowing, and known, the undivided one

Verse 2.108

2.108

हानोपादानयोस्साक्षी हानोपादानवर्जितः ॥ १०८॥

witnesses both acceptance and rejection, while itself free of either.

Text 315

2 · 315

ग्राहकादिनिष्ठैव ग्राहकादिभावाभावविभागसिद्धिः कस्मान्नेति चेत्तदाह ।

If one asks why the divisions of knower and the rest cannot be established by themselves, he says:

Text 316

2 · 316

स्वसाधन स्वयं नष्टो न नाशं वेत्त्यभावतः ।

An instrument, when destroyed, does not know its own destruction, because it is no longer present;

Verse 2.109

2.109

अत एव न चान्येषामतोऽसौ भिन्नसाक्षिकः ॥ १०९॥

nor can it know the destruction of anything else; therefore this witness is not witnessed by another.

Text 318

2 · 318

ग्राहकादेरन्यसाक्षिपूर्वकत्वसिद्धेः स्वसाक्षिणोऽप्यन्यसाक्षिपूर्वकत्वादनवस्थेति चेत्तन्न साक्षिणो व्यतिरिक्तहेत्वनपेक्षत्वादत आह ।

If one argues that the knower and the rest must first be established by another witness, and that this witness too requires another, leading to an infinite regress, that is not so, because the witness does not depend on an external cause. He says:

Text 319

2 · 319

धीवन्नापेक्षते सिद्धिमात्मान्यस्मादविक्रियः ।

Unlike the intellect, the changeless Self needs nothing else to establish it;

Verse 2.110

2.110

निरपेक्षमपेक्ष्यैव सिद्धन्त्यन्ये न तु स्वयम् ॥ ११०॥

other things are established only by depending on something else, never by themselves.

Text 321

2 · 321

यतो ग्राहकादिष्वात्मभावोऽविद्यानिबन्धन एव तस्मादन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां विभज्यानात्मनः स्वयम् ।

Since the “I” notion in the knower and the rest is grounded in ignorance, distinguish it from the Self through agreement and exclusion.

Text 322

2 · 322

उत्पत्तिस्थितिनाशेषु योऽवगत्यैव वर्तते ।

That which remains as awareness alone through the arising, persistence, and destruction

Verse 2.111

2.111

जगतोऽविकारयावेहि तमस्मीति न नश्वरम् ॥ १११॥

of the world—know it as changeless and not subject to decay.

Text 324

2 · 324

स्वतस्सिद्धात्मचैतन्यप्रतिबिम्बिताविचारितसिद्धिकात्मानवबोधोत्थेतरेतरस्वभावापेक्षसिद्धत्वात्स्वतश्चासिद्धेरनात्मनो द्वैतेन्द्रजालस्य ।

This magical duality of the not-Self arises from unexamined ignorance reflected in the self-established awareness of the Self. Its parts depend on one another for their existence and are not established by themselves.

Text 325

2 · 325

न स्वयं स्वस्य नानात्वं नावगत्यात्मना यतः ।

Multiplicity is not known by the Self alone;

Verse 2.112

2.112

नोभाभ्यामप्यतस्सिद्धमद्वैतं द्वैतबाधया ॥ ११२॥

nor is non-duality established by both together; it is established by the sublation of duality.

Text 327

2 · 327

यथोक्तार्थप्रतिपत्तिद्रढिम्ने श्रुत्युदाहरणोपन्यासः ।

A scriptural example is introduced to strengthen understanding of what has been said:

Text 328

2 · 328

नित्यावगतिरूपत्वात्कारकादिर्न चात्मनः ।

Because the Self is the nature of eternal awareness, it has no agency or other such properties;

Verse 2.113

2.113

अस्थूलं नेतिनेतीति न जायत इति श्रुतिः ॥ ११३॥

scripture says, “It is not gross, not this, not this; it is not born.”

Text 330

2 · 330

सर्वस्यास्य ग्राहकादेर्द्वैतप्रपञ्चस्यात्मानवबोधमात्रोपादानस्य स्वयं सेद्धुमशक्यत्वादात्मसिद्धेश्चानुपादेयत्वात् ।

Since this entire dualistic display of knower and the rest rests only on ignorance and cannot establish itself, while the Self is already established and need not be attained,

Text 331

2 · 331

आत्मनश्चेन्निवार्यन्ते बुद्धिदेहघटादयः ।

if intellect, body, pot, and the rest are removed from the Self,

Verse 2.114

2.114

षष्ठगोचरकल्पास्ते विज्ञेयाः परमार्थतः ॥ ११४॥

they should be understood as ultimately no more real than an object seen in a sixth direction.

Text 333

2 · 333

कुतो न्यायबलादेवं निश्चितं प्रतीयते । यस्मात् ।

How is this conclusion reached through reasoning? Because:

Text 334

2 · 334

नित्यां संविदमाश्रित्य स्वतस्सिद्धामविक्रियाम् ।

Relying on eternal awareness, which is self-established and changeless,

Verse 2.115

2.115

सिद्धायन्ते धियो बोधास्तांश्चाश्रित्य घटादयः ॥ ११५॥

the intellect and its cognitions are established; relying on them, pots and the rest are established.

Text 336

2 · 336

यस्मान्न कयाचिदपि युक्त्यात्मनः कारकत्वं क्रियात्वं फलत्वं चोपपद्यते तस्मादात्मवस्तुयाथात्म्यानवबोधमात्रोपादानत्वान्नभसीव रजोधूमतुषार नीहारनीलत्वाद्यध्यासो यथोक्तात्मनि सर्वोऽयं क्रियाकारकफलात्मकसंसारोऽहंममत्वयत्नेच्छादिमिथ्याध्यास एवेति सिद्धमिममर्थमाह ।

Since no reasoning can establish the Self as agent, action, or result, all this saṃsāra—consisting of action, agent, and fruit—is only a false superimposition, like dust, smoke, frost, mist, and blueness upon the sky. Its only basis is ignorance of the true nature of the Self. Thus the ego, “mine,” effort, desire, and the rest are false superimpositions. He states the point:

Text 337

2 · 337

अहंमिथ्याभिशापेन दुःख्यात्मा तद्बुभुत्सया ।

Cursed by the false notion “I,” the Self seems to suffer and longs to know;

Verse 2.116

2.116

इतः श्रुतिं तया नेतीत्युक्तः कैवल्यमास्थितः ॥ ११६॥

through the teaching “not this,” it is led away from this [error] and abides in liberation.

Text 339

2 · 339

तस्यास्य मुमुक्षोः श्रौताद्वचसः स्वप्ननिमित्तोत्सारितनिद्रस्येवेयं निश्चितार्था प्रमा जायते ।

For this seeker, certain knowledge arises from the words of revelation, like one awakened from sleep caused by a dream.

Text 340

2 · 340

नाहं न च ममात्मत्वात्सर्वदानात्मवर्जितः ।

“I am not [the body or mind], nor is anything mine; by nature I am always free of the not-Self;

Verse 2.117

2.117

भानाविव तमोध्यासोऽपह्नवश्च तथा मयि ॥ ११७॥

as darkness is superimposed upon light and then removed, so is error superimposed upon me and removed.”

Text 342

2 · 342

सोऽयमेवंप्रतिपान्नस्वभावमात्मानं प्रतिपन्नोऽनुक्रोशति ।

Having thus understood the nature of the Self, the seeker laments:

Text 343

2 · 343

यत्र त्वस्येति साटोपं कृत्स्नद्वैतनिषेधिनीम् ।

“Where is that emphatic scripture which rejects all duality

Verse 2.118

2.118

प्रोत्सारयन्तीं संसारमप्यश्रौषं न किं श्रुतिम् ॥ ११८॥

and dispels saṃsāra? Why did I not hear it before?”

Text 345

2 · 345

इत्योमित्यवबुद्धात्मा निष्कलोऽकारकोऽक्रियः ।

Understanding the Self through “Om,” he is partless, a non-agent, actionless,

Verse 2.119

2.119

विरक्त इव बुद्ध्यादेरेकाकित्वमुपेयिवान् ॥ ११९॥

and, as if detached from the intellect and the rest, has attained solitude.

Chapter colophon

Adhyāya 2

इति द्वितीयोऽध्यायः ॥ २॥

Thus ends the second chapter.

Adhyāya 3

Chapter heading

Adhyāya 3

अथ तृतीयोऽध्यायः ।

Now begins the third chapter.

Text 2

3 · 2

सर्वोऽयं प्रमितिप्रमाणप्रमेयप्रमातृलक्षण आब्रह्मस्तम्बपर्यन्तो मिथ्याध्यास एवेति बहुश उपपत्तिभिरतिष्ठिपम् । आत्मा च जन्मादिषड्भावविकारवर्जितः कूटस्थबोध एवेति स्फुटीकृतम् । तयोश्च मिथ्याध्यासकूटस्थात्मनोर्नान्तरेणाज्ञानं संबन्धोऽन्यत्र चोदनापरिप्रापितात् यथा "इयमेवर्गग्निः साम" इति । तच्चाज्ञानं स्वात्ममात्र निमित्तं न संभवतीति कस्यचित्कस्मिंश्चिद्विषये भवतीत्यभ्युपगन्तव्यम् । इह च पदार्थद्वयं निर्द्धारितमात्मानात्मा च । तत्रानात्मनस्तावन्नाज्ञानेनाभिसंबन्धः । तस्य हि स्वरूपमेवाज्ञानं न हि स्वतोऽज्ञानस्याज्ञानं घटते । संभवदप्यज्ञानस्वभावेऽज्ञानं कमतिशयं जनयेत् । न च तत्र ज्ञानप्राप्तिरस्ति येन तत्प्रतिषेधात्मकमज्ञानं स्यात् । अनात्मनश्चाज्ञानप्रसूतत्त्वात् । न हि पूर्वसिद्धं सत्ततोलब्धात्मलाभस्य सेत्स्यत आश्रयस्याश्रयि संभवति । तदनपेक्षस्य च तस्य निस्स्वभावत्वात् । एतेभ्य एव हेतुभ्यो नानात्मविषयमज्ञानं संभवतीति ग्राह्यम् । एवं तावन्नानात्मनोऽज्ञानित्वं नापि तद्विषयमज्ञानम् । पारिशेष्यादात्मन एवास्त्वज्ञानं तस्याज्ञोऽस्मीत्यनुभवदर्शनात् । "सोऽहं भगवो मन्त्रविदेवास्मि नात्मवित् "इति श्रुतेः ।

It has been established through many arguments that the whole world, from Brahmā down to a clump of grass, consisting of knower, means of knowledge, objects of knowledge, and cognition, is only a false superimposition. It has also been made clear that the Self is changeless awareness, free from the six modifications beginning with birth. The relation between this false superimposition and the changeless Self requires ignorance; it is not otherwise possible, as in a scriptural sentence such as “this very Ṛc is fire.” That ignorance cannot arise from the Self alone, so it must be admitted to occur in some subject with respect to some object. Two things have been established here: the Self and the not-Self. Ignorance cannot belong to the not-Self: its very nature is ignorance, and ignorance cannot be ignorance of itself. Even if ignorance could belong to something already ignorant, what further ignorance could it produce? Nor can the not-Self be the locus in which knowledge arises, for it is itself produced by ignorance. What already exists cannot have a support in something that comes into existence only afterward. And if ignorance did not depend on it, the not-Self would have no nature of its own. For these reasons, ignorance cannot have the not-Self as its object. Thus the not-Self is neither ignorant nor the object of ignorance. By elimination, ignorance must belong to the Self, since we experience, “I am ignorant,” and scripture says, “I know the mantras, but I do not know the Self.”

Text 3

3 · 3

न चात्मनोऽज्ञानस्वरूपता तस्य चैतन्यमात्रस्वाभाव्या दतिशयश्च संभवति ज्ञानविपरिलोपो ज्ञानप्राप्तेश्च संभवस्तस्य ज्ञानकारित्वात् । न च अज्ञानकार्यत्वं कूटस्थात्मस्वाभाव्यादज्ञानानपेक्षस्य चात्मनः स्वत एव स्वरूपसिद्धेर्युक्तमात्मन एवाज्ञत्वम् । किंविषयं पुनस्तदात्मनोऽज्ञानम् । आत्मविषयमिति ब्रूमः । नन्वात्मनोऽपि ज्ञानस्वरूपत्वादनन्यत्वाच्च ज्ञानप्रकृतित्वादिभ्यश्च हेतुभ्यो नैवाज्ञानं घटते । घटत एव । कथम् ।

But the Self cannot have ignorance as its nature, since its nature is pure consciousness alone. Nor can it contain a further quality called ignorance, since knowledge and ignorance oppose one another. If the Self is the source of knowledge, how could ignorance arise in it? Nor is it reasonable that ignorance should be produced by the Self, whose nature is immutable and whose reality is established by itself, independently of ignorance. What, then, is the object of this ignorance belonging to the Self? We say: the Self itself. One may object that ignorance is impossible because the Self is knowledge itself, is not other than knowledge, and is the source of knowledge. It is possible. How?

Text 4

3 · 4

अज्ञानमात्रनिमित्तत्वात्तद्विभागस्य सर्पात्मतेव रज्ज्वाः ।

The division [between Self and not-Self] is caused only by ignorance, as the snake-appearance is caused by a rope.

Text 5

3 · 5

तस्मात्तदपनुत्तौ द्वैतानर्थाभावः । तदपनोदश्च वाक्यादेव तत्पदपदार्थाभिज्ञस्य । अतो वाक्यव्याख्यानायाध्याय आरभ्यते । तत्र यथोक्तेन प्रकारेण तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्योपनिविष्टपदपदार्थयोः कृतान्वयव्यतिरेकः ।

When ignorance is removed, there is no harm of duality. Its removal comes from the sentence alone, for one who knows the meanings of its words. Therefore this chapter begins to explain the sentence. The word-meanings in “That thou art” are connected through the agreement and exclusion already described.

Text 6

3 · 6

यदा ना तत्त्वमस्यादेर्ब्रह्मास्मीत्यवगच्छति ।

When a person does not understand from “That thou art” that he is Brahman,

Verse 3.1

3.1

प्रध्वस्ताहंममो नैति तदा गीर्मनसोः सृतिम् ॥ १॥

and the notions “I” and “mine” have not been destroyed, the sentence does not enter the course of speech and mind.

Text 8

3 · 8

यदैव तदर्थं त्वमर्थेऽवैति तदैवावाक्यार्थतां प्रतिपद्यते गीर्मनसोः सृतिं न प्रतिपद्यत इति । कुत एतद्ध्यवसीयते । यस्मात् ।

When he understands the meaning of “That” through the meaning of “thou,” the sentence then passes beyond the course of speech and mind. How is this understood? Because:

Text 9

3 · 9

तत्पदं प्रकृतार्थं स्यात्त्वंपदं प्रत्यगात्मनि ।

The word “That” has its stated meaning, while “thou” refers to the inward Self;

Verse 3.2

3.2

नीलोत्पलवदेताभ्यां दुःख्यनात्मत्ववारणे ॥ २॥

together they remove suffering and not-Self, as the words “blue” and “lotus” do.

Text 11

3 · 11

एवं कृतान्वयव्यतिरेको वाक्यादेवावाक्यार्थं प्रतिपद्यत इत्युक्तमतस्तद्व्याख्यानाय सूत्रोपन्यासः ।

It has been said that, after agreement and exclusion, the meaning beyond the sentence is understood from the sentence alone. A concise rule is now stated to explain this:

Text 12

3 · 12

सामानाधिकरण्यं च विशेषणविशेष्यता ।

There is apposition, and a relation between qualifier and qualified;

Verse 3.3

3.3

लक्ष्यलक्षणसंबन्धः पदार्थप्रत्यगात्मनाम् ॥ ३॥

the meanings of the words are related as what is indicated and its indication.

Text 14

3 · 14

अस्मिन् सूत्र उपन्यस्ते कश्चिच्चोदयति योऽयं वाक्यार्थप्रतिपत्तौ पूर्वाध्यायेनान्वयव्यतिरेकलक्षणो न्यायः सर्वकर्मसंन्यासपूर्वकोऽभिहितः किमयं विधिपरिप्रापितः किं वा स्वरसत एवात्र पुमान्प्रवर्तत इति । किंचातः । शृणु ।

When this rule is stated, someone objects: “The method described in the previous chapter—agreement and exclusion, preceded by the renunciation of all action—is required for understanding the sentence. Is a person brought to it by an injunction, or does he engage in it naturally? What follows? Listen.”

Text 15

3 · 15

यद्यात्मवस्तुसाक्षात्करणाय विधिपरिप्रापितोऽयं न्यायस्तदावश्यमात्मवस्तुसाक्षात्करणाय व्यावृत्तशुभाशुभकर्मराशिरेकाग्रमना अन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां यथोक्ताभ्यामात्मदर्शनं करोति ।

If this method is enjoined for direct realization of the Self, then a person must turn away from all good and evil actions, make the mind one-pointed, and realize the Self through the agreement and exclusion described above.

Text 16

3 · 16

अपरिसमाप्यात्मदर्शनं ततः प्रच्यवमान आरूढपतितो भवति । यदि पुनर्यदृच्छातः प्रवर्तते तदा न कश्चिद्दोष इति । विधिपरिप्रापित इति ब्रूमो यत आह ।

If he falls away before completing realization of the Self, he becomes one who has fallen from the path. But if he takes it up by chance, there is no fault. We say it is enjoined, because scripture says:

Text 17

3 · 17

शमादिसाधनः पश्येदात्मन्यात्मानमञ्जसा ।

Using means such as tranquillity, one should clearly see

Verse 3.4

3.4

अन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां त्यक्त्वा युष्मदशेषतः ॥ ४॥

the Self in the Self, having completely set aside the meaning of “you” through agreement and exclusion.

Text 19

3 · 19

युष्मदर्थे परित्यक्ते पूर्वोक्तैर्हेतुभिः श्रुतिः ।

When the meaning of “you” has been abandoned through the reasons already given, scripture

Verse 3.5

3.5

वीक्षापन्नस्य कोऽस्मीति तत्त्वमित्याह सौहृदात् ॥ ५॥

compassionately tells the one who has entered inquiry, “That thou art.”

Text 21

3 · 21

अत्रापि चोदयन्ति सांख्याः । शरीरेन्द्रियमनोबुद्धिष्वनात्मस्वात्मेति निस्सन्धिबन्धनं मिथ्याज्ञानमज्ञानं तन्निबन्धनो ह्यात्मनोऽनेकानर्थसंबन्धस्तस्य चान्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यामेव निरस्तत्त्वान्निर्विषयं तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यं प्राप्तम् । तस्माद्वाक्यस्य चैष महिमा योऽयमात्मानात्मनोर्विभाग इति । तन्निराकरणायेदमुच्यते ।

The Sāṃkhyas object: “Ignorance is the unbroken false cognition ‘I am’ regarding what is not-Self—the body, senses, mind, and intellect. The Self’s many connections with misery are rooted in this ignorance, which has been removed by agreement and exclusion. Therefore sentences such as ‘That thou art’ have no object; their only function is to distinguish the Self from not-Self.” This is answered:

Text 22

3 · 22

भेदसंविदिदं ज्ञानं भेदाभावश्च साक्षिणि ।

The cognition of difference and the absence of difference in the witness

Verse 3.6

3.6

कार्यमेतदविद्याया ज्ञात्मना त्याजयेद्वचः ॥ ६॥

are both effects of ignorance; a sentence removes them through knowledge of the Self.

Text 24

3 · 24

ज्ञात्मना त्याजयेद्वच इत्युपश्रुत्याह कश्चित् ।

On hearing the words “a sentence removes them through knowledge of the Self,” someone asks:

Text 25

3 · 25

मिथ्याज्ञानव्यतिरेकेणात्मानवबोधस्याभावात्किं वाक्येन निवर्तते । अज्ञानं हि नाम ज्ञानाभावस्तस्य चावस्तुस्वाभाव्यात्कुतः संसारकारणत्वं न ह्यसतः सज्जन्मेष्यते "कुतस्तु खलु सोम्यैवं स्यात् " इति "कथमसतः सज्जायेत " इति श्रुतेरिति । अत्रोच्यते ।

“Apart from false cognition, there is no separate ignorance of the Self; what, then, is removed by the sentence? Ignorance is merely the absence of knowledge, and, being unreal, how could it cause saṃsāra? What is unreal does not give birth to what is real. Scripture says, ‘How could this be so, dear one?’ and ‘How could the real arise from the unreal?’” The answer is:

Text 26

3 · 26

अज्ञात एव सर्वोऽर्थः प्राग्यतो बुद्धिजन्मनः ।

Before cognition arises, every object is unknown;

Verse 3.7

3.7

एकेनैव सता संश्च सन्नज्ञातो भवेत्ततः ॥ ७॥

yet it exists, and through the one real [Self] the unknown becomes manifest.

Text 28

3 · 28

सन्नज्ञातो भवेत्तत इत्युक्तमधस्ततेन श्लोकेन ।

The previous verse has said that what exists may yet be unknown.

Text 29

3 · 29

कोऽसौ सन्नज्ञात इत्यपेक्षायां तत्स्वरूपप्रतिपादनायाह ।

When one asks, “What is that existent yet unknown thing?”, its nature is stated:

Text 30

3 · 30

प्रमित्सायां य आभाति स्वयं मातृप्रमाणयोः ।

That which shines by itself when one seeks to know,

Verse 3.8

3.8

स्वमहिम्ना च यस्सिद्धः सोऽज्ञातार्थोऽवसीयताम् ॥ ८॥

and is established by its own glory, is to be understood as the reality not yet known.

Text 32

3 · 32

अत्र केचिदाहुः । यत्किंचिदिह वाक्यं लौकिकं वैदिकं वा तत्सर्वं संसर्गात्मकमेव वाक्यार्थं गमयति ।

Some say that every sentence, whether worldly or Vedic, conveys a meaning based on relation.

Text 33

3 · 33

अतस्तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्येभ्यः संसर्गात्मकमहं ब्रह्मेति विज्ञाय तावन्निदिध्यासीत यावदवाक्यार्थात्मकः प्रत्यगात्मविषयोऽवबोधोऽहं ब्रह्मेति समभिजायते ।

Therefore, from sentences such as “That thou art,” one first understands the relational meaning “I am Brahman,” then meditates until direct knowledge of the inward Self arises, beyond the meaning of the sentence.

Text 34

3 · 34

तस्मादेव विज्ञानात्कैवल्यमाप्नोतीति तन्निराकरणायेदमुच्यते ।

They say liberation is attained from that very knowledge. To refute this, it is said:

Text 35

3 · 35

सामानाधिकरण्यादेर्घटेतरखयोरिव ।

Just as when “pot” and “not-pot” are distinguished,

Verse 3.9

3.9

व्यावृत्तेः स्यादवाक्यार्थः साक्षान्नस्तत्त्वमर्थयोः ॥ ९॥

the meanings of “That” and “thou” are directly understood as beyond relation.

Text 37

3 · 37

कुतोऽवाक्यार्थोऽवसीयत इति चेत्तत्प्रतिपत्त्यर्थं विशेषणविशेष्ययोः सामर्थ्योक्तिः ।

If one asks how a meaning beyond relation is understood, the power of qualifier and qualified is explained:

Text 38

3 · 38

निर्दुःखित्वं त्वमर्थस्य तदर्थेन विशेषणात् ।

The absence of suffering in the meaning of “thou” qualifies the meaning of “That,”

Verse 3.10

3.10

प्रत्यक्ता च तदर्थस्य त्वंपदेनास्य संनिधेः ॥ १०॥

and the inwardness of the meaning of “That” is brought near by the word “thou.”

Text 40

3 · 40

उक्तं सामानाधिकरण्यं विशेषणविशेष्यभावश्च संक्षेपतोऽथ लक्ष्यलक्षणव्याख्यानायाह ।

Apposition and the relation between qualifier and qualified have been stated briefly; he now explains what is indicated and its indication:

Text 41

3 · 41

कूटस्थबोधप्रत्यक्त्वमनिमित्तं सदात्मनः ।

The changeless awareness and inwardness of the Self are always present;

Verse 3.11

3.11

बोद्धृताहन्तयोर्हेतुस्ताभ्यां तेनोपलक्ष्यते ॥ ११॥

they are the basis for the intellect’s knowing and its sense of “I”; through these the Self is indicated.

Text 43

3 · 43

बुद्धेः कूटस्थबोधप्रत्यक्त्वनिमित्ते बोद्धृताप्रत्यक्त्वे ये त्वसाधारणे तयोर्विशेषवचनम् ।

The intellect’s inwardness is due to changeless awareness; its distinctive features—knowing and the sense of “I”—are explained:

Text 44

3 · 44

बोद्धृता कर्तृता बुद्धेः कर्मता स्यादहन्तया ।

The intellect’s knowing is its agency; through the “I”-notion it becomes an object;

Verse 3.12

3.12

तयोरैक्यं यथा बुद्धौ पूर्वयोरेवमात्मनि ॥ १२॥

just as those two are joined in the intellect, so they are spoken of as one in the Self.

Text 46

3 · 46

यथा बुद्धौ पूर्वयोरेवमात्मनीत्यतिदेशेन बुद्धिसाधर्म्य विधानान्नानात्वप्रसक्तौ तदपवादार्थमाह ।

Since the comparison would imply that the Self shares the intellect’s properties and is therefore many, he rejects that implication:

Text 47

3 · 47

धर्मधर्मित्वभेदोऽस्याः सोऽपि नैवात्मनो यतः ।

Even that distinction between property and its bearer does not belong to the Self;

Verse 3.13

3.13

प्रत्यग्ज्योतिरतोऽभिन्नं भेदहेतोरसंभवात् ॥ १३॥

the inward light is therefore undivided, since no cause of division exists.

Text 49

3 · 49

भेदहेतसंभवं दर्शयन्नाह ।

He shows that there is no cause of division:

Text 50

3 · 50

न कस्यांचिदवस्थायां बोधप्रत्यक्त्वयोर्भिदा ।

In no state are awareness and inwardness separate;

Verse 3.14

3.14

व्यभिचारोऽथवा दृष्टो यथाहन्तद्विदोस्सदा ॥ १४॥

otherwise they would vary, as the sense of “I” and the sense of being a knower always do.

Text 52

3 · 52

यस्मादज्ञानोपादानाया एव बुद्धेर्भेदो नात्मनस्तस्मादेतत्सिद्धम् ।

Since only the intellect, produced by ignorance, is divided and not the Self, this is established:

Text 53

3 · 53

कूटस्थबोधतोऽद्वैतं साक्षात्त्वं प्रत्यगात्मनः ।

changeless awareness is non-dual, and the Self is directly inward;

Verse 3.15

3.15

कूटस्थबोधाद्बोद्ध्री धीः स्वतो हीयं विनश्वरी ॥ १५॥

the intellect that knows arises from changeless awareness, but by itself is perishable.

Text 55

3 · 55

अथाधुना प्रकृतस्यैव परिणामिनः कूटस्थस्य च लक्षणमुच्यते ।

The changing and the changeless, which are under discussion, are now defined.

Text 56

3 · 56

विशेषं कंचिदाश्रित्य यत्स्वरूपं प्रतीयते ।

That whose nature is understood by depending on some particular feature

Verse 3.16

3.16

प्रत्यभिज्ञाप्रमाणेन परिणामी स देहवत् ॥ १६॥

is changing, like the body, as established by recognition.

Text 58

3 · 58

सामान्याच्च विशेषाच्च स्वमहिम्नैव यो भवेत् ।

That which exists by its own nature, apart from both general and particular features,

Verse 3.17

3.17

व्युथ्यायाप्यविकारी स्यात्कुम्भाकाशादिवत्तु सः ॥ १७॥

is changeless even when distinctions fall away, like space within a pot.

Text 60

3 · 60

आत्मनो बुद्धेश्च बोधप्रत्यगात्मत्वमभिहितं तयोरसाधारणलक्षणाभिधानार्थमाह ।

The inward awareness of both the Self and the intellect has been stated; their distinctive marks are now given:

Text 61

3 · 61

बुद्धेर्यत्प्रत्यगात्मत्वं तत्स्याद्देहाद्युपाश्रयात् ।

the intellect’s inwardness depends on the body and the rest;

Verse 3.18

3.18

आत्मनस्तु स्वरूपं तन्नभसः सुषिता यथा ॥ १८॥

but the Self’s nature is its own, like the subtle expanse of space.

Text 63

3 · 63

बोद्धृत्वं तद्वदेवास्याः प्रत्ययोत्पत्तिहेतुतः ।

The intellect is a knower because it gives rise to cognitions;

Verse 3.19

3.19

आत्मनस्तु स्वरूपं तत्तिष्ठन्तीव महीभृतः ॥ १९॥

the Self’s own nature remains as it is, like mountains that stand firm.

Text 65

3 · 65

तयोः कूटस्थपरिणामिनोरात्मानवबोध एव संबन्धहेतुर्न पुनर्वास्तवः कश्चिदपि संबन्ध उपपद्यत इत्याह ।

Ignorance alone is the supposed link between the changeless Self and the changing intellect; no real connection between them is possible.

Text 66

3 · 66

सम्यक्संशयमिथ्यात्वैर्धीरेवेयं विभज्यते ।

The intellect alone is divided into right knowledge, doubt, and error;

Verse 3.20

3.20

हानोपादानतामीषां मोहादध्यस्यते दृशौ ॥ २०॥

through delusion, the witness imagines that it accepts and rejects those states.

Text 68

3 · 68

कुतः कूटस्थात्मसिद्धिरिति चेद्यतः ।

If one asks how the changeless Self is established, it is because:

Text 69

3 · 69

न हानं हानमात्रेण नोदयोऽपीयता यतः ।

It is not a mere cessation, nor is it an arising;

Verse 3.21

3.21

तत्सिद्धिः स्यात्तु तद्धीने हानादानविधर्मके ॥ २१॥

it is established apart from those, distinct from both acceptance and rejection.

Text 71

3 · 71

एवम् ।

Thus:

Text 72

3 · 72

आगमापायिहेतुभ्यां धूत्वा सर्वाननात्मनः ।

Having removed every not-Self through causes that arise and pass away,

Verse 3.22

3.22

ततस्तत्त्वमसीत्येतद्धन्त्यस्मदि निजं तमः ॥ २२॥

the sentence “That thou art” destroys its own darkness within us.

Text 74

3 · 74

इत्यादि पुनःपुनरुच्यते ग्रन्थलाघवाद्बुद्धिलाघवं प्रयोजकमिति । तत्र यद्यपि तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यादुपादित्सिताद्वितीयात्मार्थवत्पारोक्ष्यसद्वितीयार्थः प्रतीयते ।

This point is repeated again and again because the brevity of the treatise is meant to aid understanding. Although “That thou art” may appear to convey both the intended non-dual Self and a remote, dual meaning,

Text 75

3 · 75

तथापि तु नैवासावर्थः श्रुत्या तात्पर्येण प्रतिपिपादयिषितः प्रागपेतस्य प्रतीतत्वादितीममर्थमाह ।

that remote meaning is not what revelation intends, since it has already been understood before the sentence. He says:

Text 76

3 · 76

तदित्येतत्पदं लोके बह्वर्थप्रतिपादकम् ।

In ordinary usage the word “That” conveys many meanings,

Verse 3.23

3.23

अपरित्यज्य पारोक्ष्यमभिधानोत्थमेव तत् ॥ २३॥

yet it does not abandon the remoteness produced by the word;

Text 78

3 · 78

त्वमित्यपि पदं तद्वत्साक्षान्मात्रार्थवाचि तु ।

the word “thou,” too, directly expresses its immediate meaning,

Verse 3.24

3.24

संसारितामसंत्यज्य सापि स्यादभिधानजा ॥ २४॥

and if it does not abandon saṃsāric identity, that meaning too is produced by the word.

Text 80

3 · 80

विरुद्धोद्देशनत्वाच्चपारोक्ष्यदुःखित्वयोरविवक्षितत्वमित्याह ।

Since remoteness and suffering are contradictory purposes, they are not intended here. He says:

Text 81

3 · 81

उद्दिश्यमानं वाक्यस्थं नोद्देशनगुणात्वितम् ।

A meaning stated in a sentence is not thereby endowed with the force of direct denotation.

Verse 3.25

3.25

आकाङ्क्षितपदार्थेन संसर्गं प्रतिपद्यते ॥ २५॥

It enters into relation with the meaning required by the sentence.

Text 83

3 · 83

यत एतदेवमतोऽनुपादित्सितयोरपि तत्त्वमर्थयोर्विशेषणविशेष्यभावो भेदसंसर्गरहितवाक्यार्थलक्षणायैवेत्युपसंहारः ।

Since this is so, even though the meanings of “that” and “thou” are not directly asserted in their contradictory forms, their relation as qualifier and qualified serves only to reveal the sentence meaning, which is free of a relation between separate things. This is the conclusion.

Text 84

3 · 84

तदो विशेषणार्थत्वं विशेष्यत्वं त्वमस्तथा ।

“That” has the force of a qualifier; “thou” has the force of what is qualified.

Verse 3.26

3.26

लक्ष्यलक्षणसंबन्धस्तयोः स्यात्प्रत्यगात्मना ॥ २६॥

Their relation, as indicated and indicator, is through the innermost Self.

Text 86

3 · 86

कथं पुनरविवक्षितविरुद्धनिरस्यमानस्य लक्षणार्थत्वम् ।

How can the meaning of an indicator be something whose contradictory, unintended sense is being negated?

Text 87

3 · 87

लक्षणं सर्पवद्रज्ज्वाः प्रतीचः स्यादहं तथा ।

The inner Self is like the rope; the “I” that is taken to be the Self is like the snake.

Verse 3.27

3.27

तद्बाधेनैव वाक्यार्थं वेत्ति सोऽपि तदाश्रयात् ॥ २७॥

Through the sublation of that mistaken appearance, one understands the sentence meaning; even the sublation depends on the underlying reality.

Text 89

3 · 89

इयं चावाक्यार्थप्रतिपत्तिरन्वयव्यतिरेकाभिज्ञस्यैव ।

This understanding of the sentence meaning belongs to one who knows agreement and difference through presence and absence.

Text 90

3 · 90

यस्मात् ।

For this reason:

Text 91

3 · 91

यावद्यावन्निरस्यायं देहादीन्प्रत्यगञ्चति ।

As one keeps negating the body and the rest, turning toward the innermost Self,

Verse 3.28

3.28

तावत्तावत्तदर्थोऽपि त्वमर्थं प्रविविक्षति ॥ २८॥

so, in each step, the meaning of “that” enters into the meaning of “thou.”

Text 93

3 · 93

कस्मात्पुनः कारणाद्देहाद्यनात्मत्वप्रतिपत्तावेवात्मा तदर्थमात्मत्वेनाभिलिङ्गते न विपर्यय इति । उच्यते ।

Why, when one recognizes the body and the rest as not-Self, is the Self marked out as the meaning of “that” by its very Selfhood, and not the reverse? The answer is:

Text 94

3 · 94

प्रत्यगात्मानवबोधस्यनात्मस्वाभाव्यात्तदभिनिर्वृत्तश्चायं बुद्ध्यादिदेहान्तस्तस्मिन्नात्मत्वमविद्याकृतमेवात्मत्वमिवानात्मत्वमपि साविद्यस्यैव । यतो निरविद्यो विद्वानवाक्यार्थरूप एव केवलोऽवशिष्यते तस्मादुच्यते ।

Because the innermost Self is not recognized, the mind and everything down to the body arise as not-Self; Selfhood attributed to them is created by ignorance. For one still under ignorance, even their apparent not-Selfhood is likewise bound up with ignorance. Once ignorance is gone, the knower remains alone as the very meaning of the sentence, beyond direct designation. Therefore it is said:

Text 95

3 · 95

देहादिव्यवधानत्वात्तदर्थं स्वयमप्यतः ।

Because the body and the rest stand between the knower and that meaning, even the Self

Verse 3.29

3.29

पारोक्ष्येणैव जानाति साक्षात्त्वं तदनात्मनः ॥ २९॥

knows it only indirectly; the meaning of “thou,” which is not-Self, is known directly.

Text 97

3 · 97

यथोक्तार्थप्रतिपत्तिसौकर्याय दृष्टान्तोपादानाम् ।

An example is offered to make the stated point easier to understand.

Text 98

3 · 98

प्रत्यगुद्भुतपित्तस्य यथा बाह्यार्थपीतता ।

Just as a person whose bile has risen inwardly sees an external white object as yellow,

Verse 3.30

3.30

चैतन्यं प्रत्यगात्मीयं बहिर्वद्दृश्यते तथा ॥ ३०॥

so the consciousness belonging to the innermost Self appears to be outside.

Text 100

3 · 100

यस्मादेवमतो विशुद्धमवसीयताम् ।

Since this is so, understand the point clearly:

Text 101

3 · 101

पदान्युद्धृत्य वाक्येभ्यो ह्यन्वयव्यतिरेकतः ।

By drawing out the words from the sentences, through agreement and difference,

Verse 3.31

3.31

पदार्थांल्लोकतो बुद्ध्वा वेत्ति वाक्यार्थमञ्जसा ॥ ३१॥

and learning their meanings from common usage, one readily understands the meaning of the sentence.

Text 103

3 · 103

कुतः पुनः सामान्यमात्रवृत्तेः पदस्य वाक्यार्थप्रतिपत्तिहेतुत्वमिति । बाढम् ।

How can a word, which applies only to a general meaning, serve as a means of understanding the meaning of a sentence? The reply is: Certainly it can.

Text 104

3 · 104

सामान्यं हि पदं ब्रूते विशेषो वाक्यकर्तृकः ।

A word expresses a general meaning; the sentence-maker supplies the particular.

Verse 3.32

3.32

श्रुत्यादिप्रतिबद्धं सद्विशेषार्थं भवेत्पदम् ॥ ३२॥

When constrained by scripture and the other sources of context, a word conveys a particular meaning.

Text 106

3 · 106

अन्वयव्यतिरेकपुरस्सरं वाक्यमेव सामानाधिकरण्यादिनाविद्या पटलप्रध्वंसद्वारेण मुमुक्षुं स्वाराज्येऽभिषेचयति न त्वन्वयव्यतिरेकमात्रसाध्योऽयमर्थ इत्याह ।

It is the sentence itself, using co-reference and the like, preceded by reasoning through agreement and difference, that destroys the veil of ignorance and enthrones the seeker in his own sovereignty. This meaning is not established by agreement and difference alone, as the following verse says:

Text 107

3 · 107

बुद्ध्यादीनामनात्मत्वं लिङ्गादपि च सिध्यति ।

The not-Selfhood of the mind and the rest can also be established by inference.

Verse 3.33

3.33

निवृत्तिस्तावता नेतीत्यतो वाक्यं समाश्रयेत् ॥ ३३॥

Yet that alone does not bring ignorance to an end; therefore, through the teaching “not this,” one should take refuge in the sentence of scripture.

Text 109

3 · 109

न केवलमनुमानमात्रशरणोऽभिलषितमर्थं न प्राप्नोतीत्यनर्थं चाप्नोतीत्याह ।

He says that one who relies on inference alone not only fails to attain the desired meaning but also falls into error:

Text 110

3 · 110

अनादृत्य श्रुतिं मोहादतो बौद्धास्तमस्विनः ।

Through delusion, the Buddhists who disregard scripture, shrouded in darkness,

Verse 3.34

3.34

आपेदिरे निरात्मत्वमनुमानैकचक्षुषः ॥ ३४॥

with inference as their only eye, have arrived at the doctrine that there is no Self.

Text 112

3 · 112

न चानादरे कारणमस्ति । यस्मात्सर्वत्रैवानादरनिमित्तं प्रमाणस्य प्रमाणान्तरप्रतिपन्नप्रतिपादनं वा विपरीतप्रतिपादनं वा संशयितप्रतिपादनं वा न वा प्रतिपादनमिति -- न चैतेषामन्यतमदपि कारणमस्ति ।

There is no reason to disregard scripture. A means of knowledge may be set aside if it merely repeats what another means has established, teaches the opposite, leaves its result doubtful, or yields no result. None of these is the case here.

Text 113

3 · 113

यत आह ।

For scripture says:

Text 114

3 · 114

मानान्तरानवष्टब्धं निर्दुःख्यात्मानमञ्जसा ।

“The scripture that clearly teaches the sorrowless Self, untouched by any other means of knowledge—

Verse 3.35

3.35

बोधयन्ती श्रुतिः केन न प्रमाणमितीर्यते ॥ ३५॥

by what grounds could it be declared no means of knowledge?”

Text 116

3 · 116

न च संशयितव्यमवगमयति । यतः ।

Nor does it leave the matter uncertain, because:

Text 117

3 · 117

सर्वसंशयहेतौ हि निरस्ते कथमात्मनि ।

When every cause of doubt has been removed, how could doubt arise about the Self

Verse 3.36

3.36

जायेत संशयो वाक्यादनुमानेन युष्मदि ॥ ३६॥

from the sentence, when inference concerns what is other than oneself?”

Text 119

3 · 119

अपि च ।

Moreover:

Text 120

3 · 120

यत्र स्यात्संशयो नासौ ज्ञेय आत्मेति पण्डितैः ।

“That in which doubt could arise is not held by the wise to be the knowable Self.”

Verse 3.37

3.37

न यतः संशयप्राप्तिरात्मनोऽवगतित्वतः ॥ ३७॥

“—not, because the Self is ever directly known.”

Text 122

3 · 122

अनवबोधकत्वं तु दूरोत्सारितमेव । यत आह ।

That scripture is unable to reveal the Self has already been ruled out completely. For it says:

Text 123

3 · 123

बोधेऽप्यनुभवो यस्य न कथंचन जायते ।

“For one who has no experience of awareness at all,

Verse 3.38

3.38

तं कथं बोधयेच्छास्त्रं लोष्टं नरसमाकृतिम् ॥ ३८॥

how could scripture awaken him? He would be like a stone shaped as a human being.”

Text 125

3 · 125

अन्वयव्यतिरेकपुरस्सरं वाक्यमेवावाक्यार्थरूपमात्मानं प्रतिपादयतीत्यस्य पक्षस्य द्रढिम्न श्रुत्युदाहरणमुपन्यस्यति ।

To strengthen the view that the sentence of scripture, preceded by reasoning through agreement and difference, teaches the Self beyond ordinary sentence meaning, he gives a scriptural example:

Text 126

3 · 126

जिघ्राणीममहं गन्धमिति यो वेत्त्यविक्रियः ।

“I smell this fragrance”—the changeless knower of that experience

Verse 3.39

3.39

स आत्मा तत्परं ज्योतिः शिरसीदं वचः श्रुतेः ॥ ३९॥

is the Self, the supreme light. This is the declaration of scripture.”

Text 128

3 · 128

यथा "तत्सत्यं स आत्मा तत्त्वमसि " इत्यस्य शेषत्वेनान्वयव्यतिरेकश्रुतिर्यथा "य एषोऽक्षिणिपुरुषो दृश्यते " इत्याद्या "अथ यो वेदेदं जिघ्राणि " इत्यन्ता तथा "अहं ब्रह्मास्मि " इत्यस्य शेषः ।

Just as the teaching “That is the real; that is the Self; you are that” is clarified by the passages on agreement and difference beginning “the person seen in the eye” and ending “now, the one who knows ‘I smell this’,” so too those passages explain “I am Brahman.”

Text 129

3 · 129

अहमः प्रत्यगात्मार्थो निरस्ताशेषयुष्मदः ।

“The meaning of ‘I’ is the innermost Self, with every meaning of ‘you’ negated.

Verse 3.40

3.40

बम्भणीति श्रुतिर्न्याय्या योऽयमित्यादिनाऽसकृत् ॥ ४०॥

Scripture repeatedly teaches this with the words ‘this one’ and the like; this is the fitting interpretation.”

Text 131

3 · 131

कथं पुनरयमर्थोऽवसीयते अहंव्याजेनात्रात्मार्थो बुबोधयिषित इति । यतः ।

How is it established that the intended meaning of the word “I” here is the Self? Because:

Text 132

3 · 132

एष आत्मा स्वयंज्योती रविसोमाग्निवाक्षु सः ।

“This Self is self-luminous; it is the one present in the sun, moon, fire and speech.

Verse 3.41

3.41

इतेष्वस्तं दृगेवास्ते भासयंचित्तचेष्टितम् ॥ ४१॥

When these lights have set, it alone remains as the seer, illuminating the activity of the mind.”

Text 134

3 · 134

निर्णेनेक्ति च पृष्टो मुनिः ।

The sage explains the point in response to a question:

Text 135

3 · 135

आत्मनैवेत्युपश्रुत्य कोऽयमात्मेत्युदीरिते ।

“When the reply ‘by the Self alone’ was heard, the question was asked, ‘What is this Self?’

Verse 3.42

3.42

बुद्धेः परं स्वतोमुक्तमात्मानं मुनिरभ्यधात् ॥ ४२॥

The sage described the Self as beyond the intellect and free in its own nature.”

Text 137

3 · 137

यस्माच्चात्मात्राहंव्याजेन प्रत्यङ्मात्रो जिग्राहयिषितस्तस्मादहंवृत्तिः स्वरूपस्य विलयेनैव वाक्यार्थावगमाय कारणत्वं प्रतिपद्यत इतीममर्थमाह ।

Because the Self meant here by “I” is intended to be understood as the innermost witness alone, the “I”-thought serves to reveal the sentence meaning only by dissolving into its own true nature. This is expressed in the following verse:

Text 138

3 · 138

अहंवृत्त्यैव तद्ब्रह्म यस्मादेषोऽवगच्छति ।

“Through the ‘I’-thought alone one understands that Brahman;

Verse 3.43

3.43

तत्स्वरूपलयेनातः कारणं स्यादहंकृतिः ॥ ४३॥

therefore the ego becomes the means by dissolving into its own nature.”

Text 140

3 · 140

अत एव च यः प्रतिज्ञातोऽर्थो "नाहंग्राह्ये न तद्धीने "इत्यादिः स युक्तिभिरुपपादित इति कृत्वोपसंह्रियते ।

Thus the point stated earlier—that the Self is not grasped as an object, nor dependent on an object—is established by reasoning. The author concludes:

Text 141

3 · 141

गृहीताहंपदार्थश्चेत्कस्माज्ज्ञो न प्रपद्यते ।

“If the meaning of ‘I’ has been grasped, why does the knower not attain it?

Verse 3.44

3.44

प्रत्यक्षादिविरोधाच्चेत्प्रतीच्युक्तिर्न युष्मदि ॥ ४४॥

If you say it conflicts with perception and the other means of knowledge, the inward teaching concerns no ‘you’ at all.”

Text 143

3 · 143

पूर्वस्यैव श्लोकार्थस्य विस्पष्टार्थमाह ।

He clarifies the meaning of the preceding verse:

Text 144

3 · 144

पराञ्च्येव तु सर्वाणि प्रत्यक्षादीनि नात्मनि ।

“Perception and the other means of knowledge are directed outward; they do not reach the Self.

Verse 3.45

3.45

प्रतीच्येव प्रवृत्तं तत्सदसीति वचोऽञ्जसा ॥ ४५॥

The statement ‘You are that’ plainly concerns what is inward.”

Text 146

3 · 146

तस्मात्प्रमातृप्रमाणप्रमेयेभ्यो हीयमानोपादीयमानेभ्योऽन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां मुञ्जेषीकावदशेषबुद्धिविक्रियासाक्षितयात्मानं निष्कृष्य तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्येभ्योऽपूर्वादिलक्षणमात्मानं विजानीयात् । तदेतदाह ।

Therefore, using agreement and difference, one should separate the Self from the knower, means of knowledge and objects of knowledge that are taken up and discarded, as one draws the pith from a reed. Then, from sentences such as “you are that,” one should know the Self as beyond anything previously established. This is stated here:

Text 147

3 · 147

अहं दुःखी सुखी चेति येनायं प्रत्ययोऽध्रुवः ।

“The one by whom this fleeting thought ‘I am unhappy’ or ‘I am happy’

Verse 3.46

3.46

अवगत्यन्त आभाति स म आत्मेति वाक्यधीः ॥ ४६॥

is known, and in whom it comes to awareness, is my Self”—such is the understanding born of the sentence.”

Text 149

3 · 149

प्रमाणान्तरानवष्टब्धं निरस्ताशेषकार्यकारणात्मकद्वैतप्रपञ्चं सत्यज्ञानानन्दलक्षणमात्मानं तत्त्वमस्यहंब्रह्मास्मीत्यादिवाक्यं संशयितमिथ्याज्ञानाज्ञानप्रध्वंसमुखेन साक्षादपरोक्षात्करतलन्यस्तामलकवत्प्रतिपादयत्येवेत्यसकृदभिहितम् । तत्र केचिदाहुः -- तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यैर्यथावस्थितवस्तुयाथात्म्यान्वाख्याननिष्ठैर्नयथोक्तोऽर्थः प्रतिपत्तुं शक्यतेऽभिधाश्रुतित्वात्तेषाम् ।

It has been said repeatedly that a sentence such as “you are that” or “I am Brahman” does directly reveal the Self—whose nature is reality, awareness and bliss, untouched by any other means of knowledge and free of the entire duality of effects and causes—by removing doubt, false knowledge and ignorance, like a fruit placed in one’s palm. Some object: “Sentences such as ‘you are that,’ which merely describe the nature of an existing thing as it is, cannot reveal the meaning you claim, because they are heard in their literal, declarative sense.”

Text 150

3 · 150

न हि लोकेऽभिधाश्रुतेः प्रमाणान्तरनिरपेक्षायानद्यास्तीरे फलानि सन्तीत्यादिकायाः प्रामाण्यमभ्युपगतम् ।

For in ordinary usage, the authority of a literal statement that needs no other means of knowledge—such as ‘fruit is on the far bank of the river’—is not accepted.

Text 151

3 · 151

अतो नियोगमुखेनैवाभिधाश्रुतेः प्रामाण्यं युक्तं प्रमाणान्तरनिरपेक्षत्वान्नियोगस्य । अस्य परिहारार्थमशेषप्रत्यक्षादिप्रमेयत्वनिराकरणद्वारेणातीन्द्रियार्थविषयत्वादभिधाश्रुतेः प्रामाण्यं सुप्तपुरुषप्रबोधकवाक्यस्येव वक्तव्यमित्ययमारम्भः ।

They therefore claim that a literal scriptural statement is authoritative only as an injunction, since an injunction does not depend on another means of knowledge. To answer this, the author begins: a literal scriptural statement is authoritative about a supersensible matter, because that matter is not an object of perception and the other means of knowledge—just as a sentence that awakens a sleeping person is authoritative.

Text 152

3 · 152

नित्यावगतिरूपत्वादन्यमानानपेक्षणात् ।

“Because it is the very nature of constant awareness, it does not depend on another means of knowledge;

Verse 3.47

3.47

शब्दादिगुणहीनत्वात्संशयानवतारतः ॥ ४७॥

and because it is free of qualities such as sound, doubt cannot arise about it.”

Text 154

3 · 154

तृष्णानिष्ठीवनैर्नात्मा प्रत्यक्षाद्यः प्रमीयते ।

“When craving has been cast away, the Self is not known through perception and the other means of knowledge;

Verse 3.48

3.48

प्रत्यगात्मत्वहेतोश्च स्वार्थत्वादप्रमेयतः ॥ ४८॥

because it is the innermost Self, it is not an object of knowledge but the very ground of knowing.”

Text 156

3 · 156

श्रुतिरपीममर्थं निर्वदति ।

Scripture states this same point:

Text 157

3 · 157

दिद्दक्षितपरिछिन्नपराग्रूपादिसंश्रयात् ।

“Because sight is directed toward limited outward forms, colors and the like,

Verse 3.49

3.49

विपरीतमतो दृष्ट्या स्वतोबुद्धं न पश्यति ॥ ४९॥

one does not see what is the opposite of them—the Self, already aware by itself.”

Text 159

3 · 159

न्यायसिद्धमतो वक्ति दृष्टेर्द्रष्टारमात्मनः ।

For this reason scripture states what reason establishes: the Self is the seer of seeing.

Verse 3.50

3.50

न पश्येत्प्रत्यगात्मानं प्रमाणं श्रुतिरादरात् ॥ ५०॥

“One should not see the innermost Self as an object; scripture itself is the means of knowledge, and it declares this emphatically.”

Text 161

3 · 161

अनुमानाविषयत्वेऽन्यदपि कारणमुच्यते ।

Another reason is given for why the Self is not an object of inference:

Text 162

3 · 162

प्रत्यक्षस्य पराक्त्वान्न संबन्धग्रहणं यतः ।

“Perception is directed outward, and therefore cannot grasp the relation [between knower and known];

Verse 3.51

3.51

आत्मनोऽतोऽनुमित्यास्यानुभवो न कथंचन ॥ ५१॥

so inference, too, can never yield direct experience of the Self.”

Text 164

3 · 164

एवमयं प्रमातृप्रमाणप्रमेयव्यवहारः सर्व एव पराचीनविषय एव न प्रतीचिनमात्मानमवगाहयितुमलम् ।

Thus the entire transaction of knower, means of knowledge and known objects concerns things outward; it cannot reach the inward Self.

Text 165

3 · 165

एवं च सत्यनेनैव यथोक्तोऽर्थोऽवसातुं शक्यत इत्याह ।

Since this is so, the point already explained can be established by this very reasoning:

Text 166

3 · 166

प्रमाणव्यवहारोऽयं सर्व एव पराग्यतः ।

“Every transaction of knowledge is directed outward;

Verse 3.52

3.52

सुविचार्याप्यतोऽनेन युष्मद्येव दिदृक्षते ॥ ५२॥

even after careful examination, by this means one seeks only what is other than oneself.”

Text 168

3 · 168

यस्माल्लौकिकप्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणानधिगम्योऽहंब्रह्मास्मीति वाक्यार्थस्तस्मात् ।

Since the sentence meaning “I am Brahman” cannot be reached by ordinary perception and the other means of knowledge:

Text 169

3 · 169

अन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां निरस्याप्राणतो यतेः ।

“Having negated what does not breathe and the rest through agreement and difference, the sage turns inward and asks, ‘Who am I?’

Verse 3.53

3.53

वीक्षापन्नस्य कोऽस्मीति तदसीति श्रुतिर्जगौ ॥ ५३॥

To that one who has become discerning, scripture declares: ‘You are that.’”

Text 171

3 · 171

सोऽयमन्वयव्यतिरेकन्याय एतावानेव यदवसानो वाक्यार्थस्तदभिज्ञस्याहंब्रह्मास्मीत्याविर्भवति ।

This reasoning through agreement and difference culminates in the sentence meaning becoming clear to one who understands it: “I am Brahman.”

Text 172

3 · 172
