द्रष्टृदृश्यविभागेनागमापायिसाक्षिविभागेन च श्रुत्यभ्युपगमतः संक्षिप्योच्यते ।

The distinction between seer and seen, and between the witness and what arises and subsides, is stated here in a concise form accepted by scripture:

Text 173

3 · 173

दृश्यत्वाद्घटवद्देहो देहवच्चेन्द्रियाण्यपि ।

“Because it is seen, the body is like a pot; the senses, too, are like the body.

Verse 3.54

3.54

मनश्चेन्द्रियवज्ज्ञेयं मनोवन्निश्चयादिमत् ॥ ५४॥

The mind is known like the senses, and its judgments and other states are known like the mind.”

Text 175

3 · 175

तथा सकलकार्यकारणागमापायि विभागसाक्षित्वेनापि ।

The same point follows from the Self’s witnessing all effects and causes as they arise and disappear:

Text 176

3 · 176

प्रागसद्याति पश्चात्सत्सच्च यायादसत्तथा ।

“What was absent before comes into being later; what exists may later become absent.

Verse 3.55

3.55

अनात्माभिजनं तत्स्याद्विपरीतः स्वयं दृशिः ॥ ५५॥

What has that nature belongs to the not-Self; the opposite is the seeing Self itself.”

Text 178

3 · 178

तत्र घटादीनां दृश्यानामनात्मत्वं द्रष्ट्रात्मपूर्वकं प्रत्यक्षेणैव प्रमाणेनोपलभ्यानात्मनश्चासाधारणान् धर्मानवधार्य तैर्दृश्यत्वागमापायादिभिर्धर्मैः शरीरेन्द्रियमनोनिश्चयादिवृत्तीरनात्मतया व्युदस्याहंवृत्तिमतोऽपि दृश्यत्वाविशेषाद्द्रष्टृपूर्वकत्वमवसीयते । तदेतदाह ।

The not-Selfhood of visible things such as pots is directly evident, with the seer as its ground. Their distinctive properties are recognized; then, by their being seen and by their arising and passing away, the body, senses, mind and its judgments are set aside as not-Self. Since the “I”-thought is also seen, it too is known to depend on a seer. This is expressed as follows:

Text 179

3 · 179

घटादयो यथा लिङ्गं स्युः परंपरयाहमः ।

“Just as pots and the like are signs of the ‘I’ by a chain of dependence,

Verse 3.56

3.56

दृश्यत्वादहमप्येवं लिङ्गं स्याद्द्रष्टुरात्मनः ॥ ५६॥

so the ‘I’ itself, because it is seen, is a sign of the witnessing Self.”

Text 181

3 · 181

ननु द्रष्टृदर्शनदृश्यानां जाग्रत्स्वप्नसुषुप्तेष्वागमापायदर्शनाद्यसाक्षिकौ तेषामागमापायौ स आगमापायविभागरहित आत्मा यथा यन्निबन्धनौ जगतः प्रकाशाप्रकाशौ स प्रकाशाप्रकाशविभागरहितः सूर्य इति ।

One might object: the seer, seeing and seen are all observed to arise and disappear in waking, dream and deep sleep. Their arising and disappearance are known by a witness; that witness is the Self, free of arising and disappearance. Just as the sun, on which the world’s light and darkness depend, is free of that division—

Text 182

3 · 182

यदा चैवं तदा वाक्यावगम्यस्यार्थस्यानुदितानस्तमितविज्ञानमात्रस्वभावस्यानुमानेनैव प्रतिपन्नत्वात्पुनरपि वाक्यस्य निर्विषयत्वप्रसङ्गः । नैष दोषः ।

if so, the meaning taught by the sentence, whose nature is awareness without beginning or end, has already been established by inference. The scriptural sentence would then have no subject matter. This is no fault:

Text 183

3 · 183

लिङ्गव्यवधानेन तत्प्रतिपत्तेः । ननु साक्षादपरोक्षादात्मस्वभावेनानात्मनो हानोपादानयोः संबन्धग्रहणात्कमतिशयं वाक्यं कुर्यात् । मैवं वोचः ।

because that conclusion is reached only through an intervening sign. But if the Self is already directly and immediately experienced as one’s own nature, and one can distinguish what is not-Self in order to give it up or take it up, what further work could the sentence do? Do not say this.

Text 184

3 · 184

लिङ्गाधीनत्वात्तत्प्रतिपत्तेः ।

The conclusion depends on an inferential sign.

Text 185

3 · 185

न हि लिङ्गव्यवधानेनात्मप्रतिपत्तिः साक्षात्प्रतिपत्तिर्भवति "यमेवैष वृणुते तेन लभ्यः " इति श्रुतेः । अत आह ।

Knowing the Self through an intervening sign is not direct knowledge. Scripture says, “The Self is attained by the one whom it chooses.” Therefore:

Text 186

3 · 186

लिङ्गमस्तित्वनिष्ठत्वान्न स्याद्वाक्यार्थबोधकम् ।

“A sign that establishes only existence cannot reveal the meaning of the sentence;

Verse 3.57

3.57

सदसद्व्युत्थितात्माऽयमतो वाक्यात्प्रतीयते ॥ ५७॥

the Self, beyond the alternatives ‘is’ and ‘is not,’ is understood from the sentence.”

Text 188

3 · 188

ननु यदि व्यावृत्तसदसद्विकल्पजालं वस्त्वभीष्टं वाक्याद्भवतस्तथापि तूत्सार्यते वाक्यविषया तृष्णा -- यस्मादन्तरेणापि वाक्यश्रवणं निरस्ताशेषविकल्पमागोपालाविपालपण्डितं सुषुप्ते वस्तु सिद्धमतो नार्थो वाक्यश्रवणेन । नैतदेवम् ।

Another objection: “If the reality you want is the thing beyond all alternatives of existence and nonexistence, the desire for a scriptural sentence can still be set aside. Even without hearing one, that reality is present in deep sleep, where every distinction is absent, from the cowherd to the learned scholar. So hearing scripture is unnecessary.” This is not so.

Text 189

3 · 189

किं कारणम् । सर्वानर्थबीजस्यात्मानवबोधस्य सुषुप्ते संभवात् । यदि हि सुषुप्तेऽज्ञानं नाभविष्यदन्तरेणापि वेदान्तवाक्यश्रवणमनन निदिध्यासनान्यहं ब्रह्मास्मीत्यध्यवसायात्सर्वप्राणभूतामपि स्वरसत एव सुषुप्तप्रतिपत्तेः सकलसंसारोच्छित्तिप्रसङ्गः । न च कैवल्यात्पुनरुत्थानं न्याय्यमनिर्मोक्षप्रसङ्गात् । न चान्य एव सुषुप्तोऽन्य एवोत्थित इति शक्यं वक्तुं नाद्राक्षमहं सुषुप्तेऽन्यत् किंचिदपीत्युत्तितस्य प्रत्यभिज्ञादर्शनात् । तस्मादवश्यं सुषुप्तेऽज्ञानमभ्युपगन्तव्यम् । ननु यदि तत्राज्ञानमभविष्यद्रागद्वेषघटाज्ञानादिवत्प्रत्यक्षमभविष्यद्यथेह लोके घटं न जानामीत्यज्ञानमव्यवहितं प्रत्यक्षम् । अत्रोच्यते । न । अभिव्यञ्जकाभावात् । कथमभिव्यञ्जकाभाव इति चेच्छृणु ।

Why not? Because ignorance, the seed of every misfortune, persists in deep sleep. If ignorance were absent there, then even without hearing, reflecting on and contemplating the Vedānta sentences, everyone—from the smallest living creature onward—would naturally wake from sleep convinced “I am Brahman”; the whole of worldly bondage would thereby end. Nor would it make sense to rise again after liberation, for that would make liberation impossible. One also cannot say that the sleeper and the one who wakes are different people: the person who wakes recognizes, “I saw nothing else while asleep.” Ignorance must therefore be admitted to persist in deep sleep. An objection follows: if ignorance were present there, it should be directly perceived, as attachment, aversion or ignorance of a pot is perceived here when one says, “I do not know the pot.” The answer is no, because there is no revealer of it. If asked how there can be no revealer, listen:

Text 190

3 · 190

बाह्यां वृत्तिमनुत्पाद्य व्यक्तिः स्यान्नाहमो यथा ।

“Just as the ‘I’-thought does not appear without producing an outward mental activity,

Verse 3.58

3.58

नर्तेऽन्तःकरणं तद्वद्ध्वान्तस्य व्यक्तिराञ्जसी ॥ ५८॥

so darkness is not clearly revealed without the inner instrument, the mind.”

Text 192

3 · 192

कश्चिदतिक्रान्तं प्रतिस्मृत्य "दृश्यत्वादहमप्येवं लिङ्गं स्याद्द्रष्टुरात्मनः " इति निर्युक्तिकमभिहितमित्याह । किं कारणम् ।

Someone recalls the earlier argument and says that the claim ‘because it is seen, the “I” too is a sign of the witnessing Self’ was made without proof. Why?

Text 193

3 · 193

अहंतज्ज्ञात्रोर्विवेकाप्रसिद्धेः । यथेह घटदेवदत्तयोर्ग्राह्यग्राहकत्वेन स्फुटतरो विभागः प्रसिद्धो लोके न तथेहाहंकारतज्ज्ञात्रोर्विभागोऽस्तीति तस्मादसाध्वेतदभिहितमिति । अत्रोच्यते ।

“Because no distinction between the ‘I’ and the knower is established. In ordinary life the difference between a pot and Devadatta, as object and perceiver, is quite clear; there is no such evident difference between the ego and the one who knows it. So that claim is unsound.” The reply is:

Text 194

3 · 194

दाह्यदाहकतैकत्र यथा स्याद्वह्निदारुणोः ।

“Just as fire and wood can stand in the relation of burner and burned within one place,

Verse 3.59

3.59

ज्ञेयज्ञातृकतैवं स्यादहंज्ञात्रोः परस्परम् ॥ ५९॥

so the ‘I’-thought and the knower can stand in the relation of known and knower.”

Text 196

3 · 196

एवं तावदविद्योत्थस्यान्तःकरणस्य बाह्यविषयनिमित्तरूपावच्छेदायाहंवृत्तिर्व्याप्रियते । तयावच्छिन्नं सत्कूटस्थप्रत्यगात्मोपादानावबोधरूपस्याव्यवधानतया विषयभावं प्रतिपद्यत इति । तत्र तयोर्ज्ञात्रहंतारूपयो रवभासकावभास्यसंबन्धव्यतिरेकेण नान्यत्संबन्धान्तरमुपपद्यते । अहंतारूपं त्वात्मसात्कृत्वाऽहंकञ्चुकं परिधायोपकार्यत्वोपकारकत्वक्षमः सन् बाह्यविषयेणोपकारिणापकारिणा वात्मात्मीयं संबन्धं प्रतिपद्यते ।

The mind, arising from ignorance, takes on a particular form when prompted by an external object; this is the “I”-thought. The changeless innermost Self, its basis and the form of awareness, then becomes its immediate object. Between the knower and the ego there is no relation other than that of illuminator and what is illuminated. By taking the “I”-form as itself and wearing the sheath of ego, the Self appears capable of being helped or harmed and enters into relation with external objects as “mine,” whether they benefit or injure it.

Text 197

3 · 197

तदभिधीयते ।

This is stated in the following verse:

Text 198

3 · 198

इदं ज्ञानं भवेज्ज्ञातुर्ममज्ञानं तथाहमः ।

“The knower’s cognition is ‘this’; the ego’s cognition is ‘this is mine.’

Verse 3.60

3.60

अज्ञानोपाधिनेदं स्याद्विक्रियातोऽहमो मम ॥ ६०॥

This ‘mine’-thought arises through the limiting adjunct of ignorance, from the activity of the ego.”

Text 200

3 · 200

एकस्यैव ज्ञातुरन्तर्बाह्यनिमित्तभेदाद्विभिन्नेऽपि विषय इदं ममेति ज्ञानं द्वैरूप्यं जायत इत्युक्तम् ।

Thus, even though there is only one knower, differing inner and outer objects give rise to the two forms of cognition, ‘this’ and ‘mine.’

Text 201

3 · 201

अत्रोपक्रियमाणापक्रियमाणस्यैव ज्ञातुर्विषये ममप्रत्ययो भवति विपर्यये चेदंप्रत्यय इति कथमवगम्यते ।

How is it known that the thought “mine” concerns the knower only when the knower is affected, while “this” concerns what is other?

Text 202

3 · 202

अवगम्यतामन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्याम् । तत्कथमित्याह ।

It is known through agreement and difference. How? The verse says:

Text 203

3 · 203

अनुपक्रियमाणत्वान्न ज्ञातुः स्यादहं मम ।

“Because the knower is not affected, ‘I’ and ‘mine’ cannot belong to the Self;

Verse 3.61

3.61

घटादिवदिदं तु स्यान्मोहमात्रव्यपाश्रयात् ॥ ६१॥

‘this,’ however, arises like the cognition of a pot, resting only on delusion.”

Text 205

3 · 205

मोहतत्कार्याश्रयत्वाज्ज्ञातृत्वविक्रिययोः पूर्वत्रेदंममज्ञान्वयः प्रदर्शितः । अथाधुना तद्व्यतिरेकेण व्यतिरेकप्रदर्शनार्थाह ।

Earlier, the agreement of ‘this’ and ‘mine’ with delusion and with changes in the knower was shown. Now, to show their absence when those conditions are absent, the author says:

Text 206

3 · 206

विक्रियाज्ञानशून्यत्वान्नेदं न च ममात्मनः ।

“The Self, free of change and ignorance, is neither ‘this’ nor ‘mine’;

Verse 3.62

3.62

उत्थितस्य सतोऽज्ञानं नाहमज्ञासिषं यतः ॥ ६२॥

when one wakes, one says ‘I did not know’—for ignorance belongs to the ego.”

Text 208

3 · 208

आत्मानात्मविवेकस्येयत्ताप्रदर्शनार्थमाह ।

To show how far the discrimination between Self and not-Self extends, he says:

Text 209

3 · 209

वाक्यप्रत्यक्षमानाभ्यामियानर्थः प्रतीयते ।

“This much is understood through the sentence and through perception:

Verse 3.63

3.63

अनर्थकृत्तमोहानिर्वाक्यादेव सदात्मनः ॥ ६३॥

the sentence alone removes the delusion that causes suffering and reveals the ever-present Self.”

Text 211

3 · 211

द्वितीयाध्यायादौ श्रोतृचतुष्टयमुपन्यस्तम् ।

At the beginning of the second chapter, four kinds of listeners were introduced.

Text 212

3 · 212

तत्र कृत्स्नानात्मनिवृत्तौ सत्यां यः प्रत्यगात्मन्यवाक्यार्थतां प्रतिपद्यते स क्षपिताशेषान्तरायहेतुरिति न तं प्रति वक्तव्यं किंचिदप्यवशिष्यते । योऽपि वाक्यश्रवणमात्रादेव प्रतिपद्यते तस्याप्यतीन्द्रिय शक्तिमत्त्वान्न किंचिदप्यपेक्षितव्यमस्ति । यश्च श्राविततत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यः स्वयमेवान्वयव्यतिरेको कृत्वा तदवसान एव वाक्यार्थं प्रतिपद्यतेऽसावपि यथार्थं प्रतिपन्न इति पूर्ववदेवोपेक्षितव्यः ।

Of these, nothing remains to be taught to one who, after all not-Self has been fully set aside, recognizes the Self within as beyond ordinary sentence meaning; every obstacle to that recognition has been destroyed. Nor does one need anything further who understands from merely hearing the sentence, because such a person has extraordinary insight. The person who, after hearing “you are that,” works through agreement and difference independently and understands the sentence meaning at its conclusion has also understood correctly; that person too may be left aside, as before.

Text 213

3 · 213

यः पुनरन्वयव्यतिरेकौ कारयित्वापि पुनःपुनर्वाक्यं श्राव्यते यथाभूतार्थप्रतिपत्तये तस्य कृतान्वयव्यतिरेकस्य सतः कथं वाक्यं श्राव्यत इति । उच्यते ।

But why should the sentence be repeated for someone who has already worked through agreement and difference, so that this person may understand the truth as it is? The answer is:

Text 214

3 · 214

नवसंख्याहृतज्ञानो दशमो विभ्रमाद्यथा ।

“A man who has lost count of the tenth person, though he has counted nine,

Verse 3.64

3.64

न वेत्ति दशमोऽस्मीति वीक्षमाणोऽपि तान्नव ॥ ६४॥

does not know ‘I am the tenth,’ even while looking at those nine.”

Text 216

3 · 216

अथ दृष्टान्तगतमर्थं दार्ष्टान्तिकार्थे समर्पयिष्यन्नाह ।

The author now applies the meaning of the example to the point at hand:

Text 217

3 · 217

अपविद्धद्वयोऽप्येवं तत्त्वमस्यादिना विना ।

“Even after setting aside duality, one does not know oneself as whole

Verse 3.65

3.65

वेत्ति नैकलमात्मानं नान्वेष्यं चात्र कारणम् ॥ ६५॥

without a teaching such as ‘you are that’; no further investigation is needed here.”

Text 219

3 · 219

नान्वेष्यं चात्र कारणमित्युक्तं तत्कस्मादिति चोदिते प्रत्याहान्वेषणासहिष्णुत्वात् । तत्कथमित्याह ।

When asked why no investigation is needed, he replies: because this delusion cannot withstand inquiry. How?

Text 220

3 · 220

सेयं भ्रान्तिर्निरालम्बा सर्वन्यायविरोधिनी ।

“This delusion has no foundation and conflicts with every sound line of reasoning;

Verse 3.66

3.66

सहते न विचारं सा तमो यद्वद्दिवाकरम् ॥ ६६॥

it cannot endure examination, just as darkness cannot withstand the sun.”

Text 222

3 · 222

तस्याः खल्वस्या अविद्याया भ्रान्तेः सम्यग्ज्ञानोत्पत्तिद्वारेण निवृत्तिः ।

This ignorance, this delusion, is ended by the arising of right knowledge.

Text 223

3 · 223

बुभुत्सोच्छेदिनी चास्य सदसीत्यादिना दृढम् ।

“The understanding ‘I am that real’ firmly destroys the desire to know more;

Verse 3.67

3.67

प्रतीचि प्रतिपत्तिः स्यान्नासौ मानान्तराद्भवेत् ॥ ६७॥

it arises with regard to the inward Self and does not come from any other means of knowledge.”

Text 225

3 · 225

कथं पुनर्वाक्यं प्रतिपादयत्येवेति चेद् दृष्टान्तोक्तिः ।

If asked how a sentence can indeed reveal the truth, an example is given:

Text 226

3 · 226

जिज्ञासोर्दशमं यद्वन्नवातिक्रम्य ताम्यतः ।

“Just as a man seeking the tenth, distressed after counting only nine,

Verse 3.68

3.68

त्वमेव दशमोऽसीति कुर्यादेवं प्रमां वचः ॥ ६८॥

is given true knowledge by the words ‘You yourself are the tenth,’ so too the sentence gives knowledge.”

Text 228

3 · 228

सा च तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यश्रवणजा प्रमोत्पन्नवादेव ।

The valid knowledge arising from hearing a sentence such as “you are that” is thus produced.

Text 229

3 · 229

न च नैवमिति प्रत्ययान्तर जायते । तदेतद्दृष्टान्तेन प्रतिपादयति ।

And no contrary cognition arises to dislodge it. The point is explained through the example:

Text 230

3 · 230

दशमोऽसीति वाक्योत्था न धीरस्य विहन्यते ।

“The knowledge born from the sentence ‘You are the tenth’ is not contradicted,

Verse 3.69

3.69

आदिमध्यावसानेषु न नवस्वस्य संशयः ॥ ६९॥

nor does he doubt that he is one of the nine at the beginning, middle or end.”

Text 232

3 · 232

एवं तत्त्वमसीत्यस्माद्द्वैतनुत्प्रत्यगात्मनि ।

“In the same way, the sentence ‘You are that,’ which removes duality, gives rise

Verse 3.70

3.70

सम्यग्ज्ञातत्वमर्थस्य जायेतैव प्रमा दृढा ॥ ७०॥

to firm and correct knowledge of the meaning of the Self within.”

Text 234

3 · 234

प्रत्यगात्मनि प्रमोपजायत इत्युक्तं तत्र चोद्यते ।

It has been said that valid knowledge arises regarding the inner Self. An objection is now raised:

Text 235

3 · 235

किं यथा घटादिप्रमेयविषया प्रमा कर्त्रादिकारकभेदानपह्नवेन जायते तथैवोताशेषकारकग्रामोपमर्देन कर्तुः प्रत्यगात्मनीति । उच्यते ।

“Does this knowledge of the inner Self arise like knowledge of an object such as a pot, without denying the distinctions between agent and the other factors? Or does it arise by dissolving the entire group of factors, with the inward Self as the agent?” The reply is:

Text 236

3 · 236

प्रत्यक्तास्य स्वतोरूपं निष्क्रियाकारकाफलम् ।

“Inwardness is its own nature: it is inactive, without agent, action or result.

Verse 3.71

3.71

अद्वितीयं तदिद्धा धीः प्रत्यगात्मेव लक्ष्यते ॥ ७१॥

Its nondual knowledge is indicated as being the inner Self itself.”

Text 238

3 · 238

यस्मादेवम् ।

Because this is so:

Text 239

3 · 239

विपश्चितोऽप्यतस्तस्यामात्मभावं वितन्वते ।

“Even the wise make the meaning of that sentence their very Self;

Verse 3.72

3.72

दवीयस्स्विन्द्रियार्थेषु क्षीयते ह्युत्तरोत्तरम् ॥ ७२॥

for as sense objects grow more distant, the senses become weaker in turn.”

Text 241

3 · 241

आह । यदि वाक्यमेव यथाभूतार्थवबोधकमथ कस्य हेतोरविद्योत्थापितस्य कर्तृत्वादेरुपदेश इत्युक्ते प्रतिविधीयते ।

An objection is now raised: “If the sentence itself teaches the truth as it is, why teach agency and the rest, which arise from ignorance?” The reply is:

Text 242

3 · 242

भ्रान्तिप्रसिद्ध्यानूद्यार्थं तत्तत्वं भ्रान्तिबाधया ।

“The teaching takes up what is already understood through error, in order to remove that error;

Verse 3.73

3.73

अयं नेत्युपदिश्येत तथैवं तत्त्वमित्यपि ॥ ७३॥

it teaches ‘this is that reality’ by negating the mistaken identification.”

Text 244

3 · 244

इममर्थं दृष्टान्तेन बुद्धावारोहयति ।

An example is offered to make this clear:

Text 245

3 · 245

स्थाणुः स्थाणुरितीवोक्तिर्न नृबुद्धिं निरस्यति ।

“Calling out ‘a post, a post!’ does not dispel the notion that it is a man;

Verse 3.74

3.74

व्यनुवादात्तथैवोक्तिर्भ्रान्तिं पुंसो न बाधते ॥ ७४॥

mere repetition of the mistaken appearance does not remove a person’s error.”

Text 247

3 · 247

यस्माच्छ्रोतृप्रसिद्धानुवाद्येव त्वमितिपदं तस्मादुद्दिश्यमानस्थत्वाद्दुःखित्वादेरविवक्षितत्वमेव ।

Since the word “thou” takes up what the listener already understands, its meanings such as being unhappy, which are merely indicated as its topic, are not intended [as the sentence’s final meaning].

Text 248

3 · 248

विधीयमानत्वे हि सति विरोधप्रसङ्गो न तु विधीयमाना नूद्यमानयोरिति । स्वप्रधानयोर्हि पदयोर्विरोधाशङ्का सामान्यलिङ्गतत्वात्तयोर्न विपर्यये ।

If those attributes were being newly asserted, a contradiction would follow; there is no such contradiction between what is asserted and what is merely restated. The possibility of conflict concerns two words that each primarily assert their own meaning, because the general element they share is understood by inference; it does not apply in the reverse case.

Text 249

3 · 249

अनालिङ्गितसामान्यौ न जिहासितवादिनौ ।

“The two words do not carry a shared general meaning that must be retained, nor do they propose to discard their own meanings;

Verse 3.75

3.75

व्युत्थितौ तत्त्वमौ तस्मादन्योन्याभिसमीक्षणौ ॥ ७५॥

‘that’ and ‘thou’ have distinct starting points, and so must be read in relation to one another.”

Text 251

3 · 251

अपास्तसामान्यार्थत्वादनुवादस्थत्वाद्विधीयमानेन च सह विरोधाद्दुःखित्वादेरस्तु कामं जिहासितार्थयो रसंसर्गो यथोपन्यस्तदोषविरहात्तत्त्वमर्थयोः संसर्गोऽस्तु नीलोत्पलवदिति चेन्नैवमप्युपपद्यते । तस्मात् ।

One might argue: “Let the meanings of ‘that’ and ‘thou’ be unrelated, since their shared general meanings have been removed; ‘thou’ merely restates what is known, and it conflicts with what is newly asserted. Then let the two meanings be related, as blue and lotus are related, since that avoids the stated fault.” This too does not work. Therefore:

Text 252

3 · 252

तदर्थयोस्तु निष्ठात्मा द्वयपारोक्ष्यवर्जितः ।

“The inner Self, which is the final meaning of both, is free of the indirectness found in either meaning;

Verse 3.76

3.76

नाद्वितीयं विनात्मानं नात्मा नित्यदृशा विना ॥ ७६॥

there is no Self without nonduality, nor nonduality apart from the Self that is ever the seer.”

Text 254

3 · 254

अत्राह । किमिह जिहासितं किं वोपादित्सितमिति । उच्यते ।

Someone asks: “What here is to be given up, and what is to be taken up?” The answer is:

Verse 3.77

3.77

प्रत्यगात्मार्थाभिधायिनस्त्वंपदादुभयं प्रतीयतेऽहं दुःखी प्रत्यगात्मा च । तत्र च प्रत्यगात्मनोऽहं दुःखीत्यनेनाभिसंबन्ध आत्मयाथात्म्यानवबोधहेतुक एव । अतोऽहमर्थोऽपसृष्टत्वादज्ञानोत्थत्वाच्च हेय इति प्रत्यक्षतोवसीयते । तदर्थे किं हेयं किं वोपादेयमिति नावध्रियते । तत इदमभिधीयते । पारोक्ष्यं यत्तदर्थे स्यात्तद्धेयमहमर्थवत् । प्रतीचेवाहमोऽभेदः पारोक्ष्येणात्मनोऽपि मे ॥ ७७॥

The word “thou,” which expresses the inner Self, conveys both the ordinary “I am unhappy” and the innermost Self. The connection between the innermost Self and “I am unhappy” is caused only by failure to know the Self as it truly is. The ordinary ego is therefore plainly to be relinquished: it is something superimposed and arises from ignorance. What, then, is to be given up or taken up in the meaning of “that”? This is stated in the verse: “Whatever indirectness is in the meaning of ‘that’ is to be set aside, just like the ego; the inward identity of ‘that’ with ‘I’ is the Self, though the Self too appears indirect.”

Text 256

3 · 256

कथं पुनस्तदर्थोऽद्वितीयलक्षणः प्रत्यगात्मोपाश्रयं सद्वितीयत्वं दुःखित्वं निरन्वयमपनुदतीति । उच्यते । न चैतयोर्निवर्तकनिवर्त्यभावं वयं ब्रूमः । कथं तर्हि ।

How does the meaning of “that,” characterized by nonduality and apparently distinct from the inward Self, remove the Self’s apparent duality and suffering? The answer: we do not claim that one directly removes the other. How, then?

Text 257

3 · 257

त्वमर्थे प्रत्यगात्मनि प्रागनवबुद्धाद्वितीयता सानेनावबोध्यते ।

The sentence reveals the nonduality that was previously unknown in the meaning of “thou,” the inner Self.

Text 258

3 · 258

अतोऽनवबोधनिरासेन तदुत्थस्य सद्वितीयत्वस्य त्वमर्थत्वस्य परोक्षत्वस्य च तदर्थस्थस्य निरसनान्न वैयधिकरण्यादि चोद्यस्यावसरोऽस्तीति । तदिदमभिधीयते ।

By removing that ignorance, it removes the resulting duality wrongly attributed to “thou,” and the indirectness wrongly attributed to “that.” Thus the objection that these qualities belong to different subjects has no place. This is stated as follows:

Text 259

3 · 259

तत्त्वमर्थेन संपृक्तो नानात्वं विनिवर्तयेत् ।

“Joined with the meaning of ‘that,’ the meaning of ‘thou’ removes plurality;

Verse 3.78

3.78

नापरित्यक्तपारोक्ष्यं त्वं तदर्थं सिसृप्सति ॥ ७८॥

the meaning of ‘thou,’ without first abandoning its apparent indirectness, cannot reach the meaning of ‘that.’”

Text 261

3 · 261

कस्मात्पुनः कारणात्तदर्थोऽद्वितीयलक्षणस्त्वमर्थेन प्रत्यगात्मना पृथगर्थः सन्नविद्योत्थं सद्वितीयत्वं निहन्तीति । उच्यते । विरोधात् । तदुच्यते ।

Why does the nondual meaning of “that,” though apparently distinct from the inner Self meant by “thou,” destroy the duality born of ignorance? The answer is: because they contradict it. Thus:

Text 262

3 · 262

संसारिताद्वितीयेन पारोक्ष्यं चात्मना सह ।

“The indirectness of the Self conflicts with the apparent secondness of the person in bondage;

Verse 3.79

3.79

प्रासङ्गिकं विरुद्धत्वात्तत्त्वम्भ्यां बाधनं तयोः ॥ ७९॥

the two conflicting meanings, ‘that’ and ‘thou,’ remove those appearances.”

Text 264

3 · 264

तत्त्वमर्थयोस्तु बाधकत्वेऽन्यदपि कारणमुच्यते ।

Another reason is given for the power of the meanings of “that” and “thou” to remove one another’s errors:

Text 265

3 · 265

अज्ञातपुरुषार्थत्वाच्छ्रौतत्वात्तत्त्वमर्थयोः ।

Because the meanings of “that” and “thou” concern an end not previously known and are grounded in scripture,

Verse 3.80

3.80

स्वमर्थमपरित्यज्य बाधकौ स्तां विरुद्धयोः ॥ ८०॥

they can each negate their opposed appearance without abandoning their own meaning.

Text 267

3 · 267

एवं तावद्यथोपक्रान्तेन प्रक्रियावर्त्मना न प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणान्तरैर्विरोधगन्धोऽपि संभाव्यते ।

Thus, along the line of reasoning begun earlier, no conflict whatever with perception or any other means of knowledge is possible.

Text 268

3 · 268

यदा पुनः सर्वप्रकारेणापि यतमाना नैवेमं वाक्यार्थं संभावयामः प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणान्तरविरोधत एव तस्मिन्नपि पक्ष उच्यते ।

If, despite examining the matter in every way, one still cannot reconcile the sentence meaning with other means of knowledge and assumes a conflict, the author gives an answer to that position too:

Text 269

3 · 269

प्रत्यक्षादिविरुद्धं चेद्वाक्यमर्थं वदेत्क्वचित् ।

“If a sentence were to state something contrary to perception or the other means of knowledge,

Verse 3.81

3.81

स्यात्तु तद्दृष्टिविध्यर्थं योषाग्निवदसंशयम् ॥ ८१॥

it could certainly be intended as an injunction for a ritual vision, as in the teachings about a woman and fire.”

Text 271

3 · 271

यदा तु तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यं सर्वप्रकारेणापि विचार्यमाणं न क्रियां कटाक्षेणापि वीक्षते तदा प्रसंख्यानादिव्यापारो दुस्संभाव्य इति । तदुच्यते ।

But when a sentence such as “you are that,” examined in every way, does not even glance toward an action, it is hard to maintain that an activity such as repeated meditation is required. The author therefore says:

Text 272

3 · 272

वस्त्वेकनिष्ठं वाक्यं चेन्न तस्य स्यात्क्रियार्थता ।

“If a sentence is wholly concerned with an existing reality, it cannot have action as its purpose;

Verse 3.82

3.82

वस्तुनो ह्येकरूपत्वाद्विकल्पस्याप्यसंभवः ॥ ८२॥

for reality has one nature, and therefore no alternative to be chosen among.”

Text 274

3 · 274

भिन्नविषयत्वाच्च न प्रमाणान्तरविरोधः । कथम् ।

Nor is there conflict with another means of knowledge, because their subject matters differ. How?

Text 275

3 · 275

उच्यते ।

The author says:

Text 276

3 · 276

अपूर्वाधिगमं कुर्वत्प्रमाणं स्यान्न चेन्न तत् ।

“A means of knowledge must disclose what was not previously known; if it does not, it is not a means of knowledge.

Verse 3.83

3.83

न विरोधस्ततो युक्तो विभिन्नार्थावबोधिनोः ॥ ८३॥

So there can be no reasonable conflict between two means that reveal different things.”

Text 278

3 · 278

य एवमपि भिन्नविषयाणां विरोधं वक्ति सोऽत्रापि विरोधं ब्रूयात् ।

Someone who nonetheless claims that means of knowledge with different subjects conflict could also claim a conflict in this case:

Text 279

3 · 279

नायं शब्दः कुतो यस्माद्रूपं पश्यामि चक्षुषा ।

“This is not a sound—how could it be? I see a form with my eyes.”

Verse 3.84

3.84

इति यद्वत्तथैवायं विरोधोऽक्षजवाक्ययोः ॥ ८४॥

In the same way, that would be a supposed conflict between a verbal teaching and what is seen by the senses.

Text 281

3 · 281

प्रमाणानां सतां न विरोधः श्रोत्रादीनामिव भिन्नविषयत्वात् ।

“Valid means of knowledge do not conflict, since their subjects differ, as do hearing and the other senses.”

Text 282

3 · 282

ययोश्चाभिन्नविषयत्वं तयोराखुनकुलयोरिव प्रतिनियत एव बाध्यबाधकभावः स्यात् । अतस्तदुच्यते ।

Where two means have the same subject, one has a fixed power to correct the other, as with a mongoose and a snake. Therefore:

Text 283

3 · 283

प्रत्यक्षं चेन्न शाब्दं स्याच्छाब्दं चेदक्षजं कथम् ।

“If it is perceptual, it is not verbal knowledge; if verbal, how can it be perceptual?

Verse 3.85

3.85

प्रत्यक्षाभासः प्रत्यक्षे ह्यागमाभास आगमे ॥ ८५॥

A false appearance of perception is corrected by perception; a false scriptural appearance by scripture.”

Text 285

3 · 285

न च प्रतिज्ञाहेतुदृष्टान्तन्याय इह संभवति शब्दादीनां प्रत्येकं प्रमाणत्वादत आह ।

The formal pattern of a thesis, reason and example does not apply here, because words and the other means of knowledge each have authority on their own. Therefore:

Text 286

3 · 286

स्वमहिम्ना प्रमाणानि कुर्वन्त्यर्थावबोधनम् ।

“Each means of knowledge reveals its subject by its own power;

Verse 3.86

3.86

इतरेतरसाचिव्ये प्रामाण्यं नेष्यते स्वतः ॥ ८६॥

we do not hold that its authority depends on the help of the others.”

Text 288

3 · 288

न च सुखदुःखादिसंबन्धोऽवगत्यात्मनः प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणैर्गृह्यते येन विरोधः प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणैरुद्धाट्यते ।

Nor do perception and the other means of knowledge grasp any relation between awareness and happiness, suffering and the like that could be used to show a conflict with them.

Text 289

3 · 289

दुःखितावगतिश्चेत्स्यान्न प्रमीयेत सात्मवत् ।

“If awareness itself were established as suffering, it would not be established as the Self;

Verse 3.87

3.87

कर्मण्येव प्रमा न्याय्या न तु कर्तर्यपि क्वचित् ॥ ८७॥

valid knowledge properly concerns an action or object, never the agent as such.”

Text 291

3 · 291

अभ्युपगमेऽपि च प्रसंख्यानशतेनापि नैव त्वं संभावितदोषान्मुच्यते । अत आह ।

Even if one grants this, a hundred practices of contemplation will not free you from the imagined fault. Therefore:

Text 292

3 · 292

प्रमाणबद्धमूलत्वाद्दुःखित्वं केन वार्यते ।

“If suffering is rooted in a means of knowledge, what can remove it?

Verse 3.88

3.88

अग्न्युष्णवन्निवृत्तिश्चेन्नैरात्म्यं ह्येति सौगतम् ॥ ८८॥

If it cannot be removed, the Buddhists’ doctrine of no-Self follows, as heat follows from fire.”

Text 294

3 · 294

अथ मतम् ।

Another view is stated:

Text 295

3 · 295

निराकुर्यात्प्रसंख्यानं दुःखित्वं चेत्स्वनुष्ठितम् ।

“If properly undertaken contemplation removes suffering,

Verse 3.89

3.89

प्रत्यक्षादिविरुद्धत्वात्कथमुत्पादयेत्प्रमाम् ॥ ८९॥

how could it produce valid knowledge when that knowledge conflicts with perception and the other means?”

Text 297

3 · 297

ननु प्रसंख्यानं नाम तत्त्वमस्यादिशब्दार्थान्वयव्यतिरेकयुक्तिविषयबुद्ध्याम्रेडनमभिधीयते तच्चानुष्ठीयमानं प्रमितिवर्धनया परिपूर्णां प्रमितिं जनयति न पुनरैकाग्र्यवर्धनयेति ।

One might say that contemplation means repeatedly turning the mind, through reasoning by agreement and difference, toward the meaning of words such as “you are that.” Practised in this way, it produces complete knowledge by increasing the knowledge itself, not by increasing one-pointedness.

Text 298

3 · 298

यथाशेषाशुचिनीडे स्त्रीकृणपे कामिनीति निर्वस्तुकः पुरुषायासमात्रजनितः प्रत्यय इति । तन्न । यतः ।

For example, a man may imagine a woman to be desirable even though she is only a mass of impurity and the notion is produced by his own effort; this objection does not apply, because:

Text 299

3 · 299

अभ्यासोपचयाद्बुद्धेर्यत्स्यादैकाग्र्यमेव तत् ।

“What grows through repeated practice of thought

Verse 3.90

3.90

न हि प्रमाणान्यभ्यासात्कुर्वन्त्यर्थावबोधनम् ॥ ९०॥

is only one-pointedness of mind; means of knowledge do not reveal their object through repetition.”

Text 301

3 · 301

अभ्यासोपचिता कृत्स्नं भावना चेन्निवर्तयेत् ।

“If a mental exercise strengthened by practice could remove everything,

Verse 3.91

3.91

नैकान्तिकी निवृत्तिरस्स्याद्भावनाजं हि तत्फलम् ॥ ९१॥

its removal would not be certain, for its result is produced by mental exercise.”

Text 303

3 · 303

अपि चाह ।

The author adds:

Text 304

3 · 304

दुःखस्मीत्यपि चेद्ध्वस्ता कल्पकोट्युपबृंहिता ।

“If the idea ‘I am suffering,’ reinforced for millions of ages, were destroyed,

Verse 3.92

3.92

स्वल्पीयोऽभ्यासजास्थास्न्वी भावनेत्यत्र का प्रमा ॥ ९२॥

what proof is there that a much weaker, practice-born conviction would remain firm?”

Text 306

3 · 306

ननु शास्त्रात्स्थास्नुत्वं भविष्यति । नैवम् ।

One might say that scripture will make that conviction firm. No:

Text 307

3 · 307

यथावस्थितवस्तुयाथात्म्यावबोधमात्रकारित्वाच्छास्त्रस्य ।

scripture only produces understanding of the nature of an existing thing as it is.

Text 308

3 · 308

न हि पदार्थशक्त्याधानकृच्छास्त्रम् । प्रसिद्धं च लोके ।

Scripture does not implant new powers in the things it describes. This is well known in ordinary life.

Text 309

3 · 309

भावनाजं फलं यत्स्याद्यच्च स्यात्कर्मणः फलम् ।

“The result produced by mental exercise, and the result produced by action,

Verse 3.93

3.93

न तत्स्थास्न्विति मन्तव्यं द्रविडेष्विव सङ्गतम् ॥ ९३॥

should not be thought lasting; their connection is like that of things in the Drāviḍa country.”

Text 311

3 · 311

यद्यपि प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणोपातमात्मनो दुःखित्वम् ।

Even if suffering in the Self is presented by perception and the other means of knowledge,

Text 312

3 · 312

तथापि तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्योत्थप्रत्यय एव बलीयानिति निश्चयोऽव्यभिचारिप्रामाण्यवाक्योपत्तत्वात्प्रमेयस्य च स्वत एव निर्दुःखित्वसिद्धेः । प्रत्यक्षादेस्तु व्यभिचारित्वात्संभावनायाश्च पुरुषपरिकल्पनामात्रावष्टम्भत्वाच्चेति ।

the conviction born from “you are that” is stronger: it arises from a sentence whose authority never fails, while the knowable Self is by its own nature free from suffering. Perception can err, and the appearance it supports rests only on a person’s imagination.

Text 313

3 · 313

निर्दुःखित्वं स्वतस्सिद्धं प्रत्यक्षादेश्च दुःखिता ।

“Freedom from suffering is established by itself, while perception and the like report suffering;

Verse 3.94

3.94

को ह्यात्मानमनादृत्य विश्वसेद्बाह्यमानतः ॥ ९४॥

who would trust an outward means of knowledge while disregarding the Self?”

Text 315

3 · 315

संबन्धार्थ एव ।

This is said only to explain the connection [between the passage and its context].

Text 316

3 · 316

अपि प्रत्यक्षबाधेन प्रवृत्तिः प्रत्यगात्मनि ।

“Even when one proceeds in the face of perception’s obstruction, the turn toward the inward Self

Verse 3.95

3.95

"पराञ्चि खानि" त्येतस्माद्वचसो गम्यते श्रुतेः ॥ ९५॥

is understood from scripture’s statement that the sense openings are directed outward.”

Text 318

3 · 318

अभ्युपगम्यैवमुच्यते न तु प्रमाणं सत्प्रमाणान्तरेण विरुध्यत इत्यसकृदवोचाम । यत्रापि वाक्यप्रत्यक्षयोर्विरोधाशङ्का तत्रापि पुरुषमोहवशादेव सा जायते न तु परमार्थत इति ।

This is said only for the sake of argument; we have repeatedly explained that one valid means of knowledge does not truly conflict with another. Even where a conflict between a scriptural sentence and perception is suspected, it arises only from a person’s delusion, not in reality.

Text 319

3 · 319

अत आह ।

Therefore the author says:

Text 320

3 · 320

प्रमां चेज्जनयेद्वाक्यं प्रत्यक्षादिविरोधिनीम् ।

“If a sentence should produce knowledge that conflicts with perception and the other means of knowledge…”

Verse 3.96

3.96

गौणीं प्रत्यक्षतां ब्रूयान्मुख्यार्थासंभवाद्बुधः ॥ ९६॥

“The wise would therefore call its directness figurative, since a literal meaning is impossible.”

Text 322

3 · 322

तस्यार्थस्य सुखप्रतिपत्त्यर्थमुदाहरणम् ।

An example is given to make this point easier to grasp:

Text 323

3 · 323

अग्निस्सम्यगधीतेऽसौ जहासोच्चैश्च मञ्चकः ।

“The fire was properly studied, and the bed laughed aloud.”

Verse 3.97

3.97

यथा तद्वदहंवृत्त्या लक्ष्यतेऽनर्हयापि सः ॥ ९७॥

In the same way, that Self is indicated by the unsuitable “I”-thought.

Text 325

3 · 325

कस्मात्पुनः कारणात्साक्षादेवात्मा नाभिधीयते किमनया कल्पनयेति तत्राह ।

Why is the Self not stated directly, without this imagined device? The author explains:

Text 326

3 · 326

त्वमित्यतद्विहायान्यन्न वर्तात्मावबोधते ।

“Apart from the meaning of ‘thou,’ once what is not-Self has been set aside, the Self does not become known;

Verse 3.98

3.98

समस्तीह त्वमर्थोऽपि गुणलेशेन वर्तते ॥ ९८॥

even the meaning of ‘thou’ operates here only through a trace of its attributes.”

Text 328

3 · 328

कस्मात्पुनर्हेतोर्ह्यहमित्येतदपि गुणलेशेन वर्तते न पुनः साक्षादेवेति । विधूतसर्वकल्पनाकारणस्वाभाव्यादात्मनः अत आह ।

Why does the word “I” also operate only through a trace of attributes, rather than directly? Because the Self is naturally free of every imagined distinction. Therefore:

Text 329

3 · 329

व्योम्नि धूमतुषाराभ्रमलिनानीव दुर्धियः ।

“Fools imagine, in the sky, stains of smoke, frost and clouds;

Verse 3.99

3.99

कल्पयेयुस्तथा मूढाः संसारं प्रत्यगात्मनि ॥ ९९॥

in the same way they imagine the world of bondage in the innermost Self.”

Text 331

3 · 331

ननु सर्वकल्पनानामप्यात्मन्यत्यन्तासंभवे समानेऽहंवृत्तौ कः पक्षपाते हेतुर्येन वृत्त्यन्तराणि विधूयाहंवृत्त्यैवात्मोपलक्ष्यत इति । उच्यते ।

One might object: “All imagined forms are equally impossible in the Self. Why favor the ‘I’-thought and use it to indicate the Self after setting aside every other thought?” The answer is:

Text 332

3 · 332

चिन्निभेयमहंवृत्तिः प्रतीचीवात्मनोऽन्यतः ।

“This ‘I’-thought reflects awareness and turns inward, unlike the other thoughts;

Verse 3.100

3.100

पूर्वोक्तेभ्यश्च हेतुभ्यस्तस्मादाअत्मानयोच्यते ॥ १००॥

for the reasons already given, the Self is therefore taught through it.”

Text 334

3 · 334

वृत्तिभिर्युष्मदर्थाभिर्लक्ष्यते चेद्दृशिः परः ।

“If the supreme Seer were indicated by thoughts whose meanings are ‘you’ and the rest,

Verse 3.101

3.101

अनात्मत्वं भवेत्तस्य वितथं च वचः श्रुतेः ॥ १०१॥

the Self would become not-Self, and scripture’s teaching would be false.”

Text 336

3 · 336

यथोक्तेन ।

As explained:

Text 337

3 · 337

अनेन गुणलेशेन ह्यत्यहंकर्तृकर्मया ।

“Through this slight trace of attributes, by means of the ‘I’-thought,

Verse 3.102

3.102

लक्ष्यतेऽसावहंवृत्त्या नाञ्जसात्राभिधीयते ॥ १०२॥

the Self is indicated, though it is not directly stated here.”

Text 339

3 · 339

नाञ्जसात्राभिधीयत इति को हेतुरिति चेत् ।

If asked why it is not stated directly:

Text 340

3 · 340

षष्ठीगुणक्रियाजातिरूढयः शब्दहेतवः ।

“Words depend on relation, qualities, action, class and convention;

Verse 3.103

3.103

नात्मन्यन्यतमोऽमीषां तेनात्मा नाभिधीयते ॥ १०३॥

none of these applies to the Self, and so the Self is not directly denoted.”

Text 342

3 · 342

यदि शब्दोऽभिधानाभिधेयत्वसंबन्धाङ्गीकारेण नात्मनि वर्तते कथं शब्दादहं ब्रह्मास्मीति सम्यग्बोधोत्पत्तिः ।

If words do not apply to the Self through a relation between word and denoted thing, how can the sentence “I am Brahman” produce correct knowledge?

Text 343

3 · 343

उच्यते ।

The reply is:

Text 344

3 · 344

असत्ये वर्त्मनि स्थित्वा निरुपायमुपेयते ।

“One reaches the goal by first taking a path that is not ultimately real;

Verse 3.104

3.104

आत्मत्वकारणाद्विद्मो गुणवृत्त्या विबोधिताः ॥ १०४॥

awakened through an indirect attribute, we know the Self by reason of its being our very Self.”

Text 346

3 · 346

कथं पुनरभिधानमभिधेयेनानभिसंबद्धं सदनभिधेयेऽर्थे प्रमां जनयतीति । शृणु यथानभिसंबद्धमप्यनभिधे येऽर्थेऽविद्यानिराकरणमुखेन बोधयतीत्याह ।

How can a word, unconnected with what it denotes, produce knowledge of something that cannot be denoted? Listen: though it is unconnected, it awakens knowledge by removing ignorance, as the following verse says:

Text 347

3 · 347

शयानाः प्रायशो लोके बोध्यमानाः स्वनामभिः ।

“In ordinary life, people who are asleep are addressed by their names

Verse 3.105

3.105

सहसैव प्रबुध्यन्ते यथैवं प्रत्यगात्मनि ॥ १०५॥

and at once awaken; in the same way, one awakens to the inward Self.”

Text 349

3 · 349

न हि नाम्नास्ति संबन्धो व्युत्थितस्य शरीरतः ।

“A name has no relation to the body of the person who wakes;

Verse 3.106

3.106

तथापि बुध्यते तेन यथैवं तत्त्वमित्यतः ॥ १०६॥

yet through that name the person understands—just so with ‘you are that.’”

Text 351

3 · 351

यथा च ।

And again:

Text 352

3 · 352

बोधाबोधौ नभोऽस्पृष्ट्वा कृष्णधीनीडगौ यथा ।

“Awareness and its absence, though lodged in a darkened mind, do not touch space;

Verse 3.107

3.107

बाध्येतरात्मकौ स्यातां तथेहात्मनि गम्यताम् ॥ १०७॥

in the same way, understand that in the Self they are subject to sublation and what is not sublated.”

Text 354

3 · 354

"असत्ये वर्त्मनि स्थित्वा" इत्युपश्रुत्यातिविस्मितो महता संभ्रमेण कश्चिच्चोदयति ।

Hearing the words “one reaches the goal by taking an unreal path,” someone, greatly astonished, objects:

Text 355

3 · 355

नासन्नुपायो लोकेऽस्ति परमार्थविनिश्चये ।

“There is no unreal means in ordinary life for determining what is ultimately true;

Verse 3.108

3.108

नासल्लिङ्गाद्धि बाष्पदेः कश्चिदग्निं प्रपद्यते ॥ १०८॥

no one concludes that there is fire from a false sign such as mist.”

Text 357

3 · 357

इत्येवं चोदयेद्योऽपि जोषयेत्तं घटादिना ।

To anyone who argues this way, one can point to the pot and other familiar examples:

Verse 3.109

3.109

सदसद्भ्यां विभक्तोऽसौ पर्यायश्च न चानयोः ॥ १०९॥

“The pot, in turn, is distinct from both [its cause and the absence of the pot]; the alternatives are not the same.”

Text 359

3 · 359

एवं कुचोद्यमुन्मूल्याथेदानीं प्रकृतमभिधीयते ।

Having uprooted this mistaken objection, the author returns to the main point:

Text 360

3 · 360

प्रकृतं चानभिधेये कथमभिधाश्रुतिरविद्याध्वंस्यात्मनि ज्ञानं जनयतीति । तत्रैव कारणान्तरमुच्यते ।

The question at hand is how a scriptural statement can produce knowledge of the Self, which cannot be denoted and which destroys ignorance. Another reason is now given.

Text 361

3 · 361

अतिदुस्स्थोऽप्रबोधोऽत्र ह्यात्मैवास्य प्रबुद्धता ।

“Ignorance is difficult to dislodge; the Self’s awakening is simply the recognition of the Self.

Verse 3.110

3.110

निमित्तमात्राव्द्येत्येषा नासाग्रे बदरं यथा ॥ ११०॥

It comes from a mere occasion, like a berry resting at the tip of the nose.”

Text 363

3 · 363

अनुदितानस्तमितकूटस्थबोधमात्रस्वाभाव्यादात्मनो दुस्संभाव्योऽविद्यासद्भाव इति चेन्न । अविद्याप्रसिद्ध्यैव तत्सद्भावसिद्धेरुलूकनिशावदित्यत इदमुच्यते ।

One might say that ignorance is hard to conceive when the Self is changeless, pure awareness, neither arising nor setting. This is no objection: ignorance is established by ordinary experience, just as an owl’s night is established. Hence the verse:

Text 364

3 · 364

अहो धार्ष्ट्यमविद्याया न कश्चिदतिवर्तते ।

“How bold ignorance is! No one escapes its reach.

Verse 3.111

3.111

प्रमाणं वस्त्वनादृत्य परमात्मेव तिष्ठति ॥ १११॥

Ignoring the means of knowledge, it stands there as though it were the Supreme Self.”

Text 366

3 · 366

यस्मादविद्याप्रसिद्ध्यैवाविद्यासद्भावसिद्धिरतआत्मवस्तुवृत्तानुरोधेन न कथंचनापि तत्संभावनाप्यस्ति यत आह ।

Since ignorance is established only through ordinary experience, and cannot be supposed in keeping with the reality of the Self, the author says:

Text 367

3 · 367

ज्ञानं यस्य निजं रूपं क्रियाकारकवर्जितम् ।

“For the one whose own nature is knowledge, free of action and the factors of action,

Verse 3.112

3.112

संभावनाप्यविद्यायास्तत्र स्यात्केन हेतुना ॥ ११२॥

by what reason could ignorance even be conceived?”

Text 369

3 · 369

सोऽयमेवमनुदितानस्तमितावगतिमात्रशरीर आत्मापि सन्नविचारितप्रसिद्धाविद्यामात्रव्यवहित एवातथैवेक्ष्यते यतोऽतः ।

Though the Self is awareness alone, without arising or setting, it is nevertheless seen otherwise, screened by ignorance accepted without examination. Therefore:

Text 370

3 · 370

अनुमानादयं भावाव्द्यावृत्तोऽभावमाश्रितः ।

“Through inference this reality is excluded from what exists and assigned to nonexistence;

Verse 3.113

3.113

ततोऽप्यस्य निवृत्तिः स्याद्वाक्यादेव बुभुत्सतः ॥ ११३॥

for the seeker, its removal too comes only from the sentence.”

Text 372

3 · 372

भाववदभावादपि निवृत्तिरनुमानादेव किमिति न भवतीति चेच्छृणु ।

If asked why inference cannot remove nonexistence just as it removes what exists, listen:

Text 373

3 · 373

यतो नानुमानेन व्याविद्धाशेषक्रियाकारकफलात्मनि स्वाराज्येऽभिषेक्तुं शक्यते तस्मात् ।

Inference cannot enthrone one in the sovereignty free from all actions, agents, instruments and results. Therefore:

Text 374

3 · 374

अविद्यानिद्रया सोऽयं प्रसुप्तो दुर्विवेकया ।

“This person lies asleep, overcome by the sleep of ignorance, hard to discern;

Verse 3.115

3.115

भावाभावव्युदासिन्या श्रुत्यैव प्रतिबोध्यते ॥ ११५॥

only scripture, which removes both existence and nonexistence, can awaken him.”

Text 376

3 · 376

अत्राहानुदितानस्तमितविज्ञानात्ममात्रस्वरूपत्वाद्दुस्संभाव्या अविद्येति । नैतदेवम् । कुतः । यत आह ।

Someone objects: “Ignorance is hard to conceive because the Self is awareness alone, without arising or setting.” This is not so. Why? The verse says:

Text 377

3 · 377

कुतो अविद्येति चोद्यं स्यान्नैव प्राग्घेत्वसंभवात् ।

“Beforehand, the question ‘Whence did ignorance come?’ cannot arise, because its cause is not available;

Verse 3.116

3.116

कालत्रयापरिच्छित्तेर्न चोर्ध्वं चोद्यसंभवः ॥ ११६॥

nor can the question arise afterward, since it is not bounded by any of the three times.”

Text 379

3 · 379

यस्मात्तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यमेवात्मनोऽशेषामविद्यां निरन्वयामपनुदति तस्मात् ।

Because a sentence such as “you are that” alone removes every trace of ignorance about the Self:

Text 380

3 · 380

अद्धातममनादृत्य प्रमाणं सदसीति ये ।

“Those who disregard the true means of knowledge and seek to know ‘you are that’ by other means

Verse 3.117

3.117

बुभुत्सन्तेऽन्यतः कुर्युस्तेऽक्ष्णापि रसवेदनम् ॥ ११७॥

might as well try to taste flavors with their eyes.”

Text 382

3 · 382

एवमप्रतिहतामहं ब्रह्मेति प्रमां तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्यं कुर्वदपि न प्रतिपादयतीति चेदभिमतं न कुतश्चनापि प्रतिपत्तिः स्यादत आह ।

If one says that even though the sentence “I am Brahman” produces unobstructed valid knowledge, it does not actually teach it, then nothing could ever be understood. Therefore:

Text 383

3 · 383

इदं चेदनृतं ब्रूयात्सत्यामवगतावपि ।

“If this sentence were false, even when awareness is present,

Verse 3.118

3.118

न चान्यत्रापि विश्वासो ह्यवगत्यविशेषतः ॥ ११८॥

there would be no ground for trusting any other sentence either, since they are all alike in yielding awareness.”

Text 385

3 · 385

न चोपादित्सिताद्वाक्यार्थाद्वाक्यार्थान्तरं कल्पयितुं युक्तम् । यस्मात् ।

It is not reasonable to invent a different meaning for the sentence than the one intended. For:

Text 386

3 · 386

न चेदनुभवोऽतः स्यात्पदार्थावगतावपि ।

“If no experience arose even when the word’s meaning was understood,

Verse 3.119

3.119

कल्प्यं विध्यन्तरं तत्र न ह्यन्योऽर्थोऽवगम्यते ॥ ११९॥

one would have to invent a separate injunction; no other meaning is actually understood.”

Text 388

3 · 388

न च यथाभिमतोऽर्थो यथोक्तेन न्यायेन नावसीयते ।

Nor is the intended meaning impossible to determine by the reasoning already explained.

Text 389

3 · 389

कोऽसौ न्याय इत्याह ।

The author states that reasoning:

Text 390

3 · 390

नामादिभ्यो निराकृत्य त्वमर्थं निष्परिग्रहः ।

“Setting aside names and the rest, one should relinquish the meaning of ‘thou,’

Verse 3.120

3.120

निस्स्पृहो युष्मदर्थेभ्यः शमादिविधिचोदितः ॥ १२०॥

be free of desire for what is other than oneself, and follow the disciplines of calm and the rest.”

Text 392

3 · 392

भङ्क्त्वा चान्नमयादींस्तान्पञ्चानात्मतयार्गलान् ।

“Breaking through the five sheaths, beginning with the food-made body, as barriers that are not-Self,

Verse 3.121

3.121

अहं ब्रह्मेति वाक्यार्थं वेत्ति चेन्नार्थ ईहया ॥ १२१॥

one understands the sentence meaning ‘I am Brahman’ without further striving.”

Text 394

3 · 394

न चेदेवमुपगम्यते वाक्यस्य प्रमाणस्य सतोऽप्रामाण्यं प्राप्नोति । तदाह ।

If this is not accepted, a sentence that is in fact a valid means of knowledge would be declared invalid. The author says:

Text 395

3 · 395

यदर्थं च प्रवृत्तं यद्वाक्यं तत्र न चेच्छ्रुतम् ।

“If the sentence is heard but does not produce valid knowledge

Verse 3.122

3.122

प्रमामुत्पादयेत्तस्य प्रामाण्यं केन हेतुना ॥ १२२॥

of the very matter for which it was spoken, on what grounds is it a valid means of knowledge?”

Text 397

3 · 397

अथ मन्यसे ।

Suppose you think:

Text 398

3 · 398

जानीयाच्चेत्प्रसंख्यानाच्छब्दस्सत्यवचाः कथम् ।

“If the truth is known through repeated contemplation, how can the word itself be true?

Text 399

3 · 399

पारोक्ष्यं शब्दो नः प्राह प्रसंख्यानात्त्वसंशयम् ॥

The word tells us the Self is indirect; through contemplation it becomes certain.”

Text 400

3 · 400

१२३॥

[This is the conclusion of verse 123.]

Text 401

3 · 401

न च युक्तिशब्दावृत्तिलक्षणात्प्रसंख्यानाद्यथावत्प्रतिपत्तिर्भविष्यतीति संभावयामः । यस्मात् ।

We cannot suppose that correct understanding will arise from contemplation consisting of repeatedly turning over the reasoning and the words. For:

Text 402

3 · 402

युक्तिशब्दौ पुराप्यस्य न चेदकुरुतां प्रमाम् ।

“If the words and reasoning did not produce valid knowledge before,

Verse 3.124

3.124

साक्षादावर्तनात्ताभ्यां किमपूर्वं फलिष्यति ॥ १२४॥

what new result will come from simply repeating them inwardly?”

Text 404

3 · 404

अथैवमपि प्रसंख्यानमन्तरेण प्राणान्धारयितुं न शक्नोषीति चेच्छ्रवणादावेव संपादयिष्यामः । कथम् ।

If you say that without such contemplation you cannot even sustain life, we will provide for it during listening itself. How?

Text 405

3 · 405

प्रसंख्याने श्रुतावस्य न्यायोऽस्त्वाम्रेडनात्मकः ।

“The same principle of repetition used in contemplation can be applied to hearing:

Verse 3.125

3.125

ईषच्छ्रुतं सामिश्रुतं सम्यक् श्रुत्वावगच्छति ॥ १२५॥

what was heard only faintly or in part is understood when heard properly.”

Text 407

3 · 407

ननु प्रसंख्यानविधिमनभ्युपगच्छतः पारमहंसी चर्या बौद्धादिचर्यावदशास्त्रपूर्विका प्राप्नोति ततश्चारूढपतितत्वं न स्यादशेषकर्मणां च निवृत्तिर्न प्राप्नोतीति । उच्यते ।

An objection: “If you reject an injunction to repeated contemplation, the highest renunciant’s way of life will be unscriptural, like the practices of Buddhists and others. Then there can be no ‘fallen’ person who has attained the path, and the cessation of all action cannot follow.” The answer is:

Text 408

3 · 408

त्वमर्थस्यावबोधाय विधिरप्याश्रितो यतः ।

“An injunction has also been accepted for understanding the meaning of ‘thou’;

Verse 3.126

3.126

तमन्तरेण ये दोषास्तेऽपि नायान्त्यहेतवः ॥ १२६॥

without it, those faults would not arise either, since they have no basis.”

Chapter colophon

Adhyāya 3

इति तृतीयोऽध्यायः ॥ ३॥

[End of the third chapter.]

Adhyāya 4

Chapter heading

Adhyāya 4

अथ चतुर्थोऽध्यायः ।

Now begins the fourth chapter.

Text 2

4 · 2

पूर्वाध्यायेषु यद्वस्तु विस्तरेणोदितं स्फुटम् ।

“What was explained clearly and at length in the earlier chapters,

Verse 4.1

4.1

संक्षेपतोऽधुना वक्ष्ये तदेव सुखवित्तये ॥ १॥

I will now state in brief, for the understanding of those who seek ease.”

Text 4

4 · 4

संक्षेपविस्तराभ्यां हि मन्दोत्तमधियां नृणाम् ।

“When a subject is taught briefly and at length, people of lesser and greater understanding

Verse 4.2

4.2

वस्तूच्यमानमेत्यन्तःकरणं तेन भण्यते ॥ २॥

take it into the mind; for that reason it is taught in both ways.”

Text 6

4 · 6

आत्मानात्मा च लोकेऽस्मिन्प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणतः ।

“In this world, the Self and not-Self are established through perception and other means of knowledge;

Verse 4.3

4.3

सिद्धस्तयोरनात्मा तु सर्वत्रैवात्मपूर्वकः ॥ ३॥

yet everywhere the not-Self depends on the Self as its ground.”

Text 8

4 · 8

अनात्मत्वं स्वतस्सिद्धं देहाद्भिन्नस्य वस्तुनः ।

“The not-Selfhood of what is distinct from the body is self-evident;

Verse 4.4

4.4

ज्ञातुरप्यात्मता तद्वन्मध्ये संशयदर्शनम् ॥ ४॥

the knower too is taken to be the Self, but doubt arises between them.”

Text 10

4 · 10

असाधारणांस्तयोर्धर्मान् ज्ञात्वा धूमाग्निवद्बुधः ।

“Having learned their distinctive properties, the wise person reasons from them, as from smoke to fire,

Verse 4.5

4.5

अनात्मनोऽथ बुद्ध्यन्तान् जानीयादनुमानतः ॥ ५॥

and thus recognizes the body, intellect and the rest as not-Self.”

Text 12

4 · 12

इदमित्येव बाह्येऽर्थे ह्यहमित्येव बोद्धरि ।

“For external objects there is the thought ‘this’; for the knower, the thought ‘I.’

Verse 4.6

4.6

द्वयं दृष्टं यतो देहे तेनायं मुह्यते जनः ॥ ६॥

Since both are seen in the body, people are confused.”

Text 14

4 · 14

केन पुनर्न्यायेनात्मानात्मनोरश्वमहिषयोरिव विभागः क्रियत इति । उच्यते ।

By what reasoning can the Self be distinguished from the not-Self, as a horse from a buffalo? The answer:

Text 15

4 · 15

न्यायः पुरोदितोऽस्माभिरात्मानात्मविभागकृत् ।

“We have already explained the reasoning that distinguishes Self from not-Self.

Verse 4.7

4.7

तेनेदमर्थमुत्सार्य ह्यहमित्यत्र यो भवेत् ॥ ७॥

Using it, one should set aside the meaning of ‘this’; whatever remains as ‘I’

Text 17

4 · 17

विद्यात्तत्त्वमसीत्यस्माद्भावाभावदृशं सदा ।

one should know from ‘you are that’ as the ever-present witness of being and nonbeing,

Verse 4.8

4.8

अनन्तरमबाह्यार्थं प्रत्यक्स्थं मुनिरञ्जसा ॥ ८॥

immediately inward and not an external object, O sage.”

Text 19

4 · 19

उच्यतां तर्हि कया तु परिपाय्या वाक्यार्थं वेत्तीति ।

Then by what method does one understand the sentence meaning?

Text 20

4 · 20

उच्यते । अन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्याम् ।

The answer: through agreement and difference.

Text 21

4 · 21

त्यक्तकृत्स्नेदमर्थत्वात्त्यक्तोऽहमिति मन्यते ।

“Having given up the entire meaning of ‘this,’ one thinks, ‘I too have been given up’,

Verse 4.9

4.9

नावगच्छाम्यहं यस्मान्निजात्मानमनात्मनः ॥ ९॥

because I do not know my own Self as distinct from the not-Self.”

Text 23

4 · 23

अथ शरीरादिबुद्धिपर्यन्तः स सर्वोऽनात्मैवेति प्रमाणाद्विनिश्चित्य किमिति बुभुत्सातो नोपरमते । शृणु ।

If, on the evidence, one has determined that everything from the body through the intellect is not-Self, why does the desire to know not cease? Listen:

Text 24

4 · 24

अनुच्छिन्नबुभुत्सश्च प्रत्यग्घेतोरनात्मनः ।

“The desire to know remains unended because the cause of inwardness is not yet understood;

Verse 4.10

4.10

दोलायमानचित्तोऽयं मुह्यते भौतवन्नरः ॥ १०॥

the person’s mind wavers and is deluded, like one made of the elements.”

Text 26

4 · 26

अविलुप्तविज्ञानात्मन आत्मत्वादेव नित्यसान्निध्याद्बुभुत्सुः किमिति न प्रतिपद्यत इति। यस्मात् ।

Why does the seeker not recognize the Self, though awareness remains undiminished and is always present? Because:

Text 27

4 · 27

यैरद्राक्षीत्पुरात्मानं यमनात्मेति वीक्षते ।

“By the very means through which one formerly saw the Self, one now regards it as not-Self;

Verse 4.11

4.11

दृष्टेर्द्रष्टारमात्मानं तैः प्रसिद्धैः प्रमित्सति ॥ ११॥

one seeks to know, through familiar means of knowledge, the very Seer of seeing.”

Text 29

4 · 29

कस्मात्पुनर्हेतोः पराचीनाभिः शब्दाद्यवलेहिनीभिर्बुद्धिभिरात्मानमनात्मवन्न वीक्षत इति । उच्यते ।

Why does one not see the Self as one sees not-Self through outward-directed thoughts that savor sound and the rest? The answer:

Text 30

4 · 30

चक्षुर्न वीक्षते शब्दमतदात्मत्वकारणात् ।

“The eye does not see sound, because sound is not of its nature;

Verse 4.12

4.12

यथैवं भौतिकी दृष्टिर्नात्मानं परिपश्यति ॥ १२॥

in the same way, sight formed of the physical elements cannot fully see the Self.”

Text 32

4 · 32

प्रत्यक्षादिप्रमाणस्वाभाव्यानुरोधेन तावत्तददर्शनकारणमुक्तम् ।

The reason it is not seen has so far been explained from the nature of perception and the other means of knowledge.

Text 33

4 · 33

अथ प्रमेयस्वाभाव्यानुरोधेन प्रतिषेध उच्यते ।

Now the denial is explained from the nature of the object of knowledge:

Text 34

4 · 34

धीविक्रियासहस्राणां हानोपादानधर्मिणाम् ।

“The Self is the constant witness of thousands of changes in the intellect, which are taken up and set aside;

Verse 4.13

4.13

सदा साक्षिणमात्मानं प्रत्यक्त्वान्नाहमीक्षते ॥ १३॥

because it is inward, the ‘I’-thought does not see it.”

Text 36

4 · 36

क्व पुनरियं विवेकबुद्धिः किमात्मन्युतानात्मनीति । किंचातः ।

Where does this discriminating understanding occur—in the Self or in the not-Self? And what follows from either answer?

Text 37

4 · 37

यद्यात्मनि कूटस्थत्वव्याघातोऽनात्मदर्शित्वात् ।

If it occurs in the Self, seeing not-Self contradicts the Self’s changelessness;

Text 38

4 · 38

अथानात्मनि तस्याप्यचैतन्यान्न विवेकसंबन्ध इति । उच्यते ।

if in the not-Self, that too is unconscious, so how could discrimination belong to it? The reply is:

Text 39

4 · 39

"दाह्यदाहकतैकत्र" इत्युक्तपरिहारात् ।

As already resolved by the statement “burner and burned can be in one place,”

Text 40

4 · 40

बुद्धावेव विवेकोऽयं यदनात्मतया भिदा ।

“this discrimination occurs in the intellect: it distinguishes the Self from what is not-Self.”

Verse 4.14

4.14

बुद्धिमेवोपमृद् नाति कदलीं तत्फलं यथा ॥ १४॥

“It acts on the intellect alone, as fruit is said to act on a banana plant.”

Text 42

4 · 42

सोऽयमतत्त्वे तत्त्वदृक् ।

This is the one who sees truth in what is not true.

Text 43

4 · 43

अनुमानप्रदीपेन हित्वा सर्वाननात्मनः ।

“Using the lamp of inference, he sets aside all that is not-Self;

Verse 4.15

4.15

संसारैकावलम्बिन्या तदभावं धियेप्सति ॥ १५॥

yet with a mind relying solely on worldly bondage, he seeks the absence of that bondage.”

Text 45

4 · 45

योऽयमन्वयव्यतिरेकजो विवेक आत्मानात्मविभागलक्षणोऽनात्मस्थः स्थाणौ संशयावबोधवत् प्रतिपत्तव्योऽन्यथावस्तुस्वाभाव्यान्मृगतृष्णिकोदकप्रबोधववदित्यत आह ।

The discrimination born from agreement and difference, which distinguishes Self from not-Self, occurs in the not-Self, like mistaking a post for a person. It must be understood otherwise, in keeping with the nature of the thing, as one recognizes water in a mirage. Therefore:

Text 46

4 · 46

संसारबीजसंस्थोऽयं तद्धिया मुक्तिमिच्छति ।

“Established in the very seed of bondage, this person seeks liberation through a thought born of it;

Verse 4.16

4.16

शशो निमीलनेनेव मृत्युं परिजिहीर्षति ॥ १६॥

like a hare, he hopes to escape death by closing his eyes.”

Text 48

4 · 48

अस्यार्थस्य द्रढिम्ने श्रुत्युदाहरणम् ।

A scriptural example is offered to strengthen this point:

Text 49

4 · 49

इममर्थं पुरस्कृत्य श्रुत्या सम्यगुदाहृतम् ।

“With this meaning in view, scripture states it clearly,

Verse 4.17

4.17

"यच्चक्षुषे"ति विस्रब्धं "न दृष्टे "रिति च स्फुटम् ॥ १७॥

plainly saying ‘that which is before the eye’ and ‘not seen.’”

Text 51

4 · 51

बुद्ध्यन्तमपविद्ध्यैवं को न्वहं स्यामितीइक्षितुः ।

“Having set aside everything down to the intellect, one asks, ‘Who am I?’

Verse 4.18

4.18

श्रुतिस्तत्त्वमसीत्याह सर्वमानातिगामिनी ॥ १८॥

Scripture replies, ‘You are that,’ which goes beyond every means of knowledge.”

Text 53

4 · 53

एष संक्षेपतः पूर्वाध्यायत्रयस्यार्थ उक्तः ।

This is a brief statement of the meaning of the first three chapters.

Text 54

4 · 54

सोऽयं न्याय्योऽपि वेदान्तार्थः शास्त्राचार्यप्रसादलभ्योऽप्यनपेक्षितशास्त्राचार्यप्रसादोऽनन्यापेक्षसिद्धस्वभावत्वात्कैश्चिच्छ्रद्धधानैर्न प्रतीयते ।

Although this Vedānta teaching is reasonable and can be gained through the grace of scripture and teacher, some faithful people do not understand it, because its nature is self-established and depends on nothing else.

Text 55

4 · 55

तेषां संग्रहार्थमभिमतप्रामाण्योदाहरणम् ।

To gather their understanding, an example accepted as authoritative is offered:

Text 56

4 · 56

भगवत्पूज्यपादैश्च उदाहार्येवमेव तु ।

“The revered teacher, who sought the welfare of all beings, also taught just this;

Verse 4.19

4.19

सुविस्पष्टोऽस्मदुक्तोऽर्थः सर्वभूतहितैषिभिः ॥ १९॥

in this way he made the point we have stated perfectly clear.”

Text 58

4 · 58

किं परमात्मन उपदेश उतापरमात्मन इति । किं चातः ।

Is the teaching addressed to the Supreme Self or to the individual self? What follows?

Text 59

4 · 59

यदि परमात्मनस्तस्योपदेशमन्तरेणैव मुक्तत्वान्निरर्थक उपदेशः । अथापरमात्मनस्तस्यापि स्वत एव संसारस्वभावत्वान्निष्फल उपदेशः । एवमुभयत्रापि दोषवत्त्वादत आह ।

If to the Supreme Self, the teaching is pointless because that Self is already free; if to the individual, the teaching is fruitless because that self is by nature bound. Since either view has a fault, the author says:

Text 60

4 · 60

"अविविच्योभयं वक्ति श्रुतिश्चेत्स्याद् ग्रहस्तथा " ।

“If scripture spoke of the two without distinguishing them, it would be caught in that same confusion”

Verse 4.20

4.20

इति पक्षमुपादाय पूर्वपक्षं निशात्य च ॥ २०॥

—this position is taken up and refuted below.

Text 62

4 · 62

तच्चेदमविवेकात्स्वतो विविक्तात्मने तत्त्वमसीत्युपदिष्टम् ।

In fact, “you are that” is taught to one who, through confusion, fails to distinguish the Self that is already distinct by nature.

Text 63

4 · 63

युष्मदस्मद्विभागज्ञे स्यादर्थवदिदं वचः ।

“This statement is meaningful for one who knows the difference between ‘you’ and ‘I’;

Verse 4.21

4.21

यतोऽनभिज्ञे वाक्यं स्याद्बधिरेष्विव गायनम् ॥ २१॥

to one who does not know it, the words are like singing to the deaf.”

Text 65

4 · 65

तस्य च युष्मदस्मद्विभागविज्ञानस्य का युक्तिरुपायभावं प्रतिपद्यते । शृणु ।

What reasoning or method establishes knowledge of the distinction between ‘you’ and ‘I’? Listen:

Text 66

4 · 66

"अन्वयव्यतिरेकौ हि पदार्थस्य पदस्य च ।

“Agreement and difference are the reasoning for the meaning of a thing and of a word;

Verse 4.22

4.22

स्यादेतदहमित्यत्र युक्तिरेवावधारणम् " ॥ २२॥

here they alone establish what is meant by ‘I.’”

Text 68

4 · 68

कथं तौ युक्तिरित्यत्राह ।

How are these two forms of reasoning applied? The author says:

Text 69

4 · 69

"नाद्राक्षमहमित्यस्मिन् सुषुप्तेऽन्यन्मनागपि ।

“In deep sleep I did not see even the slightest thing other than this;

Verse 4.23

4.23

न वारयति दृष्टिं स्वां प्रत्ययं तु निषेधति " ॥ २३॥

this does not deny my own awareness, only the mental cognition.”

Text 71

4 · 71

"एवं विज्ञातवाच्यार्थे श्रुतिलोकप्रसिद्धितः ।

“When the word’s ordinary meaning has been understood from scripture and common usage,

Verse 4.24

4.24

श्रुतिस्तत्त्वमसीत्याह श्रोतुर्मोहापनुत्तये " ॥ २४॥

scripture says ‘you are that’ to remove the listener’s delusion.”

Text 73

4 · 73

तत्र त्वमिति पदं यत्र लक्षणया वर्तते सोऽर्थ उच्यते ।

Here the meaning expressed figuratively by the word “thou” is now stated:

Text 74

4 · 74

"अहं शब्दस्य या निष्ठा ज्योतिषि प्रत्यगात्मनि ।

“The essential meaning of the word ‘I’ is the light within, the innermost Self;

Verse 4.25

4.25

सैवोक्ता सदसीत्येवं फलं तत्र विमुक्तता " ॥ २५॥

that alone is stated as ‘you are real’; its fruit is liberation.”

Text 76

4 · 76

अन्यच्चान्वयव्यतिरेकोदाहरणम् । तथा ।

Here is another example of reasoning by agreement and difference:

Text 77

4 · 77

"छित्त्वा त्यक्तेन हस्तेन स्वयं नात्मा विशेष्यते ।

“A hand, once cut off and discarded, no longer serves to distinguish the person;

Verse 4.26

4.26

तथा शिष्टेन सर्वेण येनयेन विशेष्यते ॥ २६॥

in the same way, whatever remains after each [attribute] is set aside may be used to distinguish the Self.”

Text 79

4 · 79

विशेषणमिदं सर्वं साध्वलंकरणं यथा ।

“All these attributes are like fine ornaments;

Verse 4.27

4.27

अविद्याध्यस्तमतः सर्वं ज्ञात आत्मन्यसद्भवेत् ॥ २७॥

once known as superimposed by ignorance, they are seen to be absent in the Self.”

Text 81

4 · 81

तस्मात्त्यक्तेन हस्तेन तुल्यं सर्वं विशेषणम् ।

“Every attribute is therefore like the discarded hand;

Verse 4.28

4.28

अनात्मत्वेन तस्माज्ज्ञो मुक्तः सर्वविशेषणैः ॥ २८॥

knowing this, the wise are free of every attribute, since all are not-Self.”

Text 83

4 · 83

ज्ञातैवात्मा सदा ग्राह्यो ज्ञेयमुत्सृज्य केवलः ।

“The knower alone is always to be recognized as the Self; the known is to be set aside.

Verse 4.29

4.29

अहमित्यपि यद्ग्राह्यं व्यपेताङ्गसमं हि तत् " ॥ २९॥

Whatever can be grasped as ‘I’ is like a limb that has been removed.”

Text 85

4 · 85

"दृश्यत्वादहमित्येष नात्मधर्मो घटादिवत् ।

“Because it is seen, the ‘I’-thought is not a property of the Self, like a pot;

Verse 4.30

4.30

तथान्ये प्रत्यया ज्ञेया दोषाश्चात्मामलो ह्यतः " ॥ ३०॥

the other thoughts are likewise known, and their defects do not stain the Self, which is pure.”

Text 87

4 · 87

सर्वन्यायोपसंग्रहः ।

This gathers together all the reasoning:

Text 88

4 · 88

"नित्यमुक्तत्वविज्ञानं वाक्याद्भवति नान्यतः ।

“Knowledge of being ever free arises from the sentence, not from anything else.

Verse 4.31

4.31

वाक्यार्थस्यापि विज्ञानं पदार्थस्मृतिपूर्वकम् ॥ ३१॥

Understanding the sentence meaning, too, is preceded by recalling the meanings of its words.

Text 90

4 · 90

अन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां पदार्थस्स्मर्यते ध्रुवम् ।

Through agreement and difference the meanings of the words are surely recalled;

Verse 4.32

4.32

एवं निर्दुःखमात्मानमक्रियं प्रतिपद्यते ॥ ३२॥

in this way one recognizes the sorrowless, actionless Self.”

Text 92

4 · 92

सदेवेत्यादिवाक्येभ्यः प्रमा स्फुटतरा भवेत् ।

“From sentences such as ‘Being alone was this,’ knowledge becomes especially clear,

Verse 4.33

4.33

दशमस्त्वमसीत्यस्माद्यथैवं प्रत्यगात्मनि " ॥ ३३॥

just as the sentence ‘You are the tenth’ does; so too regarding the inward Self.”

Text 94

4 · 94

वीक्षापन्नस्योदाहरणम् ।

Here is an example of someone who has fallen into error:

Text 95

4 · 95

"नवबुद्ध्यपहाराद्धि स्वात्मानं दशपूरणम् ।

“Having lost count by miscounting the nine, a person does not see himself as the tenth,

Verse 4.34

4.34

अपश्यन् ज्ञातुमेवेच्छेत्स्वमात्मानं जनस्तथा ॥ ३४॥

and yet longs to know his own self, which completes the ten.”

Text 97

4 · 97

अविद्याबद्धचक्षुष्ट्वात्कामापहृतधीः सदा ।

“With sight blinded by ignorance and understanding stolen by desire,

Verse 4.35

4.35

विविक्तं दृशिमात्मानं नेक्षते दशमं यथा " ॥ ३५॥

he does not see the distinct witnessing Self, just as the man fails to see that he is the tenth.”

Text 99

4 · 99

सोऽयमेवमविद्यापटलावगुण्ठितदृष्टिः सन्कथमुत्थाप्यत इत्याह ।

How, then, is this person, whose vision is veiled by ignorance, awakened? The author says:

Text 100

4 · 100

यथा स्वापनिमित्तेन स्वप्नदृक्प्रतिबोधितः ।

“Just as a dreamer is awakened by the cause of waking,

Verse 4.36

4.36

करणं कर्म कर्तारं स्वाप्नं नैवेक्षते स्वतः ॥ ३६॥

and then sees none of the dream’s instruments, actions or agents as real,”

Text 102

4 · 102

अनात्मज्ञस्तथैवायं सम्यक् श्रुत्यावबोधितः ।

“so the one who does not know the Self is rightly awakened by scripture;

Verse 4.37

4.37

गुरुं शास्त्रं तथा मूढं स्वात्मनोऽन्यं न पश्यति ॥ ३७॥

the deluded person then sees neither teacher nor scripture as other than his own Self.”

Text 104

4 · 104

स किं सकलसंसारप्रविविक्तमात्मानं वाक्यात्प्रतिपद्यत उत नेतीति । अत्र ब्रूमः । कूटस्थावगतिशेषमात्रत्वात्प्रतिपत्तेरत आह ।

Does the person, then, understand from the sentence a Self entirely separate from worldly existence, or not? We answer: recognition leaves only changeless awareness. Therefore:

Text 105

4 · 105

दण्डावसाननिष्ठस्स्याद्दण्डसर्पो यथा तथा ।

“Just as a snake imagined on a staff resolves into the staff,

Verse 4.38

4.38

नित्यावगतिनिष्ठं स्याद्वाक्याज्जगदसंशयम् ॥ ३८॥

so, without doubt, the whole world resolves into ever-present awareness through the sentence.”

Text 107

4 · 107

कुत एतत् । यस्मात् ।

Why is this so? Because:

Text 108

4 · 108

पश्यन्निति यदाहोच्चैः प्रत्यक्त्वमजमव्ययम् ।

“The scripture that speaks clearly of seeing, of what is inward, unborn and undecaying,

Verse 4.39

4.39

अपूर्वानपरानन्तं त्वमा तदुपलक्ष्यते ॥ ३९॥

indicates you as without beginning, without end and boundless.”

Text 110

4 · 110

तत्त्वमस्यादिवाक्योत्थविज्ञानैव बाध्यते । यस्मात् ।

Only the knowledge arising from a sentence such as “you are that” sublates the error. For:

Text 111

4 · 111

अस्माद्यदपरं रूपं नास्तीत्येव निरूप्यते ।

“It teaches that no form other than this Self exists;

Verse 4.40

4.40

अन्यथाग्रहणाभावाद्बीजं तत्स्वप्नबोधयोः ॥ ४०॥

misapprehension is the seed of both dream and waking.”

Text 113

4 · 113

अस्यार्थस्य द्रढिम्न उदाहरणम् ।

An example is given to strengthen this point:

Text 114

4 · 114

"कार्यकारणबद्धौ ताविष्येते विश्वतैजसौ ।

“The waking person and the dreamer are held to effects and causes;

Verse 4.41

4.41

प्राज्ञः कारणबद्धस्तु द्वौ तौ तुर्ये न सिध्यतः " ॥ ४१॥

the sleeper is held to causes, but neither is established in the Fourth.”

Text 116

4 · 116

"अन्यथागृहणतः स्वप्नो निद्रा तत्त्वमजानतः ।

“Dream arises from misapprehension; sleep from not knowing reality.

Verse 4.42

4.42

विपर्यासे तयोः क्षीणे तुरीयं पदमश्नुते " ॥ ४२॥

When both errors are ended, one attains the state called the Fourth.”

Text 118

4 · 118

तथा भगवत्पादीयमुदाहरणम् ।

Here is another example from the revered teacher:

Text 119

4 · 119

"सुषुप्ताख्यं तमोऽज्ञानं बीजं स्वप्नप्रबोधयोः ।

“The darkness called deep sleep, ignorance, is the seed of dream and waking;

Text 120

4 · 120

आत्मबोधप्रदग्धं स्याद्बीजं दग्धं यथाभवम् " ॥

when knowledge of the Self burns it, that seed is like a seed that has been scorched.”

Text 121

4 · 121

४३॥

[End of verse 43.]

Text 122

4 · 122

एवं गौडैर्द्राविडैर्नः पूज्यैरयमर्थः प्रभाषितः ।

“In this way our revered teachers of Gauḍa and Drāviḍa have explained the point:

Verse 4.44

4.44

अज्ञानमात्रोपाधिस्सन्नहमादिदृगीश्वरः ॥ ४४॥

the Lord, the Seer of ‘I’ and the rest, is conditioned only by ignorance.”

Text 124

4 · 124

तत्रान्यथाग्रहणवदन्यथाग्रहणबीजमग्रहणमनात्मधर्म एवेत्याह ।

Just as the mistaken appearance has ignorance as its seed, so the failure to recognize the Self that seeds it belongs to the not-Self. The verse says:

Text 125

4 · 125

इदं ज्ञानमहं ज्ञाता ज्ञेयमेतदिति त्रयम् ।

“The triad ‘this is knowledge, I am the knower, this is what is known’—

Verse 4.45

4.45

योऽविकारो विजानाति परागेवास्य तत्तमः ॥ ४५॥

the changeless one who knows it is beyond that darkness.”

Text 127

4 · 127

यत एतदेवमतस्तस्यैव बीजात्मनस्तमसश्चित्तधर्मविशिष्टस्य स्वकार्यद्वितीयाभिसंबन्धो न त्वविकारिण आत्मन इत्याह दृष्टान्तेन ।

The seed-like darkness, which belongs to the mind, is connected with its changing effects and with what is other than the Self, not with the changeless Self. An example is given:

Text 128

4 · 128

रूपप्रकाशयोर्यद्वत्संगतिर्विक्रियावतः ।

“As form is joined to the changing light that reveals it,

Verse 4.46

4.46

सुखदुःखादिसंबन्धश्चित्तस्यैवं विकारिणः ॥ ४६॥

so happiness, sorrow and the rest are joined to the changing mind.”

Text 130

4 · 130

तदेतदन्वयव्यतिरेकाभ्यां दर्शयिष्यन्नाह ।

The author now shows this through agreement and difference:

Text 131

4 · 131

संप्रसादेऽविकारित्वादस्तं याते विकारिणि ।

“In deep repose, when what changes has subsided, because the Self is changeless,

Verse 4.47

4.47

पश्यतो नात्मनः किंचिद्द्वितीयं स्पृशतेऽण्वपि ॥ ४७॥

not even the slightest second thing touches the Seer.”

Text 133

4 · 133

सोऽयं कूटस्थज्ञानमूर्तिरात्मा ।

This is the Self, whose very nature is changeless awareness.

Text 134

4 · 134

यथा प्राज्ञे तथैवायं स्वप्नजागरितान्तयोः ।

“As in deep sleep, so too at the ends of dream and waking:

Verse 4.48

4.48

पश्यन्नप्यविकारित्वाद्द्वितीयं न पश्यति ॥ ४८॥

though it seems to see, because it is changeless it sees no second thing.”

Text 136

4 · 136

एवं ज्ञानवतो नास्ति ममाहंमतिसंश्रयः ।

“For one who knows, there is no foothold for the thoughts ‘mine’ and ‘I’,

Verse 4.49

4.49

भास्वत्प्रदीपहस्तस्य ह्यन्धकार इवाग्रतः ॥ ४९॥

just as darkness cannot remain before someone holding a shining lamp.”

Text 138

4 · 138

तत्र दृष्टान्तः ।

Here is an example:

Text 139

4 · 139

आ प्रबोधाद्यथासिद्धिर्द्वैतादन्यस्य वस्तुनः ।

“Before waking, the existence of any object other than the dream is established only within that dream;

Verse 4.50

4.50

बोधादेवमसिद्धत्वं बुद्ध्यादेः प्रत्यगात्मनः ॥ ५०॥

with awakening, the intellect and the rest are likewise not established apart from the Self within.”

Text 141

4 · 141

स एष विद्वान्हानोपादानशून्यमात्मानमात्मनि पश्यन् ।

The wise person, seeing within the Self the Self that is free of taking up and laying down,

Text 142

4 · 142

सर्वमेवानुजानाति सर्वमेव निषेधति ।

“accepts everything and denies everything;

Verse 4.51

4.51

भेदात्मलाभोऽनुज्ञा स्यान्निषेधोऽतत्स्वभावतः ॥ ५१॥

acceptance is the appearance of a separate form, while denial is knowing that it is not the Self’s nature.”

Text 144

4 · 144

सर्वस्योक्तत्वादुपसंहारः ।

Everything has now been explained; the author concludes:

Text 145

4 · 145

परमाथार्थनिष्ठं यत्सर्ववेदान्तनिश्चितम् ।

“I have set out the knowledge established by all Vedānta, resting on the highest truth,

Verse 4.52

4.52

तमोपनुद्धियां ज्ञानं तदेतत्कथितं मया ॥ ५२॥

which removes the darkness from the understanding.”

Text 147

4 · 147

एतावदिहोक्तम् ।

This much has been taught here:

Text 148

4 · 148

नेहात्मविन्मदन्योऽस्ति न मत्तोऽज्ञोऽस्ति कश्चन ।

“There is no knower of the Self other than me, and no one ignorant apart from me.

Verse 4.53

4.53

इत्यजानन्विजानाति यस्स ब्रह्मविदुत्तमः ॥ ५३॥

Whoever knows this without distinction is the highest knower of Brahman.”

Text 150

4 · 150

एवमात्मानं ज्ञात्वा किं प्रवर्तितव्यमुत निवर्तितव्यमाहोस्विन्मुक्तप्रग्रहतेति । उच्यते ।

After knowing the Self in this way, should one act, refrain from acting, or have complete freedom to act? The answer is:

Text 151

4 · 151

ज्ञेयाभिन्नमिदं यस्माज्ज्ञेयवस्त्वनुसार्यतः ।

“Because this is not different from what is to be known, and follows the nature of the knowable,

Verse 4.54

4.54

न प्रवृत्तिं निवृत्तिं वा कटाक्षेणापि वीक्षते ॥ ५४॥

it does not even glance toward action or the cessation of action.”

Text 153

4 · 153

कुत एतज्ज्ञेयाभिन्नमिति । यतः ।

Why is it not different from what is to be known? Because:

Text 154

4 · 154

प्रागात्मबोधाद्बोधोऽयं बाह्यवस्तूपसर्जनः ।

“Before knowing the Self, awareness takes external things as secondary;

Verse 4.55

4.55

प्रध्वस्ताखिलसंसार आत्मैकालम्बनः श्रुतेः ॥ ५५॥

when all worldly bondage is destroyed, scriptural knowledge rests on the Self alone.”

Text 156

4 · 156

एवमवगतपरमार्थतत्त्वस्य न शेषशेषिभावस्तत्कारणस्योत्सारितत्वादित्याह ।

One who has understood ultimate reality has no relation of dependent means to an end, since the cause of that relation has been removed. The verse says:

Text 157

4 · 157

वास्तवेनैव वृत्तेन निरुणद्धि यतो भवम् ।

“Because true knowledge ends worldly becoming as it actually is,

Verse 4.56

4.56

निवृत्तिमपि मृद् नाति सम्यग्बोधः प्रवृत्तिवत् ॥ ५६॥

it does not undergo cessation any more than it undergoes action.”

Text 159

4 · 159

सकृदात्मप्रसूत्यैव निरुणद्ध्यखिलं भवम् ।

“The very arising of knowledge of the Self, by itself, puts an end to all worldly becoming;

Verse 4.57

4.57

ध्वान्तमात्रनिरासेन न ततोऽन्यान्यथामतिः ॥ ५७॥

by removing darkness alone, it leaves no further mistaken notion.”

Text 161

4 · 161

देशकालाद्यसंबद्धाद्देशादेर्मोहकार्यतः ।

“Because the Self is unrelated to place, time and the rest, which are effects of delusion,

Verse 4.58

4.58

नानुत्पन्नमदग्धं वा ज्ञानमज्ञानमस्त्यतः ॥ ५८॥

ignorance cannot be some separate thing that must first arise and then be burned away.”

Text 163

4 · 163

सम्यग्ज्ञानशिखिप्लुष्टमोहतत्कार्यरूपिणः ।

“When the fire of right knowledge has once burned up delusion and all its effects,

Verse 4.59

4.59

सकृन्निवृत्तेर्बाध्यस्य किं कार्यमवशिष्यते ॥ ५९॥

what further task remains for what has been sublated?”

Text 165

4 · 165

वास्तवेनैव वृत्तेनाविद्यायाः प्रध्वस्तत्वान्न किंचिदवशिष्यत इत्युक्तः परिहारः । अथापरस्सांप्रदायिकः परिहारः ।

This was the first reply: ignorance is destroyed as a real fact. Now comes another traditional reply:

Text 166

4 · 166

निवृत्तसर्पस्सर्पोत्थं यथा कम्पं न मुञ्चति ।

“Even after the snake has disappeared, a person may still tremble from the snake he saw;

Verse 4.60

4.60

विध्वस्ताखिलमोहोऽपि मोहकार्यं तथात्मवित् ॥ ६०॥

so the knower of the Self may retain an effect of delusion, though all delusion is destroyed.”

Text 168

4 · 168

यतः प्रवृत्तिबीजमुच्छिन्नं तस्मात् ।

Since the seed of action has been cut off:

Text 169

4 · 169

तरोरुत्खातमूलस्य शोषेणैव यथा क्षयः ।

“A tree whose root is torn out perishes by drying up;

Verse 4.61

4.61

तथा बुद्धात्मतत्त्वस्य निवृत्त्यैव तनुक्षयः ॥ ६१॥

in the same way, for one who knows the truth of the Self, the body ends through withdrawal.”

Text 171

4 · 171

अथालेपकपक्षनिरासार्थमाह ।

The author now rejects the view that realization permits any conduct:

Text 172

4 · 172

बुद्धद्वैतसत्त्वस्य यथेष्टाचरणं यदि ।

“If one who knows that duality is real may act as he pleases,

Verse 4.62

4.62

शुनां तत्त्वदृशां चैव को भेदोऽशुचिभक्षणे ॥ ६२॥

what difference is there between the wise and dogs when they eat what is impure?”

Text 174

4 · 174

कस्मान्न भवति । यस्मात् ।

Why should this not be allowed? Because:

Text 175

4 · 175

अधर्माज्जायतेऽज्ञानं यथेष्टाचरणं ततः ।

“From unrighteousness comes ignorance, and from it comes heedless conduct;

Verse 4.63

4.63

धर्मकार्ये कथं तत्स्याद्यत्र धर्मोऽपि नेष्यते ॥ ६३॥

how could that be the result of virtue, when even virtue is not accepted?”

Text 177

4 · 177

प्रत्याचक्षाण आहातो यथेष्टाचरणं हरिः ।

Rejecting heedless conduct, the Lord says:

Verse 4.64

4.64

"यस्य सर्वे समारम्भाः " "प्रकाशं चे "ति सर्वदृक् ॥ ६४॥

“The one whose every undertaking…” and “the one who sees the light…”—the all-seeing Lord [teaches this discipline].”

Text 179

4 · 179

तिष्ठतु तावत्सर्वप्रवृत्तिबीजघस्मरं ज्ञानं मुमुक्ष्ववस्थायामपि न संभवति यथेष्टाचरणम् ।

Set aside for now the liberated knower, whose knowledge consumes the seed of every action: even while one is still seeking liberation, heedless conduct is impossible.

Text 180

4 · 180

तदाह ।

The text says:

Text 181

4 · 181

" यो हि यत्र विरक्तः स्यान्नासौ तस्मै प्रवर्तते ।

“One who is dispassionate toward a thing does not pursue it;

Verse 4.65

4.65

लोकत्रयविरक्तत्वान्मुमुक्षुः किमितीहते " ॥ ६५॥

being dispassionate toward all three worlds, what would a seeker of liberation strive for here?”

Text 183

4 · 183

तत्र दृष्टान्तः ।

An example is given:

Text 184

4 · 184

"क्षुधया पीड्यमानोऽपि न विषं ह्यत्तुमिच्छति ।

“Even when tormented by hunger, a person does not wish to eat poison;

Verse 4.66

4.66

मिष्टान्नध्वस्ततृड् जानन्नामूढस्तज्जिघत्सति " ॥ ६६॥

one who knows that sweet food ends thirst, and is not deluded, longs for that.”

Text 186

4 · 186

यतोऽवगतपरमार्थतत्त्वस्य यथेष्टाचरणं न मनागपि घटते मुमुक्षुत्वेऽपि च तस्मात् ।

Because heedless conduct cannot occur even slightly for one who knows ultimate reality, or even for one seeking liberation:

Text 187

4 · 187

रागो लिङ्गमबोधस्य चित्तव्यायामभूमिषु ।

“Attachment is a sign of ignorance in the fields where the mind ranges;

Verse 4.67

4.67

कुतः शाड्वलता तस्य यस्याग्निः कोटरे तरोः ॥ ६७॥

how could grass grow on a tree whose hollow is filled with fire?”

Text 189

4 · 189

सकलपुरुषार्थसमाप्तिकारिणोऽस्यात्मावबोधस्य कुतः प्रसूतिरिति । उच्यते ।

How does this knowledge of the Self, which fulfills every human aim, arise? The answer:

Text 190

4 · 190

अमानित्वादिनिष्ठो यो यश्चाद्वेष्ट्रादिसाधनः ।

“Knowledge arises in one established in humility and the other virtues, and equipped with means such as freedom from hatred;

Verse 4.68

4.68

ज्ञानमुत्पद्यते तस्य न बहिर्मुखचेतसः ॥ ६८॥

it does not arise in a mind turned outward.”

Text 192

4 · 192

उत्पन्न आत्मविज्ञाने किमविद्याकार्यत्वात्प्रवृत्तिवन्निवृत्त्यात्मकामानित्वदयो निवर्तन्त उत नेति । नेति ब्रूमः । किं कारणम् । निवृत्तिशास्त्राविरुद्धस्वाभाव्यात्परमात्मनो न तु नियोगवशात् । कथं तर्हि । शृणु ।

When knowledge of the Self has arisen, do humility and the other qualities, which take the form of withdrawal, cease as effects of ignorance, like activity? We say no. Why? The Supreme Self naturally accords with the teaching of renunciation; these qualities do not arise from an injunction. How, then? Listen:

Text 193

4 · 193

उत्पन्नात्मप्रबोधस्य त्वद्वेष्टृत्वादयो गुणाः ।

“For one awakened to the Self, qualities such as freedom from hatred

Verse 4.69

4.69

अयत्नतो भवन्त्यस्य न तु साधनरूपिणः ॥ ६९॥

arise effortlessly; they are not practices to be cultivated as means.”

Text 195

4 · 195

यत एतदेवमतः ।

Because this is so:

Text 196

4 · 196

इमां ग्रन्थमुपादित्सुरमानित्वादिसाधनः ।

“One who wishes to study this treatise should diligently cultivate humility and the other means;

Verse 4.70

4.70

यत्नतः स्यान्न दुर्वृत्तः प्रत्यग्धर्मानुगो ह्ययम् ॥ ७०॥

one should not behave badly, for this teaching accords with the nature of the inward Self.”

Text 198

4 · 198

न दातव्यश्चायं ग्रन्थः ।

This treatise must not be given to:

Text 199

4 · 199

नाविरक्ताय संसारान्नानिरस्तैषणाय च ।

“One who is not dispassionate toward worldly existence, or who has not cast aside desire,

Verse 4.71

4.71

न चायमवते देयं वेदान्तार्थप्रवेशनम् ॥ ७१॥

or to one unfit to enter into the meaning of Vedānta.”

Text 201

4 · 201

ज्ञात्वा यथोदितं सम्यग्ज्ञातव्यं नावशिष्यते ।

“One who has properly known what was taught has nothing further left to know;

Verse 4.72

4.72

न चानिरस्तकर्मेदं जानीयादञ्जसा ततः ॥ ७२॥

one should therefore not imagine this knowledge while action still remains unremoved.”

Text 203

4 · 203

निरस्तसर्वकर्माणः प्रत्यक्प्रवणबुद्धयः ।

“Renunciants who have laid aside all action, whose minds are turned inward,

Verse 4.73

4.73

निष्कामा यतयः शान्ता जानन्तीदं यथोदितम् ॥ ७३॥

desireless and peaceful, know this teaching as it has been set out.”

Text 205

4 · 205

श्रीमच्छङ्करपादपद्मयुगलं संसेव्य लब्ध्वोचिवान् ज्ञानं पारमहंस्यमेतदमलं स्वान्तान्धकारापनुत् ।

“Having served the twin lotus feet of the blessed Śaṅkara, I received this pure knowledge of the highest renunciation, which removes the darkness of the heart.

Verse 4.74

4.74

मा भूदत्र विरोधिनी मतिरतः सद्भिः परीक्ष्यं बुधैः सर्वत्रैव विशुद्धये मतमिदं सन्तः परं कारणम् ॥७४॥

Let no contrary view arise here; let wise and good people examine this teaching for the sake of purity everywhere. The good are its highest cause.”

Text 207

4 · 207

सुभाषितं चार्वपि नामहात्मनां दिवाकरो नक्तदृशामिवामलः ।

“The sound teaching of great souls shines pure, like the sun to those who see by night;

Verse 4.75

4.75

प्रभाति भात्येव विशुद्धचेतसां निधिर्यथापास्ततृषां महाधनः ॥ ७५॥

for minds made clear, it shines like a great treasure to one whose thirst has been quenched.”

Text 209

4 · 209

विष्णोः पादानुगां यां निखिलभवनुदं शंकरोऽवापयोगात् सर्वज्ञं ब्रह्मसंस्थं मुनिगणसहितं सम्यगभ्यर्च्यभक्तया ।

“Śaṅkara attained through yoga the knowledge that follows Viṣṇu’s path and removes all worldly becoming. I worshipped with devotion the all-knowing sage, established in Brahman, together with the company of sages.

Verse 4.76

4.76

विद्यां गङ्गामिवाहं प्रवरगुणनिधेः प्राप्य वेदान्तदीप्तां कारुण्यात्तामवोचं जनिमृतिनिवहध्वस्तये दुःखितेभ्यः ॥ ७६॥

Receiving from that treasure of excellent qualities the knowledge that flows like the Gaṅgā and illumines Vedānta, I have spoken it out of compassion for the suffering, to end the multitude of births and deaths.”

Text 211

4 · 211

वेदान्तोदरवर्ति भास्वदमलं ध्वान्तच्छिदस्मद्धियो दिव्यं ज्ञानमतीद्रियेऽपिविषये व्याहन्यते न क्वचित् ।

“This pure, radiant knowledge, dwelling in the heart of Vedānta and removing the darkness of our understanding, concerns a supersensible truth, yet is never obstructed.

Verse 4.77

4.77

यो नो न्यायशलाकयैव निखिलं संसारबीजं तमः प्रोत्सार्याविरकार्षीद्गुरुगुरुः पूज्याय तस्मै नमः ॥ ७७॥

We bow to that revered teacher of teachers, who, solely through the rod of reasoning, drove away the darkness that is the seed of all worldly bondage and revealed the truth.”

Text 213

4 · 213

संबन्धोक्तिरियं साध्वी प्रतिश्लोकमुदाहृता ।

“This account of the connections between the verses is skillfully given for each verse;

Verse 4.78

4.78

नैष्कर्म्यसिद्धेर्ज्ञात्वेमां व्याख्यातासौ भवेद् ध्रुवम् ॥ ७८॥

one who knows it will surely have explained the Naiṣkarmyasiddhi correctly.”

Chapter colophon

Adhyāya 4

इति चतुर्थोऽध्यायः ॥ ४॥

[End of the fourth chapter.]
